CHAPTER 11. No.2.
SUPPLEMENTAL POLICE.
Regulations made under section 12 of the Supplemental Police Ordinance. 1. These regulations may b~ cited as the Supplemental Police Regulations. 2. Every rural aDd estate constable shall be directly under the orders of Magis-trates, Gazetted Police Officers, Subordinate Police Officers and of the Officer in charge of the station to which he is attached.
3. Every rural and estate constable shall obey all Gazetted Police Officers and Subordinate Police Officers in matters of official duty.
4. Every rural and estate constable shall record in tae official'pocket.book which will be issue'd -te him for the purpose- .
(a) any information or intelligence obtained by him in regard to the public
tranquillity, the preservation o~ the peace, the commission. of. crime or other infringement of the law;
(b) any report or complaint made to him in his capacity of constable;
(e) a note of any act done by him in the execution of his dutyas'constable;
(d) a note of any other matter whatsoever arising out of his duty as rural or
estate constable. .
5. Every rural constable shall report himself regularly at intervals not exceeding one month at a time to the Subordinate Police Officer in charge of the station to which he is attached.
The Subordinate Police Officer shall on such report being made-
(a) check and initial the rural constable's pocket-book;
(b) instruct him in the descriptions of any notorious offenders at large and
ensure that such descriptions ate briefly entered in the pocket-book;
(c) instruct him as regards any recent Ordinance or Regulations affecting
police work;
(d) examine him as to his knowledge of habitual criminals or known thieves
residing in his district;
(e) furnish' him with a list of persons in his district against whom there are
outstanding warrants or summonses.
6. The badge shall always be worn when the rural or estate constable is exercising his office; if it is necessary, for the purpose of detecting crime, that he should not be known, he may keep his badge in his pocket or otherwise hidden, but when the moment comes for him to use his power as a constable he shall show it.
7. Every rural and estate constable shall report as soon as possible to the Sub-ordinate Police Officer in charge of the station to which he is attached any serious crime or unusual event occurring in his district.
8. When an arrest is made by a rural or estate constable he shall forthwith take the person or persons arrested to the Police Station to which he is attached or to the nearest Police Station to lay the necessary information as may be directed by the Subordinate Police Officer in charge.
9. When persons are arrested handcuffs shall never be used except in cases of necessity when a prisoner is violent and likely to attempt to escape or if the number of prisoners to be conveyed or the special circumstances render such a protection necessary. Females, lunatics and young children shall never be handcuffed.
10. No promise or threat shall be made to any person arrested or any violence used to make him say anything; if he wishes to speak, the rural or estate constable in whose custody he is must caution him that anything he may say may be given in evidence.
11. Every rural or estate constable shall be responsible for any government property issued to him and shall, subject to the discretion of the.commissioner, pay for any loss or damage to same.
12. Every rural constable on appointment shall, unless specially exempted by the Commissioner, be attached for seven days for training at the Police Station of his district and shall be paid at the rate of $1.00 per day during this period.
13. For neglect or violation of duty the Superintendent or Assistant Superinten-dent in charge of the division or district may inflict a fine not exceeding $2.00 on any member of the Supplemental Police; Provided that there shall be a right of appeal to the Commissioner against any fine inflicted. In more serious cases of neglect or violation of duty, the Commissioner may, after due enquiry by himself or by sworn enquiry ordered by him, inflict a fine not exceeding $10.00 for each offence.
14. All offences of which members of the Supplemental Police have been found guilty shall be entered in a " defaulters' offences" book kept for this purpose.
15. Superintendents of Police in charge of divisions shall annually, when forward-ing the promotion list of Subordinate Police Officers and constables, forward at the same time a separate list of rural and estate Subordinate Police Officers and constables recommended for promotion.
16. Whenever any rural or estate Subordinate Police Officer or constable is en-gaged on any official police duty as set out in the Second Schedule of the Supplemental Police Ordinance, he shall be subject to any Police Regulations or Standing Orders in force governing the Police Force.
CIIAPTER 11. ~To. 4.
FIRE BRIGADES.
VOLUNTEER SECTION.
Regulations lllade under section 22 of the Fire Brigades Ordinance. 1. These regulations may be cited as the Fire Brigade (Volunteer Sections) Regulations, and shall severally apply to each and every volunteer section of any Brigade wherever constituted.
2. Each volunteer section of a Brigade shall consist of such number of officers, non-commissioned officers and firemen as the Governor may from time to time approve and appoint for and in respect of any city, borough, town or district in the Colony.
3. Every person applying for admission into a volunteer section of a Brigade may be required to undergo an examination -for physical fitness by a member of the Medical Board and must satisfy the following conditions;-
Age-over 18 years and under 55 years. Height-not less than 5 feet 4 inches. Chest measurement-not less than 33 inches.
4. The Superintendent of a Brigade in command of a volunteer section shall recommend, for the approval of the Governor, any person to be a member of a volunteer section.
5. (1) Every volunteer shall serve until the termination of the present war; Provided that the Commissioner of Police may in his discretion-
(a) permit any volunteer to withdraw from a volunteer section on good and
sufficient reasons being submitted to him through the Superintendent in command of such. section; or .
(b) dispense with the services of any volunteer on the recommendation of the
Superintendent in command of such section.
(2) In this regulation "termination of the present war" means the date which shall be notified by the Governor by Proclamation as the date on which the-present war shall have terminated.
6. Every volunteer shall attend such parades and drills and shall carry out such duties, in such places, at such times, and in such manner as may be ordered by the' Superintendent in command of the particular section.
7. (1) Subject to the approval of the Governor, promotion to commissioned and non-commissioned rank shall be determined by the Commissioner of Police.
(2) Every candidate for promotion may be required to pass such qualifying examination as may from time to time be approved by the Commissioner of Police.
S. (1) The uniform of a volunteer shall consist of such clothing and equipment as shall be provided by the Commissioner of Police.
(2) Such clothing and equipment. shall be, and remain the property of the Fire Brigade.
(3) In any case of damage to or loss of a uniform or any part thereof, the volunteer to whom such uniform was issued shall, wherever such damage or loss has; b::en occasioned by his wilfulness or carelessness, be liable to make good the cost of repairing or replacing the same, and shall also be liable to any other penalty that may be imposed under these regulations or any other law.
9. Every volunteer whilst on duty shall obey the la"wiul orders of-
(a) The Superintendent of Brigade in command of the volunteer section and
his Assistanf Superintendent of Brigade; and
(b) all officers and non-commissioned officers of the volunteer section.
10. Every volunteer-
(a) when called out for service on the occasion of an alarm of fire or of an
air raid; or
(b) when required to attend a parade; or
(e) when required to attend a lecture, shall be paid for his services or his
attendance, as the case may be, at such rates as may from time to time be fixed by resolution of the Legislative Council.
11. Every volunteer who shall be guilty of any of the following offences-
(1) insubordination;
(2) wilful disobedience of alawful order;
(3) absence from parade, drill, or duty without leave;
(4) making away with, selling, losing by carelessness or wilfully damaging a
uniform or any part thereof or any article which is the property of the Brigade;
(5) any act, conduct, or neglect to the prejudice of good order and discipline,
not specified in these regulations;
(6) any breach of these regulations, shall, on conviction before the Superintendent of Brigade in command of his section, be liable to one or more of the following punishments-
(a) dismissal;
(b) reduction to a lower rank;
(e) a fine not exceeding twenty-four dollars;
(d) extra parades, drills, and duties.
CHAPT;' L. No.7.
PRISONS.
Rules made by the Governor and Executive Council under the West Indian
Prisons Act, 1838 (1 & 2 Viet. C. 67).
1. These Rules may be cited as the Prison Rules~ 2. The staff of the Prisons Department shall consist of a Superintendent, a Deputy Superintendent, Assistant Superintendents, Medical Officers, Clerks, teachers, ,dispensers, storekeepers, subordinate officers, and such other officers and servants -as the Governor may from time to time appoint.
PART I.
THE INSPECTOR OF PRISONS.
3. The Inspector of Prisons (hereinafter referred to as "the Inspector") shall inspect the Royal Gaol, the' Female Prison, the Carrera Convict Prison, the Youth Training Centre at Golden Grove at least once in each month, and when so doing may exercise any of the powers vested in him by section 3 of the West Indian Prisons Act, 1838.*
4. The Inspector shall, upon such visits, investigate every complaint or applica- -tion made to him by any prisoner, and the prisoners shall be given opportunity of laying their complaints or applications before him.
5. The Inspector shall, upon such visits, have the right to see every prisomr ,detained in the prison and shall pay special attention to those prisoners who are undergoing punishment in cells and those sick in the infirmaries.
6. \Vhen the Superintendent shall report to him that any prisoner has been _guilty of repeated offences against these Rules or rules governing Young Offenders, ,as the case may be, or of any offence which the Superintendent himself is not em-powered to punish, the Inspector shall forth,vith enquire into and determine the -case in a summary manner and shall order the offender to be punished according to the scale of punishment in these Rules or rules governing Young Offenders cas the case may be.
7. The Inspector shall investigate all complaints made against subordinate ,officers and, in cases where he does not think dismissal necessary, he shall have the power to inflict any fine not exceeding one month's salary, or he may reduce the .officer to a lower place in the same rank.
8. When a subordinate officer is found guilty of an offence for which, in the ,opinion of the Inspector, he ought to be dismissed or to be reduced in rank and pay, _he shall report the case for the approval of the Governor.
9. He shall submit to the Colonial Secretary as soon as possible after the 31st March in every year, a report on the prisons specified in rule 3, together with the Annual Report of the Superintendent of Prisons, and shall specify in such report ,all defects in the construction, management, or discipline of the said prisons, and all improvements which are, in his opinion, requisite for maintaining and improving the government and discipline of the said prisons and for promoting the reformation of prisoners,
* Golden Grove, proclaimed a convict depot to be known as His Majesty's Prisons, ,Golden Grove, by G.N. 40-1948.
PART II.
OFFICIAL VISITORS.
10. The Governor may appoint such persons as he may think iit to be Official Visitors to one or more prisons. Such appointments shall be for a period not exceeding three years but may be terminated at any time at the Governor's pleasure.
11. Any Official Visitor may enter and inspect at any time any prison of the Colony to which he has been appointed a Visitor and shall be afforded full information on all matters connected with such prison and shall be given free access to all parts of such prison and to all prisoners therein.
12. On the visit of any Official Visitor, the Superintendent or Offi<;;er in charge shall. announce his arrival to the prisoners and opportunity shall be given to every prisoner to interview him. Should the Visitor desire to interview any prisoner, such prisoner shall be brought before him, or he may see such prisoner as he desires,. either in his cell or in a room out of sight and hearing of prison officers.
13. An Official Visitor's Book shall be kept at each prison in which Official Visitors, shall record their visit and make any remarks or recommendations they may consider necessary. A copy of every such entry shall be forwarded to the Colonial Secretary,. for the information of the Governor, without delay.
14. The Chief Justice, the members of the Executive Council, the Puisne Judges,. and the Chief Magistrate shall be ex officio Official Visitors to any prison.
PART III.
STAFF.
SUPERIOR OFFICERS.
(i) The Superintendent of Prisons.
15. The Superintendent of Prisons shall have charge of and be responsible for the'! proper management of all prisons (but not of Industrial Schools or Orphanages). He shall take care that the prisoners are humanely treated, that discipline and security are strictly enforced and that everything is done to encourage and promote· the reformation of the prisoners.
16. The Superintendent shall reside as near as possible to the Royal Goal and his, office shall be at that prison. He shall not be absent from Port-of-Spain at night except with the permission of the Colonial Secretary who shall be informed of the· reason for such absence and the arrangements made for the performance of his. duties.
17. The Superintendent shall carefully examine all requisitions for stores, materiah and tools before approving orders therefor so as to ensure that rigid economy is. practised.
18. The Superintendent shall not have any other employment except with the'! consent of the Governor.
19. The Superintendent shall strictly conform to the laws and rules relating to' prisons and shall be responsible for the due observance of them by all members of the, staff ..
20. The Superintendent shall observe the conduct of all officers and enforce on, each of them the due execution of his duties. .
21. The Superintendent shall interview, or cause to be interviewed, all prisoners; on reception and prior to their discharge.
22. The Superintendent shall inspect all the prisons and all prisoners as often as is, possible paying special attention to gangs working outside the prisons in order to· ensure that they are industrious and that all orders respecting their safe custody and, the application of labour are duly enforced. He shall state in his Journal '\-vhen .such inspections have been made. He shall at intervals make surprise visits to the: prisons at night, and record in his Journal each such visit and the result thereof.
23. The Superintendent shall require a report to be made to him at the hour of locking up of the prisoners for the night, as to whether all the officers for duty are present and whether or not everything is correct.
24. The Superintendent shall call the attention of the Medical Officer to any prisoner whose state of mind or body appears to require attention, and shall, so far as practicable, carry into effect any written recommendation made by the Medical Officer.
25. ~When a prisoner is, in the opinion of the Medical Officer, dangerously ill, the Superintendent shall whenever practicable forthwith inform the relatives of such prisoner.
26. The Superintendent shall, without delay, report to the Colonial Secretary any case of insanity or apparent insanity occurring among the prisoners, or any case in which the Medical Officer is of the opinion that the mental state of any prisoner is becoming impaired or enfeebled by continued imprisonment, or that the life of any prisoner will be endangered by further imprisonment, or that any sick prisoner will not survive his sentence or is totally and permanently unfit for prison discipline.
27. The Superintendent shall upon the death of a prisoner give immediate notice thereof to the Coroner and bther authorities as prescribed by the Coroners' Ordinance or other law for the time being applicable.
He shall also, when practicable, notify the nearest relative of the deceased.
28. The Superintendent shall not allow any person to view any prison or to hold conversation with a prisoner except in accordance with the provisions of these Rules or other relevant provisions.
29. The Superintendent shall ensure that no person authorised to view any prison makes any sketch or takes any photograph thereof except with the per-mission of the Colonial Secretary.
30. The Superintendent shall have the power to remove from any prison any visitor whose conduct is improper or to order his removal therefrom by any officer.
31. The Superintendent shall cause a notice to be affixed at the entrance to each prison cautioning,persons against bringing in prohibited articles, a list of which will be given in the notice. Should he suspect any person of bringing in any prohibited article into or of carrying out any property belonging to the government from a prison, he may order such person to be searched.
32. The Superintendent shall cause an abstract of such of the Rules as relate to the treatment, privileges, earnings and remission of sentence of prisoners to be placed in a conspicuous part of each prison and he shall also ensure that such Rules~ are explained to prisoners on admission.
33. The Superintendent shall arrange that offences against prison discipline shall be promptly dealt with and shall, as soon as possible, investigate any complaint and attend to any application from a prisoner and shall ensure that any prisoner desiring to see the Inspector or any Official Visitor shall have an opportunity of doing so.
34. The Superintendent shall enforce a high degree of cleanliness amongst the staff, the prisoners and in every part of the prisons.
35. The Superintendent shall frequently test the quantity and the quality of the diets supplied to prisoners.
36. In cases of gross misconduct on the. part of any subordinate ofncer, the Superintendent may suspend such officer from duty and place him on half pay, reporting the case at once to the Inspector. The Superintendent is empowered to impose npon subordinate officers and servants fines according to the scale in Schedule III to these Rules.
37. The Superintendent shall keep or cause to be kept all records, registers, journals, accounts and other books necessary for the efficient administration of the Prisons Department.
T.-VIII. 20 38. The Superintendent shall arrange for the regular check of all stores, inven-
tories, records, work orders and accounts by officers of the Department.
39. Whenever visits are made to a prison by the Inspector or by an Official Visitor,
the Superintendent shall supply such Inspector or Official Visitor with full information regarding such prison and shall give free access to all parts of the prison and to the prisoners therein.
40. The Superintendent shall take care that precautions against fire are adopted,
and that the appliances for the extinction of fire are at all times kept in good order and ready for use. He shall take care that instructions are given as to the steps to be taken in case of fire, and that the officers concerned are acquainted with their duties on such occasions.
4Jl. In C2_ses of emergency not sufficiently provided for in these Rules the Super-
intendent shall be guided in taking action by the Rules and Standing Orders for Prisons in England except where the same are clearly inconsistent with any local law.
42. The Superintendent may communicate direct with the Colonial Secretary on
any matter connected with the Prisons Department. All officers may communicate with the Colonial Secretary in writing through the Superintendent who shall forward such communication without delay, making any observations he may deem necessary upon the contents thereof. . .
43. The charge of the Prisons Department shall, in the absence of the Superin-tendent, devolve on the Deputy Superintendent-or in his absence on one of the Assistant Superintendents deputed by the Superintendent; and the Deputy Superin-tendent or other officer so deputed shall be competent to perform and shall perform any duty required to he performed by the Superintendent.
44. The Superintendent may delegate to the Deputy Superintendent or to an Assistant Superintendent certain duties other than those connected with their particular station.
45. The Superintendent shall, as soon as possible after the 31st of March in each year,submit to the Inspector for the information of the Governor a report in writing with respect to the administration of the prisons of the Colony for the past year.
46. The Superintendent and his family shall be entitled to free medical attendance and medicines. . 47. The Superintendent shall be entitled to leave in accordance with the Civil Service Regulations for the time being in force.
(ii) The Deputy Superintendent and Assistant S~tPerintendents.
48. The Deputy Superintendent shall be in charge of the Royal Gaol and Female Prison and shall reside as near to the Royal Gaol as possible. He shall assist the Superintendent in the administration of the Prisons Department.
49. The Deputy Superintendent shall be responsible for the training of the sub-ordinate officers of the prisons and shall be assisted in these duties by the Assistant Superintendents.
50. The Deputy Superintendent shall carefully check all warrants of commitment and see that they are entered daily in the registers and that the dates of discharge of prisoners are accurately entered in the Discharge Journals.
51. An Assistant Superintendent shall be in charge of the Carrera Convict Prison and shall reside in the quarters provided thereat.
52. An Assistant Superintendent shall be in charge of the Youth Training Centre and shall administer that Centre in accordance with the Regulations made by the Governor in Executive Council under the Young Offenders Detention Ordinance. He shall reside as near as possible to that Centre.
53. The Deputy Superintendent and the Assistant Superintendents shall daily inspect every part of their respective stations and shall see every prisoner once at least in every twenty-four hours, special attention being paid to those undergoing punishment and those in the infirmaries.
54. The Deputy Superintendent and the Assistant Superintendents shall see that a careful check is kept of all stores, clothing and tools at their respective stations in order to ensure that waste is prevented.
55. The Deputy Superintendent and the Assistant Superintendents shall as frequently as possible inspect gangs working outside their stations in order to ensure that discipline is being maintained and that a full day's work is being done.
56. The Deputy Superintendent and the Assistant Superintendents shall take care that all written instructions of the Medical Officer are carried out. Should these instructions interfere with the daily routine, good order and discipline of their stations they shall at once confer with the Medical Officer and report the case to the Superintendent.
57. The Deputy Superintendent and the Assistant Superintendents shall co-operate with and facilitate the work of the Chaplains and the Teachers.
58. The Deputy Superintendent and the Assistant Superintendents shall fre-quently inspect the quality and quantity of the diets and make sure that every prisoner receives the diet to which he is entitled.
59. The Deputy Superintendent and the Assistant Superintendents shall, without delay, investigate all charges against prisoners and deal with them in accordance with these Rules or the Regulations governing Young Offenders, as the case may be.
60. The Deputy Superintendent and the Assistant Superintendents shall without delay, investigate all complaints and requests of prisoners.
61. The Deputy Superintendent and the Assistant Superintendents shall, at least once a week, pay a surprise visit to their station at night.
62. The Deputy Superintendent and the Assistant Superintendents shall be present at all corporal punishments inflicted at their station and shall promptly carry into effect any order or instruction of the Medical Officer in connection therewith.
63. The Deputy Superintendent and the Assistant Superintendents shall regulate the duties and hours of the staff in consultation with the Chief Officer of their Station.
64. The Deputy Superintendent and the Assistant Superintendents shall take -care that all records, Journals, and other documents are kept up to date and are carefully preserved.
65. The Deputy Superintendent and the Assistant Superintendents shall interview all prisoners on admission and on discharge, bringing to their notice the amount of remission that may be gained by good conduct and industry, and any other rules or orders governing their conduct. From those about to be discharged they shall ascertain whether or not they have received all money and property taken from them on admission. To those discharged on licence they shall explain the terms and conditions of the licence.
66. The Deputy Superintendent and the Assistant Superintendents shall not be absent from their s-tations during the prescribed hours of attendance without the permission of the Superintendent.
67. The Deputy Superintendent and the Assistant Superintendents shall carefully -observe and carry out all Prison Rules, and orders and instructions of the Superinten-dent. They shall ensure that all concerned do the same.
68. The Deputy Superintendent and the Assistant Superintendents shall be liable to be transferred from one station to another on the order of the Governor.
69. The Deputy Superintendent and the Assistant Superintendents and their -families shall be entitled to free medical attendance and medicine.
70. The Deputy Superintendent and the Assistant Superintendents shall be en-titled to leave in accordance with the Civil Service Regulations for the time being in force.
20 (2)
(iii) The Medical Officei'.
71. The :\Iedical Officer shan have the general care of the health of the prisoners and shall attend all siCk prisoners and all officers and servants of the Prisons Depart-ment residing at any prison or residing within the limits of the City of Port-of-Spain. Medicines prescribed by the ::vIedical Officer shall be supplied at the expense of the Government.
72. The Medical Officer shall report to the Colonial Secretary, through the Superin-ten dent, any circumstance connected with the prisoners or the treatment of the prisoners which at any time appears to him to require consideration on medical grounds.
73. The Medical Officer shall visit the Royal Gaol and the Fem.ale Prison daily not later than 8.00 a.m.; the Carrera Convict Prison three times a week; and the Youth Training Centre three times a week. He shall visit these stations more frequently if necessary or if called in cases of emergency by the Superintendent.
74. The Medical Officer shall examine every prisoner as early as possible after admission, and record in his Journal the state of his health, the class of "\'.'Ork he is fit to do and any other particulars he may think necessary. He shall also examine every prisoner on the morning of his discharge, noting in his Journal any injuries or maladies from. which such prisoner may be suffering.
75. The Medical Officer shall, on every visit, examine such prisoners as complain of illness, reporting to the Supelintendent in writing their fitness or otherwise for labour. He shall also see every prisoner who is sick in the Infrrmary or elsewhere.
76. The Medical Officer shall, on every visit, see every prisoner who is under restraint in cellular confinement, or on restricted diet or awaiting execution or any other prisoner to whom his attention is specially directed.
77. ,Vhen a prisoner is about to be removed from any prison the Medical Officer shall examine him and certify as to his :fitness to travel, and as to such other parti-culars regarding him as may be required.
78. The Medical Officer shall frequently examine the' provisions made for the purpose of cleanliness and sanitation and see whether they are in efficient working order. He shall report at once to the Superintendent any defect or insufficiency therein. .
79. The Medical Officer shall record the following particulars in connection witl1 the death of any prisoner-
(a) the date and time when the illness of such prisoner was first notified to.
him or noticed by him;
(b) date of beginning of illness (if known);
(c) nature of illness;
(d) date and time of death;
(e) appearance of body after death; anc1
(f) any other special remarks.
80. Once in every quarter the Medical Officer shall inspect every part of every prison for the purpose of ascertaining that nothing exists therein likely to be in-jurious to the health of the prisoners. The result of this inspection shall be reported by him in writing through the Superintendent to the Colonial Secretary for· the information of the Governor.
81. The Medical Officer shall frequently inspect the food (whether cooked or uncooked) of the prisoners and shall report to the Superintendent as to the state and quality of food and as to any deficiency in the quantity or defect in the quality of the water, and also as to the sufficiency of the clothing and bedding, and any other matter which may affect the health of the prisoners.
82. When the Medical Officer has reason to believe that a prisoner's health is likely to be injuriously affected by prison discipline or treatment, he shall report the; case in writing, through the Superintendent, to the Colonial Secretary together "with such recommendations as he thinks proper.
83. ,,¥henever the 2Vledical Officer is of the opinion that the life of any prisoner will be endangered by his continuance in prison, or that any sick prisoner will not survive his sentence, or is totally and permanently unfit for prison discipline, he shall report his opinion and the grounds thereof, in ,vriting, through the Superintendent, to the Colonial" Secretary.
84. The Medical Officer shall give notice to the Superintendent and the Chaplain when a prisoner appears to be dangerously ill.
85. The Medical Officer shall report in writing to the Superintendent the case of any prisoner to "which he thinks it necessary on medical grounds to draw attention and shall make such recommendations as he deems needful for the alteration of the discipline or treatment of the prisoner or for the supply to him of additional articles of food or clothing.
86. The Medical Officer shall have the special care of the mental health of the pnsoners, keeping under his special observation any whose mental condition appears to require such observation, and if necessary shall take such steps as he may consider proper with a view to their segregation, or treatment as mental patients.
87. The Medical Ofncer shall report to the Colonial Secretary through the Superin-tendent the case of any prisoner whose mental health appears likely to become impaired by continued imprisonment. "
88. (1) If any case of peculiar difficulty or danger occurs, the Medical Officer may call in additional medical aid, but if time permits, he shall first apply through the Superintendent for the Colonial Secretary's approval.
(2) No serious operation shall be performed without previous consultation with another medical practitioner, except under very urgent circumstances not admitting of delay, and the Medical Officer shall keep a record of such circumstances.
89. The Medical Officer shall submit to the Superintendent in vvriting a recom-mendation for separating from other prisoners any prisoner suffering, or suspected of suffering, from any infectious or contagious disease, and shall immediately take such steps as may be necessary to prevent the spread of any such disease.
90. The lYledical Officer shall prescribe whenever necessary the special diets and extra food for issue to sick prisoners.
91. The Medical Officer shall enter in a Dietary Book the name of any prisoner who is to receive special diet or extra food, the kind of diet or food and the period during which he is to receive same, and the grounds on which such diet or extra food is ordered. This book shall be signed by him and forwarded daily to the Superin-tendent, Deputy Superintendent or Assistant Superintendent, as the case may be.
92. The Medical Oftlcer shall examine all candidates for employment in the Prisons Service and certify in writing whether they are mentally and physically fit for such employmfOnt.
93. On the Superintendent bringing to the notice of the Medical Officer that an officer is absent from duty on the plea of sickness, the Medical Officer shall "visit such officer and give a certificate in ,vriting as to whether such officer is fit or unfit for duty, and if unfit he shall state the number of days sick leave recommended.
94. If the Medical Officer is of the opinion that an officer is unable to perform his duties on account of sickness, which is the result of misconduct or irregularity of life, he shall report the same in writing to the Superintendent.
95. The Medical Officer shall submit during the month of January in each year, for insertion in the Annual Report of the Superintendent, a report in writing specify-ing, with reference to the preceding year, the state of health in which the prisoners have been received and the general state of health which has been maintained; the diseases which have been most prevalent; whether any connection can be traced between the diseases which have occurred and the locality or actual state of the buildings, or the diet, employm.ent, or other particular circumstances; the number of deaths, the number of Inftrmary cases, the number of cases of slight indisposition treated outside the Inftrmary, and the proportion of sick to the whole number of prisoners during the year; and any other circumstances with reference to the health of the prisoners that he may consider proper to bring to the attention of the Authorities.
96. \lifhen the Medical Officer is ill or absent from duty for any other reason, he shall arrange with the Director of Medical Services to appoint a substitute and shall notify the Superintendent.
(iv) The Chaplains.
97. The Chaplains shall attend at the Royal Gaol every morning (except Sunday) not later than 9.00 a.m. for the purpose of interviewing prisoners newly admitted and those about to be discharged.
98. The Chaplains shall hold services for their respective denominations at least once a week at the Royal Gaol, the Carrera Convict Prison, and the Youth Train-ing Centre at hours to be fixed by the Superintendent of Prisons. They shall also visit these Institutions on at least one other occasion during the week, preferably in the evening, for the purpose of individual contacts ·with prisoners.
99. The Chaplains shall regularly visit prisoners who are under restraint, in cellular confinement, or sick in the Infirmaries.
100. The Chaplains shall pay special attention to and visit daily prisoners awaiting execution.
101. The Chaplains shall read the burial service at the funeral of any prisoner who may die in prison, in case provision for the reading of such service is not otherwise made.
102. The Chaplains shall haye the general supervision of libraries and be responsible for the selection of suitable books and magazines therefor, and shall arrange for the compilation of catalogues and the issue and return of books.
lI.03. The Chaplains shall, in consultation with the Superintendent, arrange for suitable members of the public to give concerts, cinema shows, talks, and lectures, for prisoners and shall be present thereat.
104. The Chaplains shall, once a week, read to all prisoners a summary of the news both local and foreign, and also bring to their notice any event.-which they may. consider to be of interest to them ..
105. The Chaplains shall keep a personal record of each prisoner of their denomina-tion. They shall also enter in a journal at each Institution a record of their visits.
106. The Chaplains shall at once communicate to the Superintendent any abuse or impropriety which may come to their knowledge.
107. The Chaplains shall conform to the Prison Rules and shall not interfere with the working of them as regards the safe custody, discipline and labour of prisoners, but shall support the Superintendent in the maintenance of discipline.
108. The Chaplains shall submit, during the month of January in each year, for insertion in the Annual Report of the Superintendent, a report in writing with reference to their work during the preceding year.
109. In case of illness or absence from duty for any other cause of a Chaplain he shall inform the Superintendent in writing and nominate a substitute for the approval of the Governor.
110. Ministers and priests of religious denominations, other than those for which Chaplains are appointed, may be permitted to visit members of their denomination or faith while in prison, provided that the prisoner expresses a desire to be visited by such minister or priest.
(v) The After-Care Officer.
111. The After-Care Officer shall attend at the Royal Gaol daily not later than 9.00 a.m. for the purpose of interviewing and aiding prisoners on discha;rge. He shall also be accessible to ex-prisoners at some office or place in Port-of-Spain.
112. The After-Care Officer shall whenever possible assist all prisoners on dis-charge who, in his opinion, require assistance by way of small money grants, travelling warrants, clothing or tools and shall endeavour to get employment for such ex-prisoners.
113. The After-Care Officer shall regularly visit the prisons and interview prisoners who are about to be discharged with the view of ascertaining their needs as regards clothing, tools and employment.
114. The After-Care Officer shall as far as possible keep in touch with all ex-prisoners and endeavour by all means in his power to keep them from returning to prison.
115. The After-Care Officer shall subn1.it to the Superintendent at the end of each month a statement showing the amount disbursed, and such statement shall be forwarded to the Director of Audit by the Superintendent.
116. The After-Care Officer shall submit, during the month of January in each year, for insertion in the Annual Report of the Superintendent a report on his "York for the preceding year. This report shall contain information as to. the number assisted, the nature of the assistance, the total expenditure, and any other informa-tion considered to be necessary.
117. The After-Care Officer shall conform to the Prison Rules and shall not interfere with the working of them as regards the safe custody, discipline and labour of prisoners but shall support the Superintendent in the maintenance of discipline.
118. In the case of illness or absence from duty for any other cause of the After- Care Officer, he shall inform the Superintendent in writing and nominate a substitute for the approval of the Governor.
(vi) The Matron.
119. The Matron shall reside as ncar as possible to the Royal Gaol. She shall have the care and superintendence of the whole of the female prisoners. The entrance gates of the Female Prison shall be secured by locks of a different type from those used i:q the Male Prison.
120. The Matron shall exercise a close and constant personal supervision over the whole of the Female Prison. She shall visit and inspect daily all parts of the prison where female prisoners are employed or' confined, and shall give special attention to any female prisoner who is sick or under restraint or in cellular confinement. Neither the Matron nor any female officer shall quit the Prison during duty hours without the permission of the Superintendent.
121. The Matron shall from time to time pay a surprise visit to the Female Prison during the night and satisfy herself as to its state. Such visits shall be made at varying intervals and at varying times and not less than once a week.
122. Whenever the Matron is absent, her duties shall be performed by a qualified female officer approved by the Superintendent, and such officer shall have all the powers and perform all the duties of the Matron.
123. The Matron shall take care that no male officer or visitor enters any part of the Female Prison unless accompanied by herself or some other female officer.
124. The Matron shall conform to the Prison Rules and to orders and instructions of the Superintendent in so far as they apply to female officers s.nd female prisoners.
(vii) The Steward and Chief Clerk.
125. The Steward shall, vvith the approval of the Superintendent, assign to each clerk at the Royal Gaol specific duties and shall ensure, by constant personal supervision, that these duties are efficiently performed and that the office work is kept up to date.
126. The Steward shall immediately bring to the notice of the Superintendent any omission or irregularity in the work of the clerks or in their attendance.
127. The Steward shall receive all monies paid in for fines, work orders, prisoners' property and see that same are promptly brought to account and paid into the Treasury.
128. The Steward shall be responsible for the keeping of the Imprest Account and the Cash Book.
129. The Steward shall receive and keep in safe custody the property of prisoners other than clothing.
(viii) The Warrants Clerk.
130. TIle Warrants Clerk shall carefully check all warrants received at the Royal Gaol and immediately draw the attention of the Superintendent or his Deputy to any error or omission in same. He shall carefully compute each prisoner's date of discharge and at once enter all particulars in the Registers, Discharge Journals and Criminal Records.
131. The Warrants Clerk shall compute the amount of fines and other sums to be paid by a prisoner to effect his discharge. He shall preserve unmutilated all warrants and documents in connection with prisoners.
132. The Warrants Clerk shall daily write up the returns of fines paid, and shall keep all statistics up to date.
(ix) The Storekeeper.
133. The Storekeeper shall be l'esponsible for all stores, and for the receipt and issue of same. All deficiencies due to his carelessness or negligence shall be made good by him.
134. The Storekeeper shall keep the Stores Ledgers up to date. 135. The Storekeeper shall daily prepare the Dietary Sheets in accordance vdth the Dietary Scale and shall issue the several articles required for the same.
136. The Storekeeper shall scrutinise all requisitions for stores and forward same for the approval of the Superintendent, drawing attention to any demands which he may consider excessive.
13.7. The Storekeeper shall carefully check all articles supplied by the contractors and immediately report to the Superintendent any defect in the quality or shortage in the quantity thereof.
138. The Storekeeper shall periodically check the weights and scales in use to ensure that they are correct at all times.
139. The Storekeeper shall not deliver up the -keys of the Storeroom to anyone except with the approval of the Superintendent.
140. The Storekeeper shall keep an account of all articles unfit for further use, and-shall prepare a list quarterly for condemnation by a Board of Survey.
141. The Storekeeper shall give full information and allow free access to any officer appointed to check the stores.
142. The Storekeeper shall be aided in his duties by an Assistant Storekeeper to be nominated by the Superintendent.
(xl The Correspondence Clerks and Clerks generally.
143. The Correspondence Clerk shall be responsible for the accurate filing of all correspondence, and shall keep a record of all outgoing letters and see that they are promptly despatched. He shall open in-coming letters, except those marked " secret" or " confidential", and pass them to the appropriate officer to be dealt with.
144. The Clerks stationed at Carrera Convict Prison and the Youth Training Centre shall be under the supervision and orders of the Assistant Superintendent in charge of these stations. Those stationed at the Royal Gaol shall perform the duties which have been assigned to them.
145. All clerks, other than the Steward, the 'Warrants Clerk and the Storekeeper, may be transferred at any time from one station of the Department to another, on the order of the Superintendent.
146. All clerks shall be subject to and be governed by the Civil Service Regulations for the time being in force.
(xi) The School Teachers.
147. The School Teachers shall be seconded by the Director of Education from the Education Department for duty in the Prisons Department.
148. The School Teachers shall give instruction at such hours and at such stations ·of the Prisons Department as the Superintendent may from time to time direct.
149. The School Teachers shall conform to and maintain all Prison Rules and shall be subject to the rules relating to Subordinate Ofncers.
150. The School Teachers shall endeavour by every means in their power to im-prove and reform all prisoners committed to their teaching.
151. The School Teachers shall, towards the end of each year, submit to the Director of Education, through the Superintendent, a programme of work for the ensuing year.
152. (1) The School Teachers shall be entitled to six weeks leave annually, subject to the exigencies of the service, which leave may be taken at a period indicated by the Superintendent.
(2) The School Teachers may be granted sick leave, not exceeding 28 days annually, by the Superintendent of Prisons on the recommendation of the lVIedical Officer.
153. The Male School Teachers shall be entitled to two suits of uniform yearly, in accordance with Schedule II hereto. .
(xii) The Dispensers.
154. Dispensers shall be seconded by the Director of Medical Services from the Health Department for duty in the Prisons Department. They may be granted vacation leave by the Director of Medical Services provided a leave relief is appointed.
155. The Senior Dispenser shall attend at the Royal Gaol from 8 a.m. to 4 p.m. daily and at such other times as circumstances may render necessary.
156. The Senior Dispenser shall be responsible for the drugs and medicines in the Infirmary at the Royal Gaol and for the replenishment of same. He shall also be responsible for the supply of drugs and medicines to the other stations of the Depart-ment, as may be directed by the Medical Officer.
157. The Senior Dispenser shall be aided in his duties by an Assistant Dispenser who shall be in attendance daily at the Royal Gaol from 7.30 a.m. to 4 p.m. and again at 7 p.m. and at such other times as circumstances may render necessary.
158. Two Assistant Dispensers shall reside at Carrera Convict Prison in the quarters provided and shall not leave that prison except with the permission of the Assistant Superintendent in charge. .
159. An Assistant Dispenser shall attend at the Youth Training Centre daily from 7.30 a.m. to 4 p.m. and at such other times as circumstances may render necessary.
160. The Dispensers shall conform to and maintain all Rules and orders relating to the government and administration of the prisons and Youth Training Centre, and shall be subject to the rules applicable to subordinate officers, e;x:cept with regard to leave in which case they will be subject to Civil Service Regula-hons for the time being in force.
161. The Dispensers shall be responsible for all drugs and medicines at their respective stations, the compounding of prescriptions and the carrying into effect of 3;11 ord~rs of the Medical OfIicer with regard to the treatment, diet and clothing of SIck pnsoners whether confined in the Infirmaries or treated as out-patients.
162 .. The Dispensers shall see that the Infirmaries are kept clean and tidy and that pnsoners confined therein behave in an orderly manner. They shall report to the Superior Officer in charge all cases of misconduct or other breaches of the Rules or orders.
163. The Dispensers shall prepare daily a list of the diets entered by the Medical Officer in the Dietary Book and forward these to the Storekeeper as early as possible.
164. The Assistant Dispensers may be transferred at any time from one station of the Department to another, on the order of the Superintendent.
165. The Dispensers shall be entitled to two suits of uniform yearly in accordance with Schedule II hereto.
(xiii) Prison Officers generally.
166. Every officer, with the exception of the Superintendent, the Deputy Superin-tendent, the Assistant Superintendents, the Medical Officers, the Chaplains, the After-Care Officer, the Matron and the Civil Service Clerks, shall be deemed to be a subordinate officer.
167. Subordinate officers except School Teachers and Dispensers shall be selected by the Superintendent, passed as fit by the Medical Officer, and appointed by the Governor subject to twelve months probation. This period may be increased with the approval of the Governor. Should a probationer prove to be unsuitable his services may be dispensed with by the Superintendent. Promotions shall be approved by the Governor.
168. Subordinate officers shall undergo such training during their probation and throughout their service as may be ordered by the Superintendent.
169. Subordinate officers shall strictly conform to and obey all Prison Rules and orders and instructions issued by the Superintendent, Deputy Superintendent or any Assistant Superintendent and shall assist to their utmost in maintaining order and discipline.
170. Every officer or servant of the Prisons Department shall at once com-municate to the Superintendent, or the Deputy Superintendent or an Assistant Superintendent any abuse or impropriety which may come to his knowledge.
171. No officer shall, without the authority of the Superintendent; carry out any pecuniary or business transaction with or on behalf of any prisoner.
172. (1) No officer shall receive any unauthorised fee, gratuity or other con-sideration in connection with his duties as a prison officer.
(2) No officer shall directly or indirectly have any interest in any contract in connection with any prison; nor shall he receive, directly or indirectly, under any pretence whatever, any fee, gratuity or other consideration from any contractor, or from any person tendering or any other person whatever in connection with any such contract.
(3) No officer shall at anytime receive any fee, gratuity or othercOli.sideratioll in connection with the admission of any visitors to any prison or to prisoners.
173. No officer shall allow any familiarity between a prisoner and himself or any other officer or servant of the Prisons Department, nor shall he discuss his duties. or any matters of discipline or prison management, within the hearing of a prisoner.
174. No officer shall by word, gesture or demeanour, do anything which may tend to irritate any prisoner.
Prisons. [C:" 11 -~o. 7,
175. 1\0 officer shall knowingly correspond with or hold any intercourse with any eX-Drisoner or with the friends or relatives of any prisoner or ex-prisoner unless 'with the knowledge of the Superintendent.
176. No ofncer shall use any tobacco or intoxicating liquors within the prison ,valls, except under such restrictions as to time and place as may be laid down by the Superintendent.
177. No officer shall without authority bring in or carry out, or attempt to bring in or carry o'ut, or knowingly allow to be brought in or carried out, to or for any prisoner, any article whatsoever.
178. Every officer or servant of the prison shall submit himself to be searched in the prison if called upon to do so by the Superintendent, or Deputy Superintendent, or an Assistant Superintendent.
179. No officer below the rank of Assistant Superintendent shall be absent from duty without leave from the Superintendent or the Deputy Superintendent, or an Assistant Superintendent.
lSO. An officer provided with quarters shall vacate such quarters when required to do so. Should he die while in service, his family shall give up the quarters when required to do so.
181. It is the duty of every subordinate officer to direct the attention of the Superintendent or the Deputy Superintendent or an Assistant Superintendent to any prisoner (whether he complains or not) who appears to be out of health, or whose state of mind appears to be deserving of special notice and care, and it is the duty of the Superintendent or Deputy Superintendent or Assistant Superintendent, without delay, to bring such cases to the notice of the JUedical Ofncer.
182. l\:' 0 officer in dealing with prisoners shall use force unnecessarily, and in any case in vvhich the application of force toa prisoner is needful, no more force than is necessary shall be used. The striking of a prisoner by an officer is forbidden except in self defence or to protect the life of any other person, or when the officer is ordered to do so under the provisions of these Rules.
183. A subordinate officer shall make an immediate report to a superior or senior officer of any misconduct or disobedience of orders by a prisoner.
184. No officer shall directly or indirectly make any unauthorised communication to representatives of the Press or any other persons in reference to matters which may become knovm to him in the course of his official duties.
185. No officer shall without authority publish any matter or make any public pronouncement relating to prisons or prisoners or the administration of the Prisons Department.
186. A subordinate officer shall, without delay, inform the Superintendent or the Deputy Superintendent or an Assistant Superintendent of any prisoner who desires to make any complaint or to prefer any request.
187. No officer shall be permitted to receive any visitors in any prison 'without the permission of the Superintendent.
188. Officers in charge of stores, tools or other Government property will be held responsible for them, and will be liable to pay for any articles committed to their charge which they cannot satisfactorily account for.
189. A list of tools and other implements shall be kept in every toolroom, work-room or shed and the officer in charge thereof shall be responsible that all tools enumerated thereon are available at all times, and shall produce a receipt for any article issued to another Officer.
190. Subordinate officers shall, when they relieve each other, point out to their successors all matters of special importance, check over all tools or other articles committed to their care, and pass on any orders they may have received.
191. No subordinate officer shall, on any pretext whatsoever, leave prisoners entrusted to his care until he has received an audible and proper acknowledgment for them from the officer to whom he is handing over.
192. Officers shall pay special attention to prisoners in cellular confinement and see that they are provided with necessaries, that their slops are frequently removed, and that they are visited at regular times (special care shall be taken that an interval of more than three hours does not occur bet"\veenvisits).
193. No subordinate officer shall on any account enter the cell of any prisoner at night unaccompanied by another officer and then only in case of illness of a prisoner or qther emergency.
194. Officers will not be allowed to follow or to be concerned in any trade or business, nor, without special permission, to hold any office of a public character unconnected with the Prisons Department.
195. Subordinate officers shall attend Divine Service with the prisoners, and the senior officer on duty will be held responsible for seeing that this is done.
196. Officers entrusted with keys shall not on any pretext leave them about .unguarded nor take them out of the prison. 'When leaviug the prison they shall deliver their keys to the. gatekeeper.
197. Nothing in these Rules shall be deemed to prohibit an officer from making a complaint or request in connection with his duties or conditions of service to the Superintendent, or' to the Colonial Secretary or other superior authority either by himself and forwarded through the Superintendent or forwarded through any Association which is recognised by the Colonial Secretary or other superior authority as being competent to make representations on his behalf.
198. Any such complaint or request as is mentioned in rule 197 if made by an officer in a manner prejudicial to good order or discipline or if made or forwarded contrary to the provisions of rule 197 shall render the officer liable to dismissal or some lesser punishment.
199. Subordinate officers shall reside within the limits of the City of Port-oi-Spain unless special permission ·has been granted in writing by the Superintendent to live outside that limit.
200. A subordinate officer wishing to resign from the service shall give one month's notice in writing to the Superintendent. Absence from duty subsequent to the giving of such notice shall disqualify the officer from pay, except when such absence is the result .of some accident Dr injury sustained while on duty. The Superintendent may, on receipt of notice of resignation, forthwith release the officer from further service.
201. A subordinate officer may be transferred at any time from one prison to another on the order of the Superintendent.
LEAVE.
202. Subordinate officers may be granted, subject to the exigencies of the Service, the following periods of vacation leave on full pay by the Superintendent:-
(a) During the first three years of service not exceeding 21 days in each year.
(b) During the remaining years of service 28 days in each year.
203. After three years of service, vacation leave may be accumulated by a sub-ordinate officer for a period of three years. Vacation leave in excess of 28 days shall be approved of by the Governor.
204. Acting .officers and officers on probation shall not be eligible for vacation leave.
205. Subordinate officers shall be eligible for 28 days sick leave a year; sick leave in excess of this period may be granted on half pay by the Governor. If at the end of three months the officer is still absent hom duty the matter will be reported to the Governor, with an opinion of the Medical Officer, for consideration of the officer's further retention in the Service.
206. Should the Medical Officer report that a subordinate officer's illness is due to misconduct or irregularity of life, the Superintendent shall at once engage a substitute at the officer's expense. If at the end of three months the ofncer is still absent from duty, the matter will be reported to the Governor, with the opinion of the Medical Officer, for consideration of the officer's further retention in the Service.
207. A subordinate officer who is unable through illness to attend at the Pl"ison where he is stationed to be seen by the Medical Officer shall report the circumstances in writing to the Superintendent without delay. The Superintendent shall as soon as possible request the Medical Officer to visit the officer and report on his condition.
208. Subordinate officers shall be entitled to medical attendance and medicines at the public expense. Treatment at the Colonial Hospital shall be paid for by the ofncer at the rate approved from time to time by the Governor.
209. An officer who has been granted -sick leave will not be allowed to return to duty unless the Medical Officer reports him fit to perform such duty.
DISCIPLINE.
210. A subordinate officer may be punished for misconduct, neglect or violation of duty, or breach of Prison Rules or of orders and instructions of the Superintendent . in any of the following \vays :-~ .
(a) Admonition.
(b) Reprimand.
(c). Severe Reprimand.
(d) Fined (in accordance with the scale set out in Schedule III hereto or in
accordance with rule 7 of these Rules).
(e) Special Probation.
(f) Forfeiture or suspension of increment.
(g) Reduced to a lower rank and payor to a lower place in the same rank.
(h) Dismissal from the Service. .
211. In the case of gross misconduct on the part of a subordinate officer, the Superintendent may suspend the officer fronl duty forthwith and report the cir-cumstances to the Inspector.
212. A subordinate officer suspended from duty will be entitled during the period of suspension to half pay only unless otherwise directed by the Inspector.
213. Every punishment inflicted upon a subordinate officer shall be recorded by the Superintendent in the officer's Record of Service.
214. All fines imposed on officers shall be deducted from their monthly pay and deposited by the Superintendent to the credit of the "Good Conduct and Relief Fund" in the Post Office Savings Bank' in the name of the Inspector of Prisons and the Superintendent of Prisons as trustees. This fund shall be administered in accordance with the terms set out in Schedule I hereto.
UNIFOIUvI AND ACCOUTREME::-JT.
215. Subordinate officers shall be provided with uniforms and accoutrement as set out in Schedule II hereto. All uniforms and accoutrement are to be considered as the property of the Government and must be returned to store on the resignation or dismissal of an officer. The value of articles not returned to store will be deducted il-f)m the officer's pay.
2Hi. Officers are to keep all articles of uniform and accoutrement in proper repair at their own expense, except when damaged in the execution of their duty in which case repairs or renewal shall be at the public expense.
FIREARMS.
217. Male officers shall be instructed in the use of :firearms. The Superintendent may order officers in charge of prisoners within or without any prison to carry iirearms.
218. The use of iirearms should be resorted to only in cases of extreme necessity, when all other measures have failed, in order to protect life, to frustrate mass attacks or escapes or mutiny, or to prevent the setting on :fire of Government property.
219. Officers must bear in mind that searching investigation will be made into every case of injury or death from shooting and that charges of murder or manslaughter may follow such investigation.
PART IV.
PRISONERS.
GENERAL TREATMENT.
220. The rules in this Part shall apply to all classes of prisoners except in-so-fal" as they may be inconsistent with the rules as to particular classes of prisoners.
221. Prisoners will not be received at the Royal Gaol earlier than 6.00 a.m. or later than 8.00 p.m. except with the special permission of the Superintendent.
222. N o prisoner shall be received into any prison unless the usual form .. of commitment properly iilled up in all its parts has been put into the hands of the Superintendent or officer in charge.
223. (1) Prisoners on admission shall be strictly and minutely searched by the Reception Officer (males by the Male Reception Officer, females by the Reception Matron), but with all regard to decency. Their clothing shall be removed and kept in the custody of the Reception Officer who shall take due care of them,but no responsibility shall be accepted for unavoidable deterioration through age or to damage done by rats or other pests.
(2) All personal effects (except clothing) and money shall be taken from prisoners and handed to the Steward who shall be responsible for the safe custody thereof.
(3) All property and clothing taken from prisoners on admission shall be entered in the Prisoners' Property Book by the Reception Officer, and signed for by the owner on admission and discharge. The Steward will initial all entries of personal effects and money received by hinl, and his initials shall hold good as a receipt for the articles against which they appear.
(4) Articles which, in the opinion of the Superintendent are too bulky fOl' storage, will not be received. Matches or other articles of a dangerous nature will not be received.
224. The name, age, height, weight, particular marks, and such other measurements and particulars as may be required in regard to a prisoner shall, upon his admission, and from time to time, be recorded by such officer and in such manner as may be directed by the Superintendent.
225. Every prisoner may, if required for purposes of justice, be photographed on reception and subsequently; but no copy of the photograph shall be given to any person unless he is officially authorised to receive it.
226. Every prisoner shall, as soon as possible after admission, be separately examined by the Medical Officer, who shall record the state of health of the prisoner, the class of labour he is iit to perform and such other particu:(ars as the Superintendent may request him to record.
227. Every prisoner shall take a bath on reception and at the times iixed by the Superintendent, unless it shall be otherwise directed in any particular case by these Rules or by the Superintendent or the Medical Officer.
228. If a prisoner is found to have any infectious or contagious disease, or to be in a verminous condition, steps shall at once be taken to treat the condition and to prevent it from spreading to other prisoners.
229. An abstract of the Prison Rules, relating to the discipline, conduct and treatment of the prisoners shall be kept in each division of every prison, and shall be explained to all prisoners on admission.
230. Every prisoner shall be examined by the Medical Officer before being removed to another prison or discharged. No prisoner shall be removed to any other prison unless the l'vIedical Officer certifies that he is fit for removal, and no prisoner due for discharge who is suifering from any acute or dangerous illness shall, unless he refuses to stay, be sent out of prison until in the opinion of the Medical Officer it is safe to do so.
231. A prisoner shall be exposed to public view as little as possible while being removed from or to a prison.
232. Convicted prisoners for whose production at any place an order is issued, shall, while outside the prison be kept in the custody of the prison officers directed to convey them to that place. Unconvicted prisoners shall be handed over at the Royal Gaol to the Police who shall be responsible for their conveyance and custody.
PROHIBITED ARTICLES.
233. (1) No prisoner shall have in his possession any unauthorised article,. and any such article found in the possession of a prisoner shall be deemed to be a prohibited article and may be confiscated by the Superintendent.
(2) No person shall without authority conveyor throw into, or deposit in a prison, or conveyor throw out of a prison, or convey to any prisoner, or deposit with a view to its coming into the possession of any prisoner, any money, clothing, food, drink, tobacco, letter, paper, books, tools or other article whatever. Anything so conveyed, deposited or thrown without a.lthority shall be deemed to be a prohibited article and may be confiscated by the S'lperintendent.
234. All persons or vehicles entering or leaving any prison may be examined and searched, and any person suspected of bringing any prohibited article into any prison, or of carrying out any prohibited article or any property belonging to a prison, shall be stopped and immediate notice shall be given to the Superintendent.
235. (1) No prisoner shall be given Or allowed to have any intoxicating liquor except in pursuance of a written orde:t· "f (~he Medical Officer specifying the quantity to be given and the name of the prisoner '::or whose use it is intended.
(2) No prisoner shall be allowed to smoke or to have in his possession any tobacco except in accordance with such orders as may be given by the Superintendent.
236. Any unauthorised article found secreted on a prisoner, after he has passed to his cell or ward, shall be destroyed, except money, which shall be paid into the Treasury for the use of the Colony.
DISCHARGE.
237. Subject to rules 304 and 307, prisoners shall, on the day of their discharge, be handed over to the Police for conveyance to Police Headquarters for final discharge.
238. Every prisoner whose term of imprisonment expires on Sunday, Christmas Day, Good Friday or Corpus Christi, shall be entitled to his discharge on the previous day, and the Superintendent of Prisons is hereby required to discharge such prisoner on the preceding day accordingly. Prisoners from Tobago whose sentence would expire on the day after the departure of the coastal or other convenient steamer, shall be discharged on the day before.
239. No prisoner shall be discharged before the expiration of his sentence except on the order of the Governor.
FOOD, CLOTHING AND BEDDING.
240. (1) Except when placed on restricted diet, prisoners shall be allowed their daily food in accordance with one of the dietary scales set out in Schedule IV to these i{ules, and the Superintendent of Prisons shall ensure that the provisions supplied for the use of prisoners areof proper quality and weight.
(2) The diet prescribed in any of the scales A, B, C, or D in Schedule IV to these Rules shall be issued on such days as the Superintendent may direct. Prisoners whose religious faith prohibits the eating of beef or pork may elect on admission to prison to have mutton substituted for the said articles of diet wherever any such article is prescribed in the said Schedule.
(3) Prisoners placed on restricted diet as a punishment under the provisions of these Rules shall be given food in accordance with the appropriate scale prescribed in and the provisions of Schedule V to these Rules.
241. A prisoner who has any complaint to make regarding his diet or who wishes it to be weighed to ascertain whether it contains the prescribed quantity, must make his request as soon as possible after the diet is handed to him, and it will be weighed in his presence and in that of the officer deputed for that purpose. Should, however, repeated groundless complaints be made by any prisoner under this rule, with the evident purpose of giving annoyance or trouble, it shall be treated as a breach of prison discipline, and the offender ,vill be liable to be punished accordingly.
242. On the discharge of a prisoner, his own clothes shall be returned to him unless it has been found necessary to destroy or otherwise dispose of them in which case proper clothing shall be provided.
243. (1) The prison dress of every convicted prisoner shall be the following;-
For MaZes.-A shirt, a pair of shorts and a hat, a handkerchief and
a night shirt.
For FemaZes.-A dress and a cap, combination underclothes, a
handkerchief and a nightgown.
(2) Additional underclothing and shoes or slippers shall be supplied on the written authority of the Medical Officer.
244. (1) Male prisoners shall be supplied with a hammock and a blanket. Female prisoners shall be supplied with bed bO.'lrds and a blanket.
(2) Additional bedding shall be supp1i~'4 on the written recommendation of the Medical Officer.
ACCOMMODATION.
245. When accommodation is available every prisoner shall occupy a cell by himself by night. When for medical or other special reasons, it is necessary for prisoners to be associated, no fewer than three prisoners may be placed in one cell, and each shall be supplied with separate bedding.
HEALTH AND CLEANLINESS.
246. Every prisoner shall obey such directions as may from time to time be given by the Superintendent as regards washing, bathing, shaving and hair cutting.
247. The hair of a female prisoner shall not be cut without her consent except on account of vermin or dirt or when the Medical Officer deems it requisite on the grounds of health and the hair of a male prisoner shall not be cut closer than may be necessary for purposes of health and cleanliness.
248. The hair and beard of a Mohammedan shall not be cut except on the written order of the Medical Officer on account of vermin or dirt or on the grounds of health.
249. Every prisoner shall keep his cell, and the utensils, books and other articles issued for his use, and his clothing and bedding, clean and neatly arranged, as may be directed.
250. (1) Arrangements shall be made as far as practicable for every prisoner who is not employed on outdoor work to take exercise daily for one hour, or for such longer periods as the Medical Officer may deem necessary in the case of any individual prisoner, having regard to the state of his health and the nature of his work. The exercise shall take place ~n the open air except in inclement weather.
(2) Every prisoner, except if excused by the Medical Officer on medical grounds, shall take such exercise as ordered.
EMPLOYMENT.
251. (1) Every convicted prisoner sentenced to hard labour, unless excused by the Medical Officer on medical grounds, shall from the beginning of his sentence be employed on useful work.
(2) No prisoner shall be employed on any class of work unless he has been certified by the Medical Officer as fit for that class of work.
(3) For prisoners who are required to work at prison employment, the hours of employment shall not be more than eight a day and arrangements shall,. so far as practicable, be made for such prisoners to work for at least six hours in associated or other employment outside their cells.
252. (1) No prisoner shall be employed on any work not authorised by the Governor.
(2) No prisoner shall, except 'witli the authority of the Governor or in pursuance of special rules, be employed in the service of any other prisoner or of any officer or servant of a prison, or for the private benefit of any person: Provided that the provisions of this paragraph shall not be deemed to apply to the authorised making of articles of furniture, clothing and other. articles.
(3) No prisoner shall be employed in any disciplinary capacity.
253. Arrangements shall be made for the avoidance of all unnecessary work by prisoners on public holidays and by prisoners of the Christian religion on Sunday, Christmas Day and Good Friday, and by prisoners of other religions on their recognised days of religious observance.
254. (1) If at any time it appears to the Superintendent that it is desirable for the maintenance of good order or discipline or in the interests of a prisoner that he should not be employed in association with others, the Superintendent may arrange for him to work temporarily in a cell and not in association.
(2) It shall be within the discretion of the Superintendent to arrange for such prisoner to be employed in association again whenever he considers this desirable, and he shall do so in any case if the Medical Officer so advises on medical grounds, or at the expiration of one month, unless authority is given from month to month by the Inspector.
255. Employment shall be for eight (8) hours daily and shall consist of three classes of labour as follows:-
1st Class.
Males.-Trades and hard bodily labour, as may from time to time be
appointed (either within or outside the walls of a prison).
2nd Class.
MaZes.-Sedentary or less hard bodily labour as may from time to time be·
appointed (either within or outside the walls of a prison and in workshops).
Females.-Laundry, sewing, sweeping and cleaning. Females shall not work
outside the walls of the prison.
3rd Class.
Males and Females.-Light occupation within a prison in accordance with.
the directions of the Medical Officer.
RELIGIOUS iNSTRUCTION.
256. Adequate arrangements shall be made for the provision of religious.
administration to all prisoners.
257. Services shall be held 5,t least once a week at each prison for all prisoners
of the Church of England, Roman Catholic and Non-Comformist faiths. Services for prisoners of other denominations shall be held at such times as may be arranged.
T.-VIII. 21
if there is a sufficient number of prisoners to attend. These services shall be compulsory.
258. Every prisoner shall be required on reception to state his religious denomina-
tion, and shall continue to be treated as a member of that denomination unless and until he satisfies the Superintendent that he has good grounds for desiring to join another denomination.
259. Every prisoner shall, from the beginning of his sentence, be furnished with
such Scriptures and books of religious observance and moral instruction as are recognised for the denomination to which he belongs.
EDUCATION.
260. Provision shall be made for educational classes for the benefit of such prisoners as are likely to profit thereby.
261. A library of books suitable for the use of prisoners shall be provided in every prison.
VISITS AND COMMUNICATIONS.
262. (1) Communications between prisoners and their relatives and friends shall be allowed in accordance with the following rules, subject to such restrictions as may be necessary for the maintenance of discipline and order in prison, and the prevention of crime. No other person shall be allowed to communicate with a prisoner except by special authority.
(2) Every prisoner shall be allowed to write and receive a letter on his admission and he shall be further allowed to write and to receive a letter and to receive a visit at intervals of not more than two months. This shall not be regarded as a privilege which is liable to forfeiture for misconduct under rules 270 and 272; provided, however, that when a prisoner who becomes entitled to a letter or visit under this or any special rule, is at the time subject to dietary restriction or close confinement, the letter or visit may in the Superintendent's discretion be deferred until such restriction or con:6.enement has ended.
(3) The Superintendent may allow any prisoner entitled to a visit to write a letter and receive a reply in lieu of such visit, and may also allow any prisoner to write a special letter and receive a reply or to receive a special visit, in any of the following circumstances:-
(a) Death or serious illness of a near relative.
(b) Business or family affairs of an urgent nature.
(e) Arrangements for obtaining employment or assistance from friends on
release.
(4) The Superintendent may at any time communicate to a prisoner or to his friends, any matter of importance to such prisoner, in case he should not be entitled to write or receive a letter.
(5) Not more than three persons shall be allowed to visit a prisoner at one ·time.
(6)" Male prisoners shall be visited in the sight and hearing of a male officer, .and female prisoners in the sight and hearing of a female officer.
(7) No person shall be allowed to visit a prisoner on a Sunday Qr on a public .holiday except in cases of emergency.
263. Any person committed to prison in default of the payment of any sum which in pursuance of any conviction or order he is required to pay shall be allowed to communicate or to have an interview with any of his friends on any week-day at any reasonable hour for the bona fide purpose of providing for the payment which would . procure his release from prison.
264. (1) Reasonable faCilities will be allowed the legal adviser of a prisoner who .is conducting any legal proceedings, civil or criminal, in which the prisoner is a party, to see the prisoner with reference to such proceedings in the sight but not in the .hearing of a prison officer.
