of the foregoing Regulation, a prescription for any of the drugs shall only be dispensed if the prescription is on one of thope forms, or in the case of an emergency prescription given under the conditions specified in the foregoing" Regulation, if the person dispensing the prescription is acquainted with the signature of the medical practitioner, dentist, or veterinary surgeon by whom the prescription purports to be given, or is acquainted with the person for whose use the prescription is given and has no reason to suppose that the prescription is not genuine; if an official form is not prescribed, a prescription for any of the drugs shall only be dispensed if either (i) the person dispensing the prescription is acquainted with the signature of the medical practitioner, dentist, or veterinary surgeon by whom the prescription purports .to be given and has no reason to suppose the prescription is not genuine, or (ii) the person dispensing. the prescription has taken reasonably sufficient steps to satisfy himself that the prescription is genuine;
(b) the drugs shall not be supplied more than once on the same prescription:
Provided that, if the prescription so directs, the drugs may be supplied on more than. one but not exceeding three occasions, as directed in the prescription; at intervals to be specified in the prescription;
(e) the prescription shall be marked with the date on which it is dispensed,
and shall be retained by the person, firm, or body corporate by whom the prescription is dispensed, and shall be kept on the premises where it is dispensed and shall be available for inspection.
POSSESSION.
7. No person shall be in possession of or attempt to obtain possession of any specified drug unless:-
(a) he is licensed or otherwise authorised to manufacture or supply any
specified drug, or
(b) he is otherwise licensed or authorised to be in possession of any specified
drug:
Provided that this provision shall not apply to any drug supplied to a person for his use by a medical practitioner or i.n accordance with a prescription if that person was at the time of the supply in course of receiving treatment from another medical practitioner in respect of addiction to any of th~drugs or otherwise, and of being .supplied with any of the drugs by or on a prescription given by that last-mentio~f4l...,. practitioner, and did not disclose that fact to the first-mentioned practitlOner befre- -the drug was supplied to him. .
MARKING OF PACKAGES OR BOTTLES.
8. (i) No person shall supply any morphine, cocaine, ecgonine, diacetylmorphine -or their respective salts or any medicinal opium or any extract or tincture of Indian hemp unless the package or bottle containing it is plainly marked with the amount .of the drug in the package or bottle.
(ii) No person shall supply any preparation, admixture, extract, or other :substance containing any of these last-mentioned drugs and coming within these Regulations unless the package or bottle is plainly marked:-
(a) in the case of a powder, solution, or ointment, with the total amount
thereof in the package or bottle and the percentage of the drug in the powder, solution, or ointment;
(b) in the case of tablets or other articles with the amount of the drug in
each article and the number of articles in the package or bottle.
This Regulation shall not apply to any preparation dispensed by a medical practitioner or on the prescription. of a medical practitioner.
RECORDS.
9. Every person who supplies any of the drugs shall comply with the following provisions :--
(a) he shall enter or cause to be entered in a register kept -for the sale purpose
all supplies of the drug purchased or otherwise obtained by him and all dealings in the diug effected by him (including sales or supplies to persons outside the ·Colony) in the form and containing the particulars shown in Schedule I.to these Regulations;
(b) separata registers or separate parts of the register 'shall be used for
(a) cocaine and ecgonine and substances containing them, (b) morphine and substances containing it, (e) diacetylmorphine and substances containing it,
(d) medicinal opium, (e) extract or tincture of Indian hemp;. pro"\-ided that with the approval of the Governor separate registers may be kept for separate departments of a business;
(e) he shall make the entry with respect to any of the drugs purchased or
<Jtherwise obtained by him on the day on which the drug is received and with . respect to any sale or supply by him of the drug on the day on which the transaction is effected; or where that is not reasonably convenient on the day following the day on which the drug is received or the transaction is effected;
(d) where he carries on business at more than one set of premises he shall
keep a separate register or registers in respect of eacJ:1 set of premises; . (~) he shall keep the register or registers in some part of the premises to which it relates'so that it shall at all times be available for inspection in accordance with the provisions of the Dangerous Drugs Ordinance;
(1) he shall not cancel, obliterate, or alter any entry in the register or make,
therein any entry which is untrue j.n any particular. Any mistake in an entry may be corrected by a marginal note or footnote giving the correct particulars. and dated;
(g) he shall furnish to the Governor or to any person authorised by any order
of the Governor for the purpose all information in regard to any purchases by him of the drugs, all stocks held by him of the drugs, and all transactions effected by him in the drugs as may be required by the Governor for the purpose of seeing iha t the provisions of. the Ordinance are a bserved. * -
A medical practitioner who records in a day book particulars of any of the drugs supplied by him to any patient, together with the name and address of the patient and date of the supply, may, in lieu of keeping the register required by this Regulation of drugs sold or supplied by. him, enter separately for each of the drugs in a book to be kept for the purpose references under the appropriate dates to the records in the day book of any supply of the drug. A person lawfully keeping open shop for the-retailing of poisons in accordance with the provisions of section 39 of the Medical Board Ordinance may in lieu of keeping the register 'required by this Regulation of drugs sold or supplied by him enter separately for: each of the drugs in a book to be kept for the purpose references .under the appropriate dates to the entries in the " Poisons Book" or " Prescription Book" kept by him in pursuance of section 43· of the Medical Board Ordinance relating to any supply of the drugs:
Provided that all such books shall at 'all times be available for inspection in! accordance with the provisions of the Ordinance.
Every medical practitioner, dentist arid Veterinary surgeon shall enter or cause' to be entered in a register kept for the sale purpose in respect of each such supply of each of the drugs purchased or otherwise obtained by him the particulars shown; in Schedule I (a).
GENERAL AUTHORISATIONS.
10 .. Any person lawfully keeping open shop for the re'tailing of poisons in accordance with the provisions of the Medical Board Ordinance is hereby authorised-
(a) to manufacture at the shop in the ordinary course of his retail business
any preparation, admixture or extract of any of the drugs;
(b) to carryon at the shop the business of retailing, dispensing or compounding
the drugs, but subject always to the provisions of these .Regulations.
In the event of any such person being convicted of an offence against the Ordinance or of an offence under the enactments relating to the Cq.stoms as applied by the Ordinance, the Governor may by notice in the Royal Gazette withdraw the authorisa-tion aforesaid, if, in the opinion of the Governor, such person cannot properly be allowed to carryon the business of manufacturing or selling or distributing as the case may be, any such drug; provided that the Governor shall, before vvithdrawing the authorisation in the case of any such person, c:;onsult the Medical Board of Trinidad. .
11. Any medical practitioner, or any dentist, or any veterinary surgeon or any person employed or engaged in dispensing medicines at any public hospital or other public institution, being a person duly registered under the Medical Board Ordinance, or any person in charge of a laboratory for purposeS of research or instruction attached to any College, public hospital or other institution approved by the Governor for the· purpose, or the Government Chemist, is hereby authorised so far as is necessary for the practice of his profession or employment in such capacity to be in possession of and supply the drugs.
12. In the event of any person authorised by these Regulations orby any authority granted by the Governor to manufacture, supply or be in possession of the drugs, or any of them, being convicted of any offence against the Ordinance or of an offence under the enactments relating to the Customs as applied by the Ordinance, the
* The Director of Medical Services has been authorised to obtain from persons. manufacturing, selling, distributing or supplying any of such drugs such information in regard to purchases and stocks of and transactions effected in such drugs as' he may from-time to time require.-See KG. 13.6.29. Governor may by notice in the Royal Gazette withdraw the authorisation in respect of such person, if, in the opinion of the Governor, such person cannot properly te allowed to manufacture, supply or be in possession of any such drug.
DELIVERY TO MESSENGERS.
13. No person shall deliver any of the drugs to any person not licensed or otherwise authorised to be in possession of the drugs who purports to be sent by or on behalf of a person so licensed or authorised unless such person produces an authority in writing, signed by the person so licensed or authorised, to receive the drug on his behalf and unless the person supplying the drug is satisfied that the authority is genuine. This Regulation shall not be deemed to apply to medicines dispensed in pursuance cif the foregoing Regulations.
JVIEANING OF "POSS},;SSION."
14. Any of the drugs in the order or disposition of any person shall be deemed to be in his possession.
SHIPS.
15. In the case of a ship not carrying as part of her complement a duly qualified medical practitioner, the master of the ship shall be deemed to be a person authorised to be in possession of the drugs so far as is necessary to comply w'ith the requirements of the Imperial Merchant Shipping Acts, and it shall also be lawful for him, subject to any conditions prescribed by the Secretary of State. to administer and supply the drugs to any member of the crew in accordance with instructions prepared or sanctioned by the Board· of Trade. The keeping of a record of th.e use of the drugs in the official log in accordance with the provisions of the said)Vlerchant Shipping Acts, shall be deemed to be compliance with the requirements of these Regulations as tothe keeping of records.
HOSPITALS.
16. The Governor may exempt from the operation of these Regulations any hospital or oth,er public institution subject to the observance of such conditions as he may by order prescribe.
PREPARATIONS EXEMPTED FROi>1 THE REGULATIONS.
17. These Regulations shall not apply in respect of the preparations named in Schedule II to these Regulations nor to any of the drugs when denatured in a manner' approved by the Governor. The Governor may from time to time by order published in the Royal Gazette add any.other preparation to the Schedule or remove any preparation from the Schedule.
DIVERSION OF DRUGS IN TRANSIT.
18. If any consignment of the- drugs to some destination outside the Colony is brought into any port of the Colony, no person shall, without the licence of the Governor, divert, or cause or procure to be diverted, such consignment to any destination other than the destination to which it was originally consigned. The destination to which the consignment was originally consigned shall be deemed to be the destination stated in the licence, permit or other authority for the export of the consignment granted by the Government of the country of export.
PRESERVATION' OF PRESCRIPTIONS.
19. Prescriptions, records, registers, or other documents required to be retained or kept in pursuance or these Regulations or of any Order made under these Regulations shall be preserved for not less than two years from the date of the prescription or docu:rrient.or the last entry in the record or register, as the case may be.

" Gallae Co., B.P.C. Tinct. Camph. Comp., B.P ..
Regulations made under section 5 of the Dangerous Drugs Ordinance.
SHORT TITLE.
. 1. (1) These RegUlations may be referred to as the Raw Opium. and Ganja Regulations.
ApPLICATION .
. (2) The drugs to which these Regulations apply are raw opium, coca leaves, Indian hemp, resins obtained from Indian hemp and preparations of which such resins form the base.
SALE AND DISTRIBUTioN.
2. Except under and in accordance with a licence granted by the Governor or, in the case of that form of Indian hemp known as ganja, a licence granted by the Accountant General, no person shall, whether on his own behalf or on behalf of any other person, buy or sell, or supply or procure, or offer to supply or procure, or advertise for sale, or otherwise deal in or offer to deal in any of the drugs to whiCh these Regulations apply, whether such drug be in the Colony or elsewhere and whether it be ascertained or appropriated or in existence or not. .
GANJA.
3. All ganja imported into the Colony shall be warehoused in the first instance in the Customs warehouse in Port-of-Spain, and if any ganja is imported contrary to this Regulation; or being imported is not forthwith duly entered or warehoused, the importer thereof, and every other person concerned in the importation thereof or to whose hands any such ganja comes, shall be deemed to have acted in con-travention of this Regulation.
4. No ganja shall be delivered from the Customs warehouse for consumption in the Colony except under the following conditions:-
(a) Such ganja shall be made into separate packets of such weights as the
Comptroller of Customs and Excise may direct;
(b) Each such packet shall be securely sealed by the importer ina manner
to be approved by the Comptroller of Customs and Excise; .
(c) Before any ganja is made into packets as aforesaid, the same shall be duly
entered for consumption in the Colony and the full duty of Customs paid thereon.
5. If any ganja, not being securely sealed as in these Regulations is prescribed or of which such sealing bears any mark or appearance of having been broken or tampered with, is sold or exposed for sale by, or is found in the possession of, any importer or dealer in or retailer of ganja, he shall be deemed to have acted in contravention of this Regulation.
6. The vendor of every packet of ganja sealed as required by these Regulations-shall, on the sale thereof and before delivery to the purchaser; destroy every such seal so as to render the same incapable of l:leing again used for the same purpose.
7. A=uallicences to deal in ganja may be issued by the Accountant General on payment of the sum of $48.00, subject to such terms and conditions as the Account-ant General may in his discretion consider expedient.
8. Every such licence shall be in a form approved by the Accountant General and shall contain the name and description of the licensee and the description of the premises in respect of which the licence is granted, aIl;d shall be dated on the day of issue and expire on the next following 31st day of December. No licence shall be granted on payment of a less sum than the annual fee of $48.00, nor shall any such licence be transferable: Provided that in the event of the death before the 31st day of December in any year of any person to whom there has been issued by the Accoun-tant General ari annual licence to deal 'in ganja, the widow, or surviving husband, or personal representative of such deceased licensee, or the Administrator General or Public Trustee, desiring to carryon the business of the deceased licensee during the unexpired portion of the current year covered by such licence shall be at liberty te> apply to the Accountant General for a temporary licence for that purpose. Such applicant shall submit to the Accountant General a statutory declaration in the form hereunder prescribed, and the Accountant General, if satisfied that the applicant is a person to whom he may issue such a temporary licence shall issue the same in the :(orm approved by him without the payment of any licence fee. .'
9. The Accountant General shall keep a register of all licences granted by himr specifying the name and description of every licensee, the description of the licensed premises and the date of every licence. .
POSSESSION.
10. No person shall be in possession of or attempt to obtain possession of any of the drugs to which these Regulations apply unless-
(a) he is licensed to import or export the drug, or.
(b) he is licensed or. otherwise authorised to supply the drug,. or
(c) he is otherwise licensed by the Governor or authorised by these Regula-
tIons or by any authority granted by the Governor to be in possession of the'
. drug: . .
Provided that this regulation shall not apply to the possession of ganjanot exceeding four ounces avoirdupois.
RECORDS.
- 11. Every person who supplies any of the drugs shall comply with the following provisions :--; .
(a) he shall enter or cause to be entered in a register kept for the sole purPose
all supplies of the drug purchased or otherwise obtained by him, and all dealings in the drug effected by h~ (including sales or supplies to persons outside the Colony) in the form and containing the particulars shown in the Schedule'to these Regulations; .
(b) he shall make the entry with respect to'any of t4e drugs purchased or
otherwise obtained by him on the day on which it is received and with respect to any sale or supply by him of the drug on the day on which the transaction is effected; or where that is not reasonably convenient on the day following the day on which the drug is received or the transaction is effected;
(c) where he carries on business at more than one set of premises he shall keep.
a ,separate register in respect of each set of premises;
(d) he shall keep the register in some part of the premises to which it relates
so that it shall at all times be.available for inspection in accordance with the provisions of the Ordinance;
(e) he shall in addition keep a separate register of purchasers of ganja, and
shall enter or cause to be entered therein the name of each purchaser, his address and any subsequent change of address. his occupation and age if known or approximate age. The name and particulars of each purchaser shall be entered on a separate sheet, and below such name there shall be entered in chronological order the quantities of ganja supplied or sold and the date of each transaction. An alphabetical index of such names shall also be kept and attached to such register;
(f) he shall not cancel, obliterate, or alter any entry in the registers required
to be kept hereunder or make therein any entry which is untrue in any particular. Any mistake in an entry may be corrected by a marginal note or foot note giving the correct particulars and dated and initialled.
GENERAL AUTHORISATIONS.
12. Any medical practitioner, or any person lawfully keeping open shop for the retailing of poisons in accordance with the provisions of section 39 of the Medical Board Ordinance, or any person employed or engaged in dispensing medicines at any public hospital or other public mstitution, being a person duly registered under the said Ordinance,or any veterinary surgeon or any person in charge oia labmatory for purposes of research or instruction attached to any College, public hospital or other institution approved by the Governor for the purpose, or. the Government Chemist or any other public officer, is hereby authorised so f!ir as is necessary for the practice of his profession or employment in such capacity to be in possession of and supply the drugs, but subject always to the provisions of the fDregoing Regulation.
13. In the event of any person authorised by these Regulations Ole by any authority granted by the Governor to be in possession of the drugs, or any of them, being convicted of an offence against tIlls Ordinance or an offence under the enactments relating tp the Customs as applied by the Ordinance, the Governor may by l10tice in the Royal Gazette 'withdraw the authorisation aforesaid in respe.ct of such person if in the opinion of the Governor such person cannot properly be allowed to be in possession of or supply any such drug.
DELIVERY ONLY TO AUTHORISED PERSONS.
14. No person shall deliver any of the drugs to any person not licensed or otherwise authorised to be in possession of the drugs who purports to be sent by or on behalf of a person so licensed or authorised unless such person produces an authority in writing, signed by the persoo. so licensed or authorised, to receive the drug on his behalf and unless the person supplying the drug is satisfied that the authority is genuine. No person shall deliver aJ;ly of the drugs to any person who is under the age of 21 years.
lYIEANING OF "POSSESSION."
15. Any of the drugs in the order or disposition of any person shall, for the purpose of these Regulations, be deemed to be in his possession.
DIVERSION IN TRANSIT.
16. If any . consignment of the drugs to some destination outside the Colony is brought into any port of this Colony, no person shall without the licence of the Governor, divert, or cause or procure to be diverted, such consignment to any destination other than the destination to which it was originally consigned. The destination to which the consignment was originally consigned shall be deemed to be the destination stated in the licence, permit or other authority for the expprt of the consignment granted by the Government of the country of export.
PRESERVATION OF REGISTERS.
17. The registers required to be kept in pursuance of these Regulations shall be preserved for not less than t-Ivo years from the date of the last entry in the register.

I, , of , do solemnly and sincerely declare as follows:- 1. The paper writing hereto attached and marked" A" is a true copy of' the licence issued by the Accountant General of this Colony to , to buy, sell or supply or procure or otherwise deal in or offer to deal in Ganja.
2. The said , died on the day of ,19. and the paper' 1,;riting hereto ·attached and marked " B .. is a certi:ficate of the death of the said
duly authenticated by the Registrar General of this Colony (or the Registrar of Births and Deaths for the district of within this Colony).

CHAP.l"EJi 1~. No. 7
SULPHONAMIDE DRUGS.
Regulations ll1ade under section 2 of The Sulphonall1ide Drugs Ordinance. C.N.
194-7.
1. These Regulations may be cited as the Sulphonamide Drugs Regulations. 2. In these Regulations, " drug" means any of the drugs specified in the Schedule to the· Sulphonamide Drugs Ordinance.
3. No person shall dispose of any drug except to a person registered as a member of the Medical Board of Trinidad or as a drl!ggist or dentist, under the Medical Board Ordinance, or as a Veterinary Surgeon under the Veterinary Surgeons (Registration) Ordinance, or to a person licensed under section 39 of the Medical Board Ordinance,. or except on and in accordance with a prescription given by a person registered as aforesaid as a member of the Medical Board of Trinidad, dentist or veterinary surgeon~
4. Every prescription contemplated by the preceding regulation shall;-
(a) be in indelible writing or typescript and signed by the person giving it
with his usual signature in indelible writing and be dated by him;
(b) specify the address of the person giving it;
(c) specify the name and address of the person for whose treatment it is given
or, if it is given by a registered veterinary surgeon, of the person to whom the medicine is to be delivered;
(d) if given by a licenSt;d dentist, have written thereon the words" for· dental
treatment only", or, if given by a registered veterinary surgeon, the words " for animal treatment only";
(e) indicate the total quantity of the drug to be supplied and the dose to be
·taken.
5. Every person dispensing a prescription entailing the supply of any drug shall comply with the following requirements:- .
(a) the prescription shall not be dispensed more than once unless the person
giving it has stated thereon that it may be dispensed more than once;
(b) if the prescription contains a direction that it may be dispensed a stated
number of times or at stated intervals, it shall not be dispensed any number of times greater than such stated number or otherwise than at such stated intervals;
(c) the person dispensing the prescription shall, at the time of dispensing,
record on the prescription immediately above the signature of the person giving the prescription the name and address of the person supplying the drug and the date on which the prescription is dispensed;
(d) if the prescription may be again dispensed, it shall for a period of two
years, be retained by the person supplying the drug and be made available for inspection by any visitor appointed under the authority of section 61 of the Medical Board Ordinance or by any person authorised by or on behalf of the Deputy Director of Medical Services as hereinafter provided.
6. Any visitor appointed under the authority of section 61 of the Medical Board
Ordinance and any person authorised in writing by or on behalf of the Deputy Director of Medical Services may at any time during business hours enter the premises of any person licensed under section 39 of the Medical Board Ordinance and call for and examine any prescriptions or records' relating thereto with a view to the discovery. of any breach of any of the provisions of the two last preceding regulations. Any person obstructing any such visitor or person authorised as aforesaid in the per-formance of his duty under this regulation shall be guilty of an offence against these Regulations.
7. Any person guilty of a breach of any of these Regulations shall be liable on summary conviction to a fine of four hundred and eighty dollars, or to imprisonment for six months.
CHtl.P1~R 12.
HOSPITALS.
Rules luade under the Hospitals Ordinance. G.N.69-
1. These Rules may be cited as the Colonial Hospitals (X-Ray Services and 1950. -Electrical Treatment) (Fees) Rules.
2. The fees specified in the Schedule hereto shall be the maximum fees payable ior the X-Ray diagnostic services and therapy (including written reports of the Tesults of examinations) specified therein at the Port-of-Spain and San Fernando ,Colonial Hospitals.
3. The fee for electrical treatment shall not be less than $2.40 nor more than $9.60 and shall be fixed according to the nature of the treatment by the Medical ,Officer in charge of the X-Ray Department.
4. The Medical Superintendent or Senior Medical Officer of the Colonial Hospital .shall fix the fee payable for X-Ray services or electrical treatment in each case: Provided that- .
(a) an in-patient in respect of whom a charge of $2.40 per day for accommoda-
tion has been made shall be liable to pay not less than three-fourths of the appropriate maximum fee;
(b) an in-patient in respect of whom a charge of 72 cents per day for accom-
modation has been made shall be liable to pay not less than one-tenth nor more than two-fifths of the appropriate maximum fee;
(e) an out-patient shall be charged three-fourths, two-fifths, or one-tenth, as
the case may be, of the appropriate maximum fee according to his income and family responsibility;
(d) no X-Ray fee for examination of the lungs shall be payable-
(i) in respect of any person sent to either Colonial Hospital by a Medical
Officer of Health as a suspect or contact;
(ii) in respect of any person recommended by a registered medical
practitioner and proved to the satisfaction of the IVIedical Superintendent' of the hospital, Chief Tuberculosis Officer or his, Assistant, or Medical
Officer in charge of the Chest Clinic to be unable to pay any fee;
(e) private patients sent by registered medical practitioners for consultation
shall pay the maximum X-Ray fee for examination of the lungs; .
(1) where a Medical Officer of the Chest Clinic recommends that a patient'
should pay a reduced X-Ray fee for examination of the lungs or shoulclnot be .charged at all, such patient shall be charged such reduced fee or shall not be ·charged i1t all, as the case may be;
(g) no fee shall be payable by any person possessing a pauper certificate;
(h) the Director of Medical Services or any officer authorised by him may
:sanction the payment of a reduced fee or waive payment of any fee by an in-patient or an out-patient whenever the circumstances of any particular case in his opinion justifies such action;
(-i) all fees due by out-patients for X-Ray treatment shall be paid in advance.

Caura Sanatorium and Masson Isolation Hospital Rules. G.N.208-
1950.
1. These Rules may be cited as the Caura Sanatorium and Masson Isolation Hospital Rules.
2. In these Rules-
" Caura Sanatorium" and " Masson Isolation Hospital" mean the hospitals
for the special care and treatment of persons attacked by tuberculosis established at Caura in the Ward of Tacarigua in the County of St. George and at Long Circular Road in the "Ward of St. Ann's in the County of St. George respectively;
" Director" means the Director of Medical Services.
3. The charges hereinafter in this rule specified shall be payable in respect of maintenance dues and operation theatre fees at the Caura Sanatorium, that is to say:-
. (a) Maintenance Dues
(i) For a private single room, $5.00 per day: Provided that the Director
may in any special case and on the recommendation of the Chief Tuber-culosis Officer of the Sanatorium, reduce such fee to any figure not less than $2.50 per day;
(ii) For a private double room, $2.50 per day per patient;
(iii) For general wards, not less than $1.44 per day per patient, not
being domiciled or ordinarily resident in the Colony, as determined by the Director.
(b) Operation Theatre Fees
(i) For a patient occupying a private room, $25.00 for the first operation
and $10.00 for each subsequent operation;
(ii) For a patient not domiciled or ordinarily resident in the Colony'
who occupies a public ward, $25.00 for the first operation and $10.00 for each subsequent operation.
Provided that the Director may, in any special case and on the recomn1endation of the Chief Tuberculosis Officer of the Sanatorium, reduce such fees to any lesser figure.
4. X-ray fees for diagnostic services at the rates specified in paragraph (7) of the table of fees contained in the Schedule to the Colonial Hospitals (X-Ray Services and Electrical Treatment) (Fees) Rules, shall be payable by private patients at the Caura Sanatorium.
5. Private patients at the Caura Sanatorium shall be permitted to have private· nurses who shall be provided with quarters at the Sanatorium, if available, and be accommodated at the Sanatorium, and the actual cost of accommodation shall be a charge payable by the respective private patients. Such nurses shall work in the Sanatorium under such conditions as the Matron and Chief Tuberculosis Officer of the Sanatorium shall determine.
6. A private patient may be required to make a deposit of not less than $120.00 or sign a guarantee in a form prescribed by the Director on admission for treatment at the Caura Sanatorium. .
7. No maintenance dues, oper?-tion theatre fees or X-Ray fees shall be payable· by any patient, being a person domiciled or ordinarily resident in the Colony, who. occupies a public ward at the Caura Sanatorium.
S. Treatment and accommodation at the Masson Isolation Hospital shall be free except for persons not domiciled or ordinarily resident in the Colony who shall be charged a fee of not less than $1.44 per day as determined by the Director.
T.-VIII. 34
CHA "'""'ER 12. No. 11.~
LEPROSY.
Rilles made under section 38 of the Leprosy Ordinance.
1. These Rules may be cited as the Lepers (Occupation of Rooms) Rules. 2. Single rooms may be provided at the Leprosarium at Chacachacare for the use of the inmates whose period of residence in Trinidad or Tobago prior to admission to the Leprosarium is not less than' three years and any sucll room, when available, may be allotted to an inmate at the discretion of the Medical Superintendent of the Leprosarium.
3. For the' occupation of each single room there shall be charged such monthly rental not exceeding $1.20 as the Medical Superintendent may, in his discretion, consider fair and reasonable. Such monthly rental shall be payable in advance to the Medical Superintendent at the Leprosarium.
4. If the rent payable in respect of any such room shall be in arrear for a period of more than 14 days, cir if the occupier of any such room shall contravene any 'of the rules and regulations for the time being in force at the Leprosarium, the Medical Superintendent may remove the occupier of such room to one of the ordinary cottages of the Leprosarium.
CHAPTER 12. No. 17.
NOTIFICATION OF BIRTHS.
Regulations made under section 4 of the Notification of .
Births Ordinance.
1. The prescribed person under section 4 (1) shall be one of the following persons, that is to say-
(a) the father of the child if he is actually residing in the house where the
birth took place at the time of its occurrence; or - (b) the nearest relative of the mother present; or
(c) any person in attendance upon the mother at the time or within six hours
after the birth; or -
(d) the M~dical Practitioner if any, in attendance; or.
(e) the Licensed Midwife, if any; or _
(f) in default of any of the above mentioned persons, then the occupier of
the premises.
2. The prescribed person under section 4 (2) in every area* in which the Ordinance has effect shall be one of the following, viz.-
(a) the Warden; or
(b) the District Medical Officer; or
(c) the District Registrar of Births and Deaths; or
(d) the Officer in charge of the Police Station.
* Areas proclaimed under the Ordinance-Port-of~Spain, Proc. No. 27-1917. San Fernando, Proc. No. 26-1922.
34 (2)
CHAPTER 12. No. 18.
BURIAL GROUNDS.
RURAL DISTRICTS PRIVATE BURIAL GROUNDS REGULATIONS.
Regulations made under section 4 of the Burial Grounds Ordinance. 1. Every application for the licence of a private burial ground shall be made through the Warden of the County.
Such application shall contain detailed particulars of the situation of the proposed burial ground, its area, boundaries and relations to dwellings, and shall be accompanied by a plan of the parcel of land to be used as a privateburial ground.
2. Every owner of a licensed private burial ground shall within 3 months of the making of these regulations, furnish the Warden of the County a plan of such burial ground, showing its situation, area and boundaries.
3. The ViTarden shall keep a register containing particulars of all private burial grounds within his County. The register shall be in the following form:-
COUNTY.
I Remarks.
Area and Exact situation Date licensed Date of discon- No. Owner. boundaries. of burial by Governor tinuance of burials.
ground. in Council. ordered by
Governor. --
4. The Warden and any person authorised in writing by him may at any reasonable time enter and inspect any private burial ground in his County.
5. The owner of every private burial ground shall take proper steps to keep such burial ground in a clean and sanitary condition to the satisfaction of the Warden.
6. (1) Such owner shall give previous notification to the Warden of the district in which the private burial ground is situate of every burial which is to take place therein. Such notification shall be accompanied by the certificate of the Registrar of the district in which the death occurred, referred to in section 30 of the Births and Deaths Registration Ordinance .
. (2) Such owner shall keep or cause to be kept books in which shall be accurately recorded the interment of every person buried in the private burial ground, the name, age and usual place of residence of the deceased, the dates of his death and burial, and. the name of the officiating clergyman or, in his absence, of some responsible person pres~nt at the burial.
7. No coffin shall be buried in any unwalled grave within four feet of the ordinary level of the ground unless it contains the body of a child under twelve years, when it shall not be less than three feet'"below that level.
8. All graves shall be dug to a depth of not less than four feet from the surface. Any person who shall inter or cause to be interred any corpse at a depth of less than four feet or who shall neglect or refuse to comply with the directions of the Warden in regard to the opening or filling in of any grave, shall be guilty of an infringement of these regulations.
9. One body only shall be buried in a grave at one time, unless the bodies be those of members of the same family.
10. No unwalled grave shall be re-opened within seven years after the burial of a person above twelve years of age, except for the purpose of burying another member of the same family, in which case a layer of earth not less than one foot thick shall be left undisturbed above the previously buried coffin; but if on re-opening any grave, the soil be found offensive, such soil shan not be disturbed, and the grave shall be at once closed up and in no case shall human remains be removed from the grave.
11. (1) Every application for permission to re-open an unwalled grave for the purpose of exhumation after the expiration of seven years from the date of burial in the case of a person above twelve years of age and five years after the burial of a child under twelve years of age shall be in writing and addressed to the Colonial Secretary.
Every such application shall contain the following particulars:~
(a) The name in full of the deceased person;
(b) The age of the deceased person at death;
(c) The sex of the deceased person;
(d) The date of burial;
(e) The exact purpose for which the exhumation is required;
(I) The relationship (if any) of the applicant to the deceased; and be accompanied by a copy of the Registrar's certificate of death and a medical certificate stating that the deceased whose remains it is proposed to exhume did not die from any infectious disease; and such application may be approved by the Governor.
(2) In case the remains of a deceased person shall have been placed in a properly closed leaden coffin, the exhumation may be permitted at any time subject to the production of the certificates as required in paragraph (1) of this Regulation.
12. Any person who contravenes any of the above Regulations is liable on conviction to a fine not exceeding $10.00.
PUBLIC BURIAL GROUNDS.
Regulations made under section 4 of the Burial Grourids Ordinance. 1. These Regulations may be cited as the Public Burial Grounds Regulations. 2. Each Warden shall be furnished with a plan of every licensed public burial ground in his County. Each such burial ground shall have a distinguishing name to be recorded on such plan, shall be enclosed in such manner as the Governor may direct, and shall be open daily from daylight to dusk.
3. Every such burial ground shall be laid out in numbered sections as required, and every such section shall be divided into allotments, of 5 by 10 feet each, to be consecutively numbered. "
A portion of every such burial ground may be set aside by the Warden for the burial of paupers only.
The Warden may also, in his discretion, set aside a portion of any such burial ground for any religious denomination.
4. The Warden shall appoint a Keeper of each burial ground, whose duty it will be, under the direction of the Warden, to see these Regulations carried out, to superintend and direct the opening of graves, to cause all interments to be made in such parts as may be laid out and set apart with as much regularity and uniformity as possible.
5. Books shall be kept by the Keeper; in which shall be accurately registered the interment of all persons buried, with the number of the section and allotment, and the date, name and age of the deceased, and also the name of the officiating minister, or in his absence, of some person present at the burial. .
The Keeper shall furnish certified extracts of such books to all persons applying, On payment to the Keeper (for his own use) of one dollar for every certificate.
6. No coffin shall be buried in any unwalled grave within six feet of the ordinary level of the ground, unless it contains the body of a child under twelve years, when it shall. not be less than four feet below that level.
7. All graves shall be opened in sections or rows at such distance apart as the Warden may direct and shall be dug to not less than. the following depths below the surface-
(a) four feet for the body of a child under twelve years of age;
(b) six feet for the body of an adult or the bodies of two children under
twelve years of age in separate coffins;
(c) eight feet for the bodies of two adults buried in one grave in separate
coffins. In case of burial of more than two bodies in separate coffins in one grave, such grave shall be dug to a further depth of two feet for such body. .
8. Any person who shall inter or cause to be interred any corpse at a depth of less than four feet or who shall neglect or refuse to comply with the directions of the Warden or Keeper in regard to the opening or filling in of any grave, shall be guilty of an infringement of these Regulations and shall be liable to a fine of $15.00.
9. One body only shall be buried in a grave at one time, unless the bodies be those of members of the same family.
10. No unwalled grave shall be re-opened within seven years after the burial of a person above twelve years of age, or within five years after the burial of a child under twelve years of age, except for the purpose of burying another member of .' the same family, in which case a layer of earth not less than one foot thick shall be left undisturbed above the previously buried coffin; but if on re-opening any grave, the soil be found offensive, such soil shall not be disturbed, and the grave shall be at once closed up.
11. (1) No body or remains of a body interred in any licensed public burial ground shall be disinterred or removed except upon an orderof a Coroner under the Coroners Ordinance, or under a licence of the Colonial Secretary in any other case.
(2) Every application for a licence to disinter or remove a body or remains of a body from any part of any burial ground shall be in writing addressed to the Colonial Secretary and shall be accompanied by a certified copy of the entry in the Register of Deaths relating to the person whose body or remains it is desired to disinter or remove.
Every such application shall contain the following particulars-
(a) the name in full of the deceased person;
(b) the age of the deceased person at death;
(c) the sex of the deceased person;
(d) the date of burial;
(e) the exact purpose for which the exhumation is required;
(f) the relationship (if any) of the applicant to the deceased.
(3) Every application must be accompanied by a statutory declaration that the facts and particulars stated in the application are true and correct.
(4) In every case where an application for an exhumation shall have been granted, a fee of $10.00 shall be paid by such applicant to the Warden under whose direction the exhumation shall take place, who shall pay the said fee to the credit of the General Revenue.
(5) Every person to whom a licence is granted under these Regulations shall take or cause to be taken all such precautions, and use all such disinfectants, and do all such other acts and things as the Medical Officer of Health may require with a view to the prevention of a nuisance.
(6) Every exhumation or removal under any licence granted under these Regulations shall be carried out under the direction of the Keeper of the burial ground wherein the interment took place, and subject to the supervision of the Medical Officer of Health.
(7) All expenses incurred for the re-opening of the grave, and the restoration of the ground to its original condition to the satisfaction of the Warden shall be paid by the applicant.
12. All persons who, while within the precincts of any licensed public burial ground, shall fail to comport themselves with due order and regularity, or who shall interrupt any religious service, or shall attempt in any manner to profane the sacred ch?-racter of the place, or who shall destroy or in anywise injure tombs, plantations, fences, or enclosures shall be guilty of an infringement of these Regulations, and shall be liable to a fin.e of $15.00.
13. Applications for permission to bury the dead must be made to the Warden or to the Keeper and the applicant must produce to the Warden or to the Keeper the certificate of the Registrar of the district, in which the death occurred, referred to in section 30 of the Births and Deaths Registration Ordinance to prove that the death has been registered or that the Registrar has been duly notified of such death, and the applicant must provide grave diggers and bear all costs of interment.
14. The fees and payments payable to. the Warden or the Keeper shall be as follows:-
For permission to open a grave:
For each adult $ 1.20
For each child under 12 years .72
For exclusive right of burial in perpetuity, the right of con-
structing any chapel, vault, etc.:
For an allotment 5 by 10 feet "I5.VO
For an allotment 10 by 10 feet 24.00
For every q,dditional 5 by 10 feet ... 9.60
All such fees shall be paid to the credit of General Revenue.
If the purchase of an allotment be made within three years subsequent to the fees being paid for opening a grave therein, the amount paid for opening such grave shall be deducted from the purchase money of such allotment.
15. Every person who shall open a grave without permission of the Warden or Keeper, or who having obtained such permission shall neglect or refuse to pay the fees above specified, shall be guilty of an infringement of these regulations, and shall be liable to a fine of $15.00.
16. Interments in the ground set apart for the burial of paupers only shall be made under order or certificate from the Warden, and in such cases no fees shall be payable.
17. (1) If any animal is found tethered, wandering, straying or lying in any public burial ground, the person in charge of such animal shall be guilty of an offence against these Regulations and liable on summary conviction to a fine of $10.00 unle$s he proves to the satisfaction of the court before which he is charged that he had taken proper precautions to prevent the commission of such offence .
. (2) In this regulation" animal" has the same meaning as is assigned to the expression" poundable animal" in the Pounds Ordinance.
18. It shall be the duty of the Keeper to keep the burial ground under his charge clean and in proper order. If any such Keeper shall knowingly and wilfully allow any infringement of these Regulations, he shall be liable to a penalty of $10.00.
OFFENCES IN BURIAL GROUNDS (SAN FERNANDO)
REGULATIONS.
Regulations lllade under section 4 of the Burial Grounds Ordinance.
1. No person shall be allowed to climb or pass over, or pass through any railing, fence, gate or turnstile at, in or around any of the Burial Grounds, except through a gate or turnstile lawfully open at the time. All gates and turnstiles shall be closed daily from 7 p;m. of one day to 6 a.m. of the following day, except at such other time as the Corporation shall sanction.
2. No person shall damage or destroy any grave, monument, headstone, cross or other memorial to the dead, or any building, notice board, ornamental railing, gate -or fence at, in, or around any of the Burial Grounds, grave, monument, headstone, cross or tomb.
3. No person shall steal, cut, break, root up, or otherwise damage, or destroy the whole or any part of any tree, sapling shrub or flower growing in any of the Burial Grounds.
4. No person shall steal, remove, damage or destroy any floral wreath, or other decoration, temporary or permanently placed on, or around any grave, monument, headstone, cross, tomb or railing.
5. No person shall be permitted to be guilty of any riotous, violent, disorderly or indecent behaviour at any burial, any of the Burial Grounds, nor shall wilfully obstruct any such burial or any religious service held at any such burial, in any of the Burial Grounds.
6. Every person who commits any breach of the foregoing regulations in any of the Burial Grounds within the limits of the Borough of San Fernando shall be liable to a penalty of $10.00, or in default, to imprisonment for one month.
BURIAL GROUND (SAN FERNANDO) REGULATION.
Regulation m.ade under section 4 (2) of the Burial Grounds Ordinance. It shall be lawful for the Council to order the removal of any body or the remains of any body in any burial ground under their control from one part of such burial ground to the other:
Provided tJ:tat such removal shall be ordered only where it has been found, after enquiry, that the allotment in whieh such deceased person was interred was not available for the purpose, and that the deceased person did not die of any infectious desease.
CHAPTER 12'. No. 1.
POOR RELIEF.
LOCAL POOR RELIEF BOARDS (PROCEDURE) RULES.
Rules made under section 7 (3) (b) and (c) of the Poor Relief Ordinance, for
regulating the proceedings of Local Poor Relief Boards.
1. Before any person is made the recipient of outdoor relief or is recommended for admission to the House of Refuge, full and careful enquiries shall be made into the circumstances of the applicant by the officers appointed for that purpose.
2. Assistance given as Poor Relief shall be' classified as continuous, temporary or urgent, and any such relief shall be limited to persons who are disabled from earning a living. Such Relief shall not be granted for the relief of distress arising out of unemployment except in special circumstances to be decided by the Local Poor Relief Board. There shall be made to the Secretary, Central Poor Relief Board, a monthly Report of all cases so relieved setting out the special circumstances in each case.
3. The case of every applicant for relief shall be submitted through its Secretary to the Local Board of Poor Relief with the report of enquiry .. The Local Board shall decide whether relief is to be given and whether as continuous, temporary or urgent relief, and shall fix the amount, increase, reduce or discontinue it.
4. Except in urgent cases a report shall be obtained from the Government Medical Officer of the district as to the condition of every applicant for continuous relief.
5. A monthly schedule shall be kept by the Secretary of the Local Board of all applicants for relief, and on this shall be entered the name, age, sex, race, native country, length of residence in the Colony, length of residence in the district, present place of abode, occupation, probability of being self-supporting, infirmity or other cause of the need of relief, names, occupation and place of abode of relatives, amount of relief granted and class of relief.
6. No person's name shall be added to the monthly schedule of allowances after the schedule has been sanctioned by the Local Board of Poor Relief. If any addition should appear to be necessary, a supplementary schedule containing the name or names of the applicants, together with the usual information may be authorised temporarily by the Chairman of the Local Board.
7. When temporary relief is granted to persons who have relatives ;Liable for their support, such relief shall not, without the consent of the Local Board of Poor Relief, exceed one month's ordinary relief.
8. Temporary and urgent relief may be authorised by the Chairman of the Local Board. Such relief must be reported by the Secretary of the Local Board to the Local Board at the next meeting for such decision as the Local Board may think fit.
9 .. Before any applicant is sent to the House of Refuge, the Secretary of the Local Board shall forward to the Resident Superintendent of the House of Refuge an appli-cation initialled by the Chairman for admission, and also a medical certificate as to the condition of such applicant and as to fitness for admission into such institution.
10. Other expenses in aid of applicants, such"as Transport, Burial expenses, etc., shall be authorised by the Chairman of the Local Board.
11. Every month a copy of the monthly schedule with a summary of all expendi-ture shall be forwarded by the Secretary of the Local Board to the Chief Public Assistance Officer.
12. The summary of expenditure shall show separately the total payment for continuous, temporary and urgent relief and also any other item of expenditure during the month.
13. No expenditure from Poor Relief funds for anyone month must exceed one-twelfth_of the annual amount allocated to any Local Board. If it is necessary to exceed this monthly proportion, the excess and the reason therefore must be reported to the Secretary of the Central Board by the Secretary of the Local Board.
14. The Secretary of each Local Board shall keep a Cash Book. Two such books shall be supplied to each Secretary, so that these may be used in alternate months, and at the close of each month the one used .during that month shall be forwarded to the Secretary of the Central Board for inspection.
15. All Poor Relief accounts shall be certified by the Chairman of the Local Board of the district in which the debt has been incurred and forwarded to the Chief Public Assistance Officer.
16. The payment of relief shall be made, as far as is practicable, to the applicants themselves. ViThere payment to a third party is unavoidable, it shall be made only to duly accredited agents of the applicants, and not promiscuously.
17. In all cases where a receipt is given by a third party for money to pay appli-cants in outlying parts of a district, such receipt shall be regarded as provisional only, and shall, as soon as practicable, be supplemented by a receipt from the applicants themselves. . - .
18. Notwithstanding the provisions in the preceding rules, the Chief Public Assistance officer may, on discovering immediately urgent cases, grant temporary or urgent relief. All such cases must be reported at once to the Secretary of the Local Board.
19. Applicants certified as fit subjects for admission to the House of Refuge may be given relief at a mi,nimum rate of 8 cents per day or $2.40 a month either in money or kind at the discretion of the Local Poor Relief Boards. Such relief shall not necessarily be withdrawn because the applicant refuses to enter the House of Refuge, but the circumstances of all recipients of such relief shall be inquired into once in each year.
CHAPTER 13. No.3.
BRUCE STEPHENS TRUST.
Rules of the Provident Fund of the Bruce Stephens Trust made.under the
Bruce Stephen.s Trust Ordinance. .
J. These Rules may be cited as The Bruce Stephens Provident Fund Rules.
2. In these Rules:-
" Bonus" means the sum of money credited to a contributor's account by
the Trustees out of the general fund of the Trust under Rule 7 hereof;
" Contribution" means the sum of money contributed to the Fund by an
emp~oyee under Rule 6 hereof;
" Contributor" means an employee who contributes to the Fund and is
entitled to the benefits thereof;
" Employee" means a whole time employee of the Trust;
"Fund" means tj:J.e Provident Fund established under Rule 2 hereof;
" Salary" means gross salary and includes allowances and other emoluments; " Trustees" means the persons for the time being appointed to be Trustees
of the Bruce Stephens Trust in accordance with Section 8 of the Bruce Stephens Trust Ordinance; .
" Trust" means the Bruce Stephens Trust as defined in the said Ordinance; words importing the singular number only shall include the plural number and vice versa and words importing the masculine gender only shall include the feminine gender.
3. The Trustees shall subject to the provisions here~f, establish, maintain and . administer a fund to be known as " The Bruce Stephens Provident Fund" for the purpose of providing for employees of the Trust the benefits 4ereinafter specified.
4. The Fund shall consist of:-
(a) Contribution and bonuses,
(b) r-nterest thereon, and
(c) Moneys contributed by the Trustees out of the general fund of the Trust
to provide for benefits in respect of past services of contributors.
5. All persons who are .employees shall become contributors to the Fund provided however that it shall not be .compulsory for employees, who were in the service of the Trust prior to the date on- which these Rules shall come into force, to become such contributors.
6. (1) There shall be paid into the Fund at the close of each month by every contributor an amount equal to 4 per cent. of his salary for that month.
(2) The amounts paid into the Fund under this rule shall be deducted from each payment of salary to every contributor by the Trustees.
7. At the close of each month there shall be credited to the account of each contri-butor by the Trustees out of the general fund of the Trust an amount equal to 8 per cent. of such contributor's salary for the month.
8. Interest at the rate of 3 per cent. per annum shall be credited to each contri-butor's account by the Trustees on the 30th June in each year op. the amount then standing to the credit of each contributor's account, provided that in the event of a contributor's account being closed during the course of any year, such interest will be deemed to have accrued from day to day and the proportionate part of such yearly interest shall be credited to his account accordingly.
9. The Trustees shall credit the account of every contributor, who was an em-ployee of the Trust prior to the date on which these Rules shall come into force, with a sum which would be equivalent to the bonuses payable by the Trustees and interest on such bonuses from the date of such contributor having first become an employee to the date he becomes a contributor.
10. The moneys of the Fund or any part thereof may at the discretion of the Trustees be invested by the Trustees in such security or securities as the Trustees shall under the said Bruce Stephens Trust Ordinance be entitled to invest moneys.
11. (1) The Trustees shall cause to be kept proper accounts and books in relation to the Fund.
(2) A separate account for each contributor shall be kept in the books of the Fund in which shall be entered separately all contributions, bonuses and interest.
(3) As soon as the accounts of the year are closed each contributor shall be furnished by the Trustees with a statement showing the amount to his credit at the close of the year by way of contributions, bonuses and interest; such statement shall be deemed to be accepted as correct if no objection shall have been made thereto within thirty days after the date of rendering.
(4) On the death of a conrtibutor or on the termination of the contributor's service with the Trust the account of such contributor shall be closed.
12. So long as a contributor is in the service of the Trust he shall have no claim whatsoever to a refund or to payment of the amount standing to the credit of his account or any part thereof.
13. Contributors to the Fund shall retire from the serviCe of the Trust at the age of fifty-five years unless the Trustees desire to retain the service of such contributor for a longer period. Each contributor shall upon becoming a contributor forward to the Trustees his birth certificate and such further evidence concerning his age as the Trustees may reasonably require.
14. The records of the Trust and the decision of tj:J.e Trustees concerning the age and length of service and amount of earnings of any contributor shall be conclusive and binding on every contributor.
15. In the event of the dismissal of a contributor from the service of the Trust for dishQnesty or other act by which the Trust has suffered loss, the Trustees shall be entitled to deduct from any moneys payable to such contributor hereunder so much of such moneys as is necessary to make good such loss and the balance (if any) shall be payable to such contributor subject however to the provisions of these Rules.
16. Subject to the provisions herein contained:-
(1) In the event of a contributor being permitted or requested by the Trustees
to retire from the service of the Trust on account of ill-health on a certificate of a Medical Board, or for the purpose of effecting economy or in the event of a contributor's death while in the service of the Trust, the whole amount standing to his credit in the books of the Fund shall be payable to him or to his legal personal representative or representatives as the case may be.
(2) If any contributor shall retire from the service of the Trust not having
completed ten years' service, such contributor shall be entitled to receive a sum representing the total of all his contributions and interest thereon together with a sum representing one-tenth part of the bonuses credited to his account and interest thereon in respect of each complete year of such contributor's service.
(3) Should any contributor whose service with the Trust. amounts to ten
years or more retire from the service of the Trust, such contributor shall be entitled to receive the full amount of all moneys standing to the credit of his account at the date of his retirement.
(4) If a contributor is requested by the Trustees to retire from the service of
the Trust (except where such request is on the ground of ill-health or for the purpose of effecting economy) or is dismissed from the service of the Trust for any reason whatsoever he shall upon the termination of his service sl!bject to Rule 15 hereof be entitled to and be paid a sum equal to the amount of his contribution and interest thereon only to the date of retirement or dismissal.
17. The Trustees mav from time to time with the sanction of the Governor in Council alter and amend these rules and with the like sanction may substitute other rules for the management and working of the Fund. Provided always that should such alteration curtail the rights or increase the obligations of contributors, any contributor shall be entitled by notice in writing given within thirty days of any such alteration to retain his rights, privileges and benefits under these Rules.
18. The Trustees may at any time, if they think necessary, suspend contributions and bonuses for any period of time, and may at any time, notwithstanding anything to the contrary herein, close the Fund to new contributors, and may at any time discontinue or wind up the Fund: Provided that if the Fund is discontinued or wound up all moneys standing to the credit of the accounts of .contributors shall forthwith be paid to the contributors.
19. In all matters the decision of the Trustees shall be final and all contributors shall be taken to have accepted this and all the foregoing rules as a condition of their becoming contributors.
CHAPTER 14. No.2.
COMPULSORY EDUCATION.~
Regulations m.ade under section 9 of the Com.pulsory"Education Ordinance.
1. These regulations may be cited as the Compulsory Education Regulations.
INTERPRETATION AND ApPLICATION.
2. In these regulations:
" the Ordinance" means the Compulsory Education Ordinance; " Director" " Primary School" " Secondary School" " Intermediate School"
have the same meanings respectively as defined in the Education Ordinance;
" the Committee" means the Compulsory School Attendance Committee to be
appointed in accordance with regulation 4;
" Schedule" means the schedule to these regulations.
3. The provisions of the Ordinance and these regulations shall apply only to children of or above the age of six years and who have not attained the age of twelve years.
COMPULSORY SCHOOL ATTENDANCE COMMITTEE.
4. A Committ.ee of not less than seven persons shall be appointed by the Governor to advise the Director in carrying out the provisions of the Ordinance and these regulations. The Chairman of the Committee shall be the Director or in his absence, such other member of the Committee as he may appoint .
5. The Committee shall meet at least once monthly to consider the reports sub-mitted by School Attendance Officers, and to advise the Director as regards any action to be taken thereon. Vvhen the cause of non-attendance at school is shown to be lack of food or clothing. consequent upon the poverty of the parent every endeavour shall be made to alleviate these conditions through the medium of philanthropic bodies. The Committee shall take the necessary steps to bring such cases to the notice of the appropriate social service organisations. 6. Proceedings under regulations 14'or 15 shall not be taken by a School Attendance Officer against a parent unless authorised in writing by the Director after consulting the Committee.
DUTIES OF PARENTS.
7. It shall be the duty of the parent of a child to secure the regular attendance of such child at a Primary or Secondary School maintained under the Education Ordinance or at any other school registered as a private school under any law fm-the time being in force requiring such registration or to provide such child with instruction in at least reading, writing, and arithmetic.
8. If a parent claims that he is providing efficient instruction for his child other-wise than at a school as described in regulation 7 the Director may, for the purpose of ascertaining the validity of the claim, examine such child or cause him to be examined to ascertain whether in his opinion the child's knowledge of reading, 'writing and arithmetic is equal to that of an average child of the same age in regular attend-ance at a Primary School.
9. A parent when required so to do shall furnish to a School Attendance Officer or to the Head Teacher of the School which his child should attend, such information as may be required as to absences of such child from or non-attendance at school, and the reasons for such absences or non-attendance.
10. A c:q.ild's attendance at school shall not be deemed regular for the purposes of regulation 7 unless the number of such attendances in any calendar month attains 75 per centum .of the number of meetings of the school during such month, at least two of these attendances being on separate Mondays.
An attendance shall mean attendance throughout the hours specified in regulation 16 of the Code of Regulations for Primary Education.
DUTIES OF HEAD TEACHERS.
11. The Head Teacher or Principal of every Primary, Secondary or registered Private Sehool shall render to the Director on the last day of each calendar month a return in the form in the Schedule showing the name and address of every child in his school who has not attended at least 75 per centum of the meetings of his school, which have been held during that month, together with the number of absences, and the reasons therefor, if known.
DUTIES AND POWERS OF SCHOOL ATTENDANCE OFFICERS.
12. (1) It shall be the duty of a School Attendance Officer:-
(a) to make inquiries as to the irregular attendance or non-attendance at
school of any child reported by the Head Teacher in the manner prescribed by these regulations;
(b) to ascertain and report to the Director any parent who has failed or is
omitting to cause his child to receive efficient elementary instruction in at least reading, writing and arithmetic;
(c) to enforce the provisions of the Ordinance and these regulations, and to
prosecute when authorised so to do any person not complying therewith;
(d) to submit such returns to the Director as may be required; and
(e) generally, to follow the directions laid down for his guidance by the
Director.
(2) Such inquiries may require information as to the name, age, residence, parent, employment or attendance at school of any child.
13. (1) A School Attendance Officer may stop, or cause to be stopped, any child who appears to him to be between the ages of six and twelve years, whom he finds, on any school day between the hours of 8.30 a.m. and 3 p.m., in any street or public place and, whom he suspects to be a truant from school, and make inquiries as in regulation 12, and request the child to direct and accompany him to the residence of the child's parent.
(2) Any such child who fails to stop when requested so to do or fails to reply to any inquiry as aforesaid or gives an untrue reply thereto or fails to direct and accompany the School Attendance Officer may be detained by the School Attendance Ofiicer and taken to a place of safety other than a Police Station as prescribed by section 85 of the Children Ordinance or to a.:ny other suitable place approved by the Director until delivered to the child's parent on application by or on behalf of the parent.
(3) A School Attendance Officer who detains or causes a child to be taken as aforesaid shall, as soon as practicable, cause notice of the detention to be given' to the parent of the child, if the parent is known and can be found.
(4) When a child has been detained as aforesaid and the parent fails to make application for the child within 48 hours of the child being so detained or if the parent is unknown or cannot be found tbe School Attendance Officer shall take such child before a Magistrate who shall make an order that the child be sent to a Certified School in accordance with section 44 of the Children Ordinance.
14. When authorised so to do by the Director in writing a School Attendance Officer may make a complaint before a Magistrate against the parent of any child whom he ascertains in the manner set forth in regulation 13 (1) to be a truant from school and if the Magistrate is satisfied that the parent has failed to make all reason-able efforts to enforce the child's attendance at school h~ may impose a fine of $1.00 to be paid by the parent and in the case of a second or subsequent offence a fine of $2.00.
PROCEEDINGS AGAINST PARENT.
15. (1) If the parent of a child habitually and without reasonable excuse fails to send the child regularly to school or to provide efficient elementary instruction for such child, a School Attendance Officer when authorised so to do by the Director in writing may prefer a complaint against the parent before a Magistrate who may impose a penalty under section 6 of the Ordinance.
(2) Upon imposing any penalty as aforesaid the Magistrate may also make an order that the child shall attend some school named in the order, being either such as the parent selects if accommodation is available therein, or if he does not select any, such public Primary School as the Magistrate considers expedient with due regard to the religion of the child; and the child shall attend that school every time it is opened, or in any other regular manner specified in the order. '
(3) For the purposes of this regulation it shall be deemed a reasonable excuse if the absence of the child from school has been caused by sickness or other un-avoidable cause.
(4) If, upon further complaint by a School Attendance Officer that the parent and child have failed j;o comply with the provisions of an order made under sub-regulation (2) of this regulation and the Magistrate is satisfied that the parent has used all reasonable efforts to enforce compliance with such order the Magistrate may, without inflicting any fine order that the child be sent to a Certified School in accordance with section 44 of the Children Ordinance.
16. In any proceedings before a Magistrate for an order or penalty, the following provisions shall have effect:-
(a) A certificate purporting to be under the hand of the Head Teacher of a
school stating that a child is, or is not, attending that sch.ool or stating the particulars of the attendance of a child at that school shall be evidence of the facts stated in the certificate. .
(b) Where it is alleged by the School Attendance Officer that the child appears
to be of an age between six and twelve years it shall lie on the defendant to prove that the child is not of that age. .
(c) The parent of any child or any member of the child's family, or any other
person authorised by the parent in that behalf, may represent the child during all the stages of the proceedings.
(d) The Magistrate may require by summons any parent of a child to produce
the child before him., and any parent failing to comply with such a summons without reasonable excuse to the satisfaction of the Magistrate, shall be liable on summary conviction to a penalty of $5.00.
(e) .In any such proceedings relating to a chUd the Magistrate shall sit either
in a different room from that in whicl.!- the ordinary sittings of the Court are :q.eld or on different days or at different times from those at which the ordinary sittings are held, or'in such other place as may be approved by the Governor, and no person other than the officers of the Court, officers of the Education Department, School Attendance Officers, and the parties to the case, their solicitors and counsel, and other persons directly concerned in the case shall, except by leave of the Magistrate, be allowed to attend.
17. Except as otherwise provided in these regulations proceewngs against a. parent before a Magistrate shall be in accordance with the Summary Courts Ordinarrce.
