CHAPTER 15. No.2.
WATERWORKS AND CONSERVATION.
Regulations made under section 43 of the Waterworks and Water
Conservation Ordinance.
1. These Regulations may be cited as the Water Improvement Area (Caroni Irrigation) Regulations.
2. These Regulations shall apply to the area described in Schedule A hereto and declared to be a Water Improvement Area.
3. In these Regulations-
." the Area" means the Water Improvement Area referred to in the last
preceding regulation;
"Director" means the Director of Works ilnd Hydraulics; " Irrigation Officer" means the officer appointed by the Governor to be in
charge of the irrigation works in the Area;
"irrigation works" means the works constructed or maintained by the:
Director for ab.str~cting, distributing or using water for irrigation purposes;
"the Ordinance" means the Waterworks and Water Conservation Ordinance ..
4. (1) The Irrigation Officer shall give effect to the directions, general or special,. given to him by the Director 'in the exercise of his powers under the Ordinance and shall also exercise the powers vested in him by these Regulations.
(2) The Irrigation Officer shall report to the Director upon the working of 'the: Area at such. times as the Director may fix, or whenever necessary or advisable, and shall furnish to the Director an annual balance sheet showing the amounts of Water Improvement Rates collected in respect of the Area and the expenditure.on con-struction, management, supervision and maintenance of the works in the said Area. For the purpose of such balance sheet; the Warden of County Caroni shall forward to the Irrigation Officer a monthly return of the Water Improvement Rates collected by him.
(3) The Director may from tipJ.e to time, by writing under his hand, with the approval of :the Governor authorise, ·subject to such limitations as the Governor may think fit, any officer of the Works and Hydraulics Department to exercise any of the powers and to perform any of the duties conferred or imposed on the Irrigation Officer and may in like m,aIl1l;er -and witP.. the ljke .approval withdraw any such. authority. Everything done in pursuance of authority granted under this paragraph. sh.a1l have the same effect as if it were done by the Irrigation Officer. '
5. (1) The Irrigation Officer·may by notice in: writing under his hand require the owner or occupier of any land within the Area to-
(a) remove or lop any tree or remove or keep under control any vegetation,.
or destroy any rats or other vermin,,,which are on any such land and which are' likely to damage any irrigation works;
(b) dear and remove from any watercourse used for the passage of irrigation
water on or through any .such land, any vegetation, refuse, soil or any other obstruction which obstructs or impedes or is likely to obstruct or impede the. natural flow ... 9f water. . (2) Any such notice shall require the owner or occupier to take the necessary action within a reasonable time to be specified in such notice (not being less thath fourteen days from the date of the service thereof) and if any of the requirements mentioned in subparagraph (a) of the preceding paragraph is not complied with within the time specified and to the satisfaction of the Irrigation Officer, the owner or occupier, as the case may be, shall be liable to a fine of one hundred dollars or to imprisonment for three months, and, without prejudice to the foregoing, the Irriga-tion Officer or any person acting under his orders may enter upon any such land and carry out the necessary work at the expense of the owner or occupier, as the case may be, and such expenditur-e may be recovered as a simple contract debt in a suit instituted before any competent court by the Director on behalf of the Government. If any of the requirements mentioned in subparagraph (b) of the preceding paragraph is not complied with (within the time and in the manner aforesaid) the Irrigation Officer or any person acting under his orders may enter upon such land and carry out the necessary work.
(3) In cases where compliance with the requirements of a notice issued under this regulation necessitates or causes the destruction of valuable trees or plants, the Director shall pay to the owner of such trees or plants reasonable compensation for same. If the parties fail to agree as to the amount so payable, the compensation may be recovered by action against the Director before the appropriate court as though the claim for compensation was a claim for damages against a private person and the provisions of section 59 of the Ordinance shall apply in respect of any such claim.
6. The Irrigation Officer may refuse to allow water to be supplied or taken for the irrigation of lands in respect of which the water rate is in arrears, or for the irrigation of lands which are not properly provided with banks for the retention of water or for the irrigation of lands the owners of which have been convicted of an offence against the Ordinance or these Regulations; and the water rate payable upon such land shall be payable notwithstanding such refusal.
7. Any person who shall, without authority, fish by means of a fish trap in any irrigation works shall be liable to a fine of two hundred dollars or to imprisonment for six months.
8. Any person who shall-wilfully camle waste of water conserved by any irrigation works shall be liable- to a fine of two hundred dollars or to imprisonment for six months. -
9. If the owner or occupier of any land irrigated by any irrigation works shall suffer or permit water obtained from such works or from any watercourse or channel cpnnected therewith to run to waste on his land, or shall obtain water for such land from such works, watercourse or cha.nnel in a manner not authorised by the Irrigation Officer such owner or occupier shall be liable to a fine of two hundred dollars or to imprisonment for six months. . 10. (1) The Irrigation Officer may by notice in writing under his hand require the owner or occlipier of any land within the Area to provide to his satisfaction, either individually or jointly with other owners or occupiers, proper field ridges and distribution and drainage channels for the supply, drainage, retention or exclusion of irrigation water.
(2) Such notice shall require the owner or occupier to take the necessary action within a stated time, which shall not be less tb,au fifteen days from the date of the service of the nonce.
(3) A:O:y owntr o~ o-ccupier of land failing to comply with any of the require~ _ments of a notice issued under this regulation shall be liable to a fine of one hundred dollars or to imprisonment for three months, and, without prejudice to the foregoing, the Irrigation Officer or any person acting under his orders may enter upon any such land and carry out the necessary work at the expense of the owner or occupier, as the case may be, and such expenditure may be recovered as a simple contract debt in a suit instituted before any competent court by the Director on behalf of the Government.
11. Any owner or oc-cupier. of land within the Area who shall refuse to allow the passage of irrigation water over his land, or who shall wilfully obstruct such passage, or shall knowingly do, .. or. . allow - to be 35 done, (2)" on his land any ". ac~ which impedes .,.- the normal distribution of irrigation water in the Area, shall be liable to a fine of two hundred dollars or to imprisonment for three months.
12. No person shall, without the authority in writing of the Irrigation Officer, construct any canal, drain, ditch or ford, or deepen or enlarge any existing canal, watercourse, drain, ditch or ford within the Area, and any person contravening this regulation shall beliable to a fine of two hundred dollars or to imprisonment for six months whenever any such unauthorised construction or other work interferes with the proper functioning of any irrigation works.
13. Any person who shall allow any animals to stray on the banks or sides of any irrigation works shall be liable to a fine of one hundred dollars or to imprisonment for three months.
14. The Irrigation Officer or any officer or servant of the "Narks and Hydraulics Department duly authorised in writing by the Irrigation Officer may arrest any person found committing any offence against sections 32, 33, 35 or 68 of the Ordi-nance and regulation 7 of these RegUlations. Any person so arrested shall be delivered as soon as possible into the custody of a constable.
15. The provisions of section 66 of the Ordinance shall apply in respect of the service of notices issued under the provisions of these Regulations.

CHAPTER 16. No. 1.
ROADS.
Regulations made under section 7 of the Roads Ordinance.
1. These Regulations may be cited as the Roads Regulations. 2. In these Regulations, the term "vehicle" includes "motor vehicles" as .defined in Regulation 26 hereof, carriages, waggons, carts, vans, hand-carts, sledges, trucks, barrows, and all other machines for the portage or haulage of goods or persons.
PART 1.
OPENING, DISCONTINUING, ALTERING, ETC., OF MAIN ROADS, BRIDGES OR FERRIES.
3. Every order for the" opening, lengthening, alteration, discontinuance, or per-manent stopping up of any main road or of any bridge or ferry situated on any main Toad shall be made by the Governor in Council, and when made shall be laid before -the Legislative Council.
4. No notice of any such order in the Royal Gazette or in any newspaper shall be required, but the Legislative Council may by resolution refuse to sanction the same, ,and thereupon such order shall be null and void.
5. No,main road or part of a main road shali be permanently stopped up or dis- .continued until the order sanctioning such stopping up or discontinuance ·has been before the Legislative Council for 30 days.
6. A copy of every order under Regulation 3 hereof shall be deposited by the -clerk of the Legislative Council without payment of any fee with the Registrar General to be filed and kept in the registry of deeds.
7. Every such order shall be published in the Royal Gazette and shall thereupon have the force of law.
PART II.
CONVERSION OF MAIN ROADS INTO LOCAL ROADS.
8. Every order for the conversion of a main road or part of a main road into a local road shall be made by the Governor in Council, and when made shall be laid before the Legislative Council.
9. No notice of any such order in the Royal Gazette or in any newspaper shall be required, but the Legislative Council may by resolution refuse to sanction the same, ,and thereupon such order shall be null and void.
10. No main road or part of a main road shall be treated as a local road until the order sanctioning the conversion has been before the Legislative Council for 30 days.
11. A copy of every order under Regulation 8 hereof shall be deposited by the -clerk of the Legislative Council without payment of any fee with the Registrar General to be filed and kept in the :(egistry of deeds.
12. Every such order shall be published in the Royal Gazette and shall thereupon have the force of law.
PART III.
OPENING, DISCONTINUING, ALTERING, ETC., OF LOCAL ROADS,
BRIDGES OR FERRIES.
13. Every proposal for the opening, lengthening, alteration, discontinuance, or-permanent stopping up of any local road, or of any bridge or ferry thereon shall be brought forward by resolution in the Central Road Board after public notice thereof in two successive issues of the Royal Gazette.
14. Every such resolution shall when passed be assented to by the Governor in Council, if he shall so think fit, and a copy thereof shall forthwith be deposited by the Clerk of the Executive Council without payment of any fee with the Registrar General to be filed and kept in the registry of deeds.
15. Assent by the Governor in Council to any resolution for opening or altering any local road, bridge or ferry shall be deemed to constitue and to be a dedication. to the public use of the new or altered local road, bridge or ferry referred to il1! the resolution.
16. Every such resolution when so assented to by the Governor in Council shaH be published in the Royal Gazette, and shall thereupon have the force of law.
PART IV.
LIMITING USE BY THE PUBLIC OF ROADS, BRIDGES OR FERRIES
IN CERTAIN CASES.
17. 'Where-
(a) Communication is from any cause obstructed or likely to be obstructed;
or
(b) Passage is, by reason of blasting or other operations, dangerous in the'
opinion of the Director of Works and Hydraulics, or of a Local Road Board, as· the case may be; or
(e) Injury may, in the opinion of the Director of Works and Hydraulics or
of a Local Road Board, as the case may be, be caused by the passage of traffic or any particular kind of traffic, along or over any public road or any portion thereof or along, over or on any bridge or ferry constructed or maintained at the public expense, the Director of Works and Hydraulics, or in the case of a local road situated in the district of a Local Road Board, the Board, by notice in writing to be published as hereinafter provided, may limit the use by the public of such road or portion thereof, bridge or ferry or may prohibit the carriage or haulage along or over such road or portion thereof, bridge or ferry,_ of such goods or materials as may be specified in such notice.
18. Such notice may contain such directions as to the times and manner and conditions of user of any such road or portion thereof, bridge or ferry as the Director of Works and Hydraulics or the Board, as the case may be, may consider necessary for the plirpose of protecting the same from injury or of ensuring the safety of members of the public.
19. Such notice shall be affixed in the neighbourhood of the road or portion of the' road, bridge, or ferry affected, so that members of the public approaching the same may have the notice brought to their attention.
20. It shall be the duty of members of the public, on seeing such notice or on having it brought to their attention, to make themselves acquainted with the terms' thereof, and any person contravening any direction contained in such notice is liable to a penalty of $24.00. . 21. 'IV-here any notice, or any of the provisions thereof, is or are to continue in operation for more t;han one week, the Director of Works and Hydraulics or the Board, as the case may be, shall submit a copy of the notice for the information of the' Governor in Council; and the Governor in Council may confirm, amend or disallm" the same. ' .
22. If any notice is amended by the Governor in Council, such amended notice shall be substituted for the original notice published by the Director of Works and Hydraulics or the Board, as the case may be, and the provisions of Regulations 19 and 20 shall apply thereto.
PART V.
CARRIAGE OF NIGHT-SOIL, MANURE, CANES, ETC.
23. No night-soil, manure, canes, grass, yard refuse, litter, mud, trash, oil or any other substance likely to cause damage to the surface of a public road or likely to render the use of a public road dangerous to traffic shall be carried or conveyed on or along such road except in a vehicle or receptacle so constructed as to prevent any such substance from falling on to such road, and any person acting, or ordering or suffering any person to act, in contravention of this Regulation is liable to a penalty of $2+.00.
24. Any person who has deposited or caused to be deposited on' or who has negligently allowed or suffered to fall on to any public road from a vehicle or receptacle in his charge or from any part thereof or otherwise any substance mentioned in the last preceding Regulation and neglecting to remove the same forthwith is liable to a penalty of $24.00.
25. Nothing in the two last preceding Regulations shall render any person liable to any penalty therein mentioned for depositing any manure or canes at the side of any pnblic road between the metalled suria<:<e thereof and the side drains: Provided
(a) that such manure is to b,e used in the cultivation of any field immediately
adjoining that portion of the road at the side of which the manure is deposited and is not allowed to remain there for more than 48 hours; or
(b) that the canes have been deposited as aforesaid preparatory to being
carted away and that all trash in connection therewith which is on the public road is removed before 7 p.m., on the day on which the canes are so carted away;
(c) and in either case that the free flow of water on such side drains is not
interfered with.
PART VI.
USE, ETC., OF PUBLIC ROADS.
26. In this Part of these Regulations-
" Motor vehicle" means any vehicle operated or propelled by any form of
engine, motor or mechanical power;
" Motor lorry" means any motor vehicle transporting or intended for the
transport of goods or materials, or for hauling or intended for the haulage of any goods or materials or of any other vehicle so engaged; and includes all similarlyopera:ted, propelled or hauled lonies and vans, whether commercial or private and also motor omnibuses, motor tractors, and steam and motor rollers;
" Motor omnibus" means any motor vehicle kept or used for hire or reward,
or standing or plying for hire or reward, for the conveyance 'of more than six passengers, whether at separate fares or otherwise, but does not include tram cars;
"Trailer" means any vehicle trailed after or propelled by a motor vehicle; " Maximum gross weight" in reference to any motor lorry or trailer means
the combined weight of its chassis, body and load, and in reference to any 'motor omnibus means the combined weight of its chassis, body, and number of persons to be carried;
" Tare" in reference to a motor lorry means the combined weight of the chassis
and body including a full supply of fuel, water, tools and accumulators; and in reference to a " trailer" means the weight of the trailer when unloaded. 554 ,Ch, 16. _,0. 1.J Roads.
27. (a) Prior to any motor lorry or trailer being used on any public road, the owner of such lorry or trailer shall first obtain a permit in 'writing .from the Assistant Engineer of the Works and Hydraulics Department who has charge of the particular road over which it is intended that traffic should take place, or in the case of a local road situated in the district of a Local Road Board, from the Board; and the Assistant Engineer or Board as the case may be, is hereby empowered to exercise a discretion as to granting or withholding such permit and to cancel such permit at any time. Such permit shall be kept upon the lorry or trailer and mlist be produced by any person driving or being in charge of such lorry or trailer when demanded by a member of the Police Force or any Assistant Engineer or Chief Overseer of the 'Works and Hydraulics Department or a Road Officer of a Local Road Board, If any such person fails so to produce his permit, he shall be liable to a penalty of $48.00.
(b) Provided that no permit shall be required for lorries using any road or section of road which the Director of \Norks and Hydraulics or a Local Road Board, as the case may be, has declared by notice published in the Royal Gazette to be open to motor traffic without permit, either generally or for lorries licensed for a prescribed maximum gross weight, irrespective of the load carried.
(c) No motor vehicle shall be allowed to use any public road or any portion of any such road which, in the opinion of the Director of Works and Hydraulics or, in the case of a local road situated in the district of a Local Road Board, of the Board, as the case may be, is specially dangerous to motor traffic, and on which a 'notice to that effect shall be posted.
(d) Self-propelle~ machines used for industrial purposes such as road r~ners, tractors, power shovels and other similar machines shall not be allowed to be used on any public road or any portion of any such road which, in the opinion of the Director of Works and Hydraulics or, in the case of a local road situate in the district of a Local Road Board, of the Board, as the case may be, is unfit for such traffic, and on which a notice to that effect shall be posted, unless a written permit has been first obtained from the Director of Works and Hydraulics or the Board, as the case may be; for the purpose.
28. No vehicle having a greater overall width than 84 inches or a greater height than 10 feet including the load shall be allowed to use any of the public roads of the Colony, nor shall the load in any vehicle be allowed to project beyond these measurements. An Assistant Engineer may, however, issue a permit for roads in his district for a width or heig4t greater than that specified in cases of special pieces of machinery or other articles which cannot be dismantled readily or reduced to the ·specified measurements.
29. (a) Only one trailer, the width of which shall not with its load exceed 84 inches, _ shall be allowed to any motor vehicle: Provided, however, that in special circum-stances the Director of Works and Hydraulics may grant a permit for the use of more than one trailer.
(b) If the trailer is propelled by a tractor alongside, the combined loaded widths shall not exceed 84 inches, unless a greater width is allowed by the Assistant Engineer as provided for in the last preceding regulation. .
30. Every motor lorry and trailer shall have its maximum gross weight and tare in tons painted in white figures and letters 2t inches high on a black ground on a conspicuous place on its right or off side, and this maximum gross weight and tare shall be declared by the owner when applying fora permit under Regulation 27, and a member of the Police Force or any Assistant Engineer or Chief Overseet of the vVorks and Hydraulics Department or the Road Officer of a Local Road Board shall have power to stop any such vehicle using any road in the district under his charge and to inspect, measure or weigh. such vehicle or its trailer or their contents.
31. If any 'motor vehicle is used in contravention of any of Regulations 27 to 30 (inclusive) the owner and the driver of the motor vehicle shall each be liable to a penalty of $480.00, and in the case of a continuing offence to a penalty of $96.00 for each day during which such offence continues.
32. The owner or person in charge of any cart or animal which shall be left -unattended on any public road or the owner or person in charge of any animal which shall be tethered or l:\.llowed to be at large thereon is liable to a penalty of $24.00.
33. It shall not be lawful for any person to haul timber on any public road unless it be placed on wheels and permission has been first obtained from the Assistant Engineer of the district or, in the case of a local road situated in the district of a .Local Road Board, from the Board. Any person acting in contravention of this Regulation is liable to a penalty of $48.00.
34. Any animal suffering from any infectious or contagious disease and found tethered, straying or being driven or ridden on any public road may be seized and -detained by the Assistant Engineer of the district or by any person duly authorised by him so to do, or in the case of a local road situated in the district of a Local Road Board by any member or officer of the Board, and, after examination by a Veterinary Surgeon, such animal shall, if so directed, be destroyed. The owner of any such -diseased animal is liable to a penalty of $48.00.
35. Oxen, bulls, cows, horses and mules when yoked or harnessed may be driven ,on public roads in pairs, but no more than one team of two shall be driven abreast. They shall also be under proper control. Any owner or person in charge of any such .animal acting in contravention of the provisions of this Regulation is liable to a penalty of $24.00.
36. 'Any person who uses any instrument for retarding the progress of any vehicle whereby the road is injured is liable to apenalty of $10.00.
37. Whenever any tree falls on any public ~oad in such a way as'to obstruct the :same, and the oY,;ner or tenant of the land from which such tree shall have fallen refuses or neglects to remove the same, it shall be lawful for the Assistant Engineer -of the district or in the'case of a local road, for any officer of the Local Road Board in whose district such road is situated, or the Warden, or any person authorised by them or any of them to cause such tree to be removed at the expense of the ,owner or tenant of the land from which such tree shall have fallen. All expenses incurred in such removal may be recovered from such owner or tenant as aforesaid.
38. No cedar logs, railway or tramway sleepers or other timber shall remain within the road width for a longer period than is necessary for such logs, sleepers -or timber to be loaded into carts and taken away. No logs, sleepers or timber of any kind shall be deposited in such a manner as to encroach on the actual roadway. Any person failing to comply with this Regulation is liable to a penalty of $24.00.
39. Any person who wilfully or through negligence causes damage to any public Toad, or any wall, bridge, culvert, fence, mile-post, sign-post, drain or other structure appertaining thereto is liable to a penalty of $24.00.
40. The gross load carried on any public road by any vehicle, including the weight of the vehicle itself, shall not exceed 5 tons; provided that the Director of 'Vorks and Hydra,ulics or any officer of the Works and Hydraulics Department deputed by him may grant a permit for a weight in excess of that limit in any special ease.
41. ,No four-wheeled vehicle carrying a load in excess of ten hundredweight on each axle shall be used on any public road unless such vehicle be fitted with proper and effective brakes.
42. The sum of the 'widths of the tyres of any vehicle, other than a motor lorry or trailer-
(a) shall be at least one inch to every three and one-half hundredweight
of gross load where any tyre is less than.,three inches in width;
(b) shall be at least one inch to every five hundredweight of gross load where
no tyre is less than three inches in width.
43. For the purpose of these Regulations, the weight of a person carried on any vehicle shall be taken as 126 lb.
44. If any motor vehicle is used in contravention of any of Regulations ~O to. 42 (inclusive) the owner and the driver of the motor vehicle shall each be liable to a penalty of $96.00.
PART VII.
BRIDLE ROADS.
45. Bridle Roads shall not be used by carts or wheeled vehicles of any description except bicycles and wheelbarrows. Any person using any such road in contravention of this Regulation is liable to a penalty of $15.00: Provided that the Director of Works. and Hydraulics or other officer deputed by him or, in'the case of a Bridle Road in the district of a Local Road Board, such Board or any. officer deputed by such Board, may from time to time, by notice, authorise the use of such roads by wheeled. vehicles and may from time to time withdraw such authority.
PART VIII.
RAILWAY AND TRAMWAY CROSSINGS.
46. Any person owning or in charge of a railway or tramway crossing upQn or over a public road (except crossings of the Trinidad Government Railway) shall maintain it in a safe and proper condition to the satisfaction of the Director of Works and Hydraulics . . . 47. Any person desirous of laying a railway or tramway crossing upon or over a public road shall prepare and submit to the Director of Works and Hydraulics. for his approval a plan showing the road to be crossed for a distance of 100 feet on each side of the proposed crossing and the position and the angle of the crossing' with the centre line of the road. No such angle shall be less than 60 degrees.
48. The rails of any railway or tramway shall riot project above the surface of the road, and a guard rail shall be laid on the inner side of each rail to the full width of the road.
49. At the edges of the road where any railway or tramway enters upon or leaves. it, posts of not less than 9 feet in height shall be placed. Whenever such railway . or tramway shall be in·actual use, red flags shall be hoisted on such posts.
50. When the railway or tramway is' in use a watchman shall be stationed at the crossing to give warning of the approach of any train or vehicle propelled or drawn along the railway or tramway. .
51. No train or vehicle travelling on such railway or tramway shall be propelled. or drawn at a greater speed than 6 miles an hour while passing over any such crossing.
52. All crossings sanytioned under these Regulations shall be repaired, altered or' entirely removed upon notice being given by the Director of Works and Hydraulics. to that effect, and all expenses in connection therewith shall be borne by the person laying down such railway or tramway or by the person who may for the time being be the owner of such railway or tramway.
53. Any person acting or making any omission or default in contravention of any provision of Regulations 46 to 52 (inclusive) is liable to a penalty of $96.00.
PART IX.
TELEGRAPH, ELECTRIC LINE OR TELEPHONE POSTS AND WIRES.
54. Any person desiring to erect any telegraph, electric line or telephone posts or wires on, along or across any public road shall make application in wTiting to the Director of Works and Hydraulics at least three months before the date proposed for the commencement of the work, and shall in the case of the proposed erection of telephone posts or wires, at the same time deliver to the said Director of \Vorks and Hydraulics documentary evidence of the consent. of the Trinidad Consolidated Telephones Limited to the proposed erection of such posts or wires. . 55. Such persons shall, at the same time, submit a complete specification of the 'viork proposed to be done and a plan showing each and every road on, along, or .across which it is proposed to erect such posts and/or wire~ ..
56. Subject to the production of proof of the consent referred to in Regulation 54, ·the Governor upon being satisfied of the necessity for such erection may issue a permit to the applicant embodying such terms and conditions as he may think necessary.
57. Every overhead high or low tension main running across or along any public highway shall be provided to the satisfaction of the Director of Works and Hydraulics 'with some protective device, such as an earthed metallic cradle or network, so erected ;and arranged that in the event of breakage of the main from any cause whatever the live wires must make contact with the cradle or network and be effectively earthed before reaching the highway or telephone or telegraph lines. Fuses, cut-outs, or ·other protective devices at the power station should operate directly the lines ·come in contact with the earthed cradle or network so as to cut off the supply.
58. Power lines must in every case cross above telegraph and telephone lines. 59. The sectional area of any overhead copper line shall not, except with the .consent of the Director of Works and Hydraulics, be less than 16,500 circular mils, corresponding to No. 10 Standard 'Wire Gauge and having a weight of 263 lb. per mile.
60. The interval between any two supports for an overhead line shall not exceed 200 feet, except with the consent of the Director of Works and Hydraulics.
61. Every support for an overhead line shall be of durable material and shall be properly stayed (where necessary) against force due to wind pressure, change of ,direction or unequal lengths of span.
62. All overhead lines shall be attached to insulators, and :;;hall be so guarded that -they cannot fall away from the support.
63. An overhead line shall not in any part thereof be a less height from the ground -than 20 feet, except with the consent of the Director of Works and Hydraulics, and ;shall not be accessible to any person without the use of a ladder or other special appliance.
64. Where an overhead line crosses a road or street the angle between the line and the direction of the road or street at the place of crossing shall.not be less than ,60 degrees, except with the consent of the Director of Works and Hydraulics, and the spans shall be as short as in his opinion is necessary.
65. Every overhead line, including its supports and all the structural parts and .electrical appliances and devices belonging to or connected with the line, shall be .duly and efficiently supervised and maintained as regards both electrical and mechanical conditions.
66. Every line, including its supports, shall be removed on ceasing to be used, -unless the Director. of Works and Hydraulics is satisfied that it is to be brought into use again, in which case, he may allow it to remain for such time as in all the .circumstances he considers reasonable. Should any line not in use be not removed, ·the Director of Works. and Hydraulics may remove the same and recover the cost -thereof from the owner or by the sale of the materials.
UNDERGROUND ELECTRIC LINES.
67. Any low or high tension main crossing any public highway underground .shall be provided to the satisfaction of the Director of Works and Hydraulics with an efficiently earthed metal armouring. . .•
68. The cable shall be laid sufficiently below the surface to prevent it being damaged by the road traffic, and if it is in soil containing chemicals which are liable to affect the armouring in a deleterious manner the cable shall be laid in .suitable troughing filled in with bitumen.
69. Any person who acts in contravention of any of the provisions of Regulations. 54 to 68 (inclusive), and any person who commits a breach of any of the terms. and conditions of such permit as aforesaid, is liable to a penalty of $144.00, and to a further penalty of $5.00 for every day during which any such contravention continues_
PART X.
FERRIES.
70. Ferries under the control of the Government are dedicated to the public. use and are only permitted to be used between the hours of 5 a.m. and 8 p.m. and upon the following conditions, namely: that all persons using them are permitted. to do so on the express condition that the Government is exonerated from all liability for any loss or damage to the persons, animals, or freight carried on the· ferry, although such loss or damage may be caused by the negligence of the Govern-ment's servants or by the state of disrepair or unsuitability of the ferry or any appliance used in connection therewith.
No person shall ride on any animal or remain in any vehicle while using a ferry~
PART XI.
ACCOUNTS OF LOCAL ROAD BOARDS.
71. Every Local Road Boar.d shall keep or cause to be kept the following books for-recording the accounts of the Board:-
(a) A Cash Book.
(b) A Ledger.
(c) An Abstract Book.
72. The Abstract Book shall show the expenditure at any time on each local road in the Road Union, or against each vote in the Board's annual estimate of expenditure,.
73. The Cash Book shall be kept in two volumes to be used for alternate months, and at the end of each month the Cash Book in use for the month shall be forwarded to the Director of Audit together with the vouchers in support of the entries therein.
74. Every payment voucher shall before payment thereof be certified under the·; hand of the Chairman of the Board and one other member thereof.
PART XII.
REMOVAL OF GRAVEL, ETC., NEAR BRIDGE.
75. No gravel, boulders, earth or any other material shall be removed from. within 300 feet of any bridge, aqueduct, syphon, retaining or embankment wall or' embankment fence on or used in co=ection with any public road without per-mission from the Director of Works and Hydraulics or. the Road Officer of a LocaL Road Board as the case may be. LOCAL ROAD BOARD BYE-LAWS AND STANDING
ORDERS.
Bye-Laws and Standing Orders tnade by the Chaguanas, Manzanilla,
Naparima, St. Ann's and Diego Martin, and Tacarigua Local Road Boards under Section 105 of the Roads Ordinance.
1. The Regular Meetings of the Local Road Board (hereinafter referred to as " The Board ") shall be held at the Office of the Board on such dates and at such time and place as the Board by Resolution shall appoint provided that there shall be at least one such Meeting in every month.
2. Subject to the provisions of section 44 (4) of the Roads Ordinance, the Board shall appoint the following officers and shall iL'l: their remuneration, viz.:-
(1) Road Officer
(2) Assistant Road Officer
(3) Secretary
(4) Clerical Assistant, and such other officers as may be deemed expedient.
3. (1) The Road Officer shall:-
(a) superintend the maintenance, repair, formation and construction of all
Local Roads within the Road Union and of all btidges, culverts, drains, ferries, boundary posts, mileposts, milestones, and sign posts, and the clearing of all rivers, ravines and watercourses, within the charge of the Board;
(b) employ and superintend the labour necessary at such remuneration as the
Board may approve for the execution of all work undertaken by the Board and deliver to the Secretary, labour lists in such form as the Board may approve;
(e) prepare and submit all estimates, sketches and specifications and reports
which may be required by the Board; , (d) prepare and submit Monthly Progress Reports in a form to be approved by the Board of all works for which estimates have first been submitted and approved;
(e) forward a half-yearly report of the state of all Local Roads, works and
bridges v<.jthin the Road Union;
(1) certify prior :to payment, all accounts of works done on the Local Roads
within the Road Union for which he will be held personally responsible;
(g) make daily entries in the" Daily Occurrence Book" of all. works and
roads inspected· by him with his observations thereon for the information of the Board; .
(h) constantly inspect the roads and works of the Road Union more especially
those on which expenditure is being incurred, and he will personally direct and give instruCtions to Overseers and men employed thereon;
(i) see that small job contractors properly fulfil their contracts;
(j) personally measure up all work done in his district and be responsible for
the correctness of the reports submitted by him showing the amount performed;
(k) attend each pay for the purpose of identifying the labourers and con-
tractors and satisfying himself as to the proper payment of all concerned.;
(t) report himself daily or at such other intervals to the Chairman as the latter
may direct and unless .otherwise directed, attend all Meetings of the Board.
(2) Before submitting vouchers for payment, the Road Officer must satisfy himself that the works in connection with which he forwards documents for payment have been performed properly and satisfactorily, and that the cost of them does not exceed the amounts allowed by the Board, and that the materials to be paid for have been supplied and are of the kind and value specified.
(3) Any loss incurred by reason of carelessness or neglect ""ill have to be made good by the officer concerned. He will be held personally responsible for all tools, stores, and materials of the Board, and it will be his duty to inspect constantly the former and see that the latter are applied to the purpose for which they are supplied.
(4) No contract or job above $48.00 is to be given out without the consent of the Board and in all cases written contracts or agreements are to be entered into and approved by the Board.
4. The Assistant Road Officer shall, under the supervision of the Road Officer, perform the same duties as those specified for the Road Officer in respect of such roads and works as may be decided by the Board; and shall generally assist the Road Officer.
5. The Secretary shall;-
(1) attend all meetings of the Board and keep the minlltes of the proceedings of such meetings of the Board and of committees of the whole Board; the proceedings and committees of the whole Board are to be recorded in the minutes of the Board and no report to the Board is necessary;
(Z) circulate a copy of such minutes not later than seven (7) days preceding the next meeting of the Board; except when there has not elapsed a sufficient interval of time to permit of his so doing; in which event the minutes shall be circulated at the earliest opportunity thereafter-
(a) :Minutes shall record the names of members attending, all decisions of the
Board, whether made formally ot informally, and shall be signed by the Secre-tary and the Chairman;
(b) In the case of divisions of the members of the Board on Committee of the
whole Board, the minutes shall include the numbers . voting for and against the question, and the names of members so voting or who have declined to vote;
(3) prepare from day to day and keep in his office an order book showing all business appointed for any future day, and any notices of questions or motions which have been set down for a future day, whether for a day named or not. The order book shall be open to the inspection of members at all reasonable hours;
(4) be responsible for the custody of the votes, records, bills, books, vouchers and other documents bf the Board which shall be open to inspection by members of the Board and other persons with the permission granted by or on behalf of the Chair-man, under such arrangements as may be sanctioned by the Chairman. He shall lay before the Board at every meeting all reports and other correspondence received by him;
(5) send to each member a written notice of every meeting of the Board at least .seven (7) days before it is held 'with copies of~ (a) agenda; (b) letters received;
(c) motions; (d) questions and answers tabled; (e) reports (if any); and (1) financial statements (if any). He shall take instructions from the Chairman as to the agenda for any regular or special meeting of the Board, and issue notices thereof to all members;
(6) when required by the Chairman of a Sub-Committee attend the meeting ,of such Sub-Committee;
(7) give his entire time to the service of the Board, conduct all correspondence .of the Board, check all accounts and sign them, thereby certifying to the correctness of allca1culations, copy estimates, keep all accounts, labour and other books of the Board;
(8) after cashing the cheque for the monthly or fortnightly payments, attend ,as paymaster, and conduct the pay in the presence of a member of the Board. He sha.ll be allowed annually the sum of$Z4.00 .for shorta.ges.
6. The clerical assistant shall type all correspondence, notices, minutes, circulars ,and other papers thathe or she may be called upon to type in connection with the business of the Board and shall carry out whatever duties that inay be assigned to him or her by the Chairman or Secretary.
7. In addition to the books required to be kept under the Roads Ordinance, the Road Officer shall prepare and hand to the Secretary who shall keep them in the Office of the Board the following:-
(a) "A Daily Occurrence Book" which shall be a complete record of the
daily movements and actions of the officers, and shall be available for inspection by every member of the Board;
(b) "A Labour List in Duplicate" prepared fortnightly showing in separate
:tabulated columns the names of the labourers, and other workmen, their occupation, addresses, date of work, type of work done, hours worked, name of .streets, rate of pay, amount of pay dra,Yn, by whom supervised, remarks and receipts of workmen as to pay received. At the end of every such Pay List, the Road Officer must certify as follows:-
" I certify that the workmen on the above pay list for $ and
cents were employed on the jobs specified above, that the charges
are fair and reasonable and the statement above set out is true and correct."
The paymaster will on payment of such wages sign the sheet and the member of the Board who witnessed the payment shall sign the same;
(c) "A Detailed Cost Ledger" showing in separate tabulated columns and
in detail the date when the work-started, the date of completion, particulars of work done, Expenditure (Wages $ and Materials $ ) amount ,allowed in estimates, amount transferred from other streets and name of such 'streets, date of resolution of Board authorising such transfer, and requisition number;
(d) "A Triplicate Counterfoil Requisition Book" showing in detail all tools
and/or materials issued from the stores of the Board, for whom delivered, by whom received, date of delivery, name of street on which tools and/or materials ;are to be used, Signature of Receiver, Signature of Storekeeper, balance of 'Stores in stock. A copy of each requisition slip will be kept by the Receiver, the Storekeeper, and the Original by the Secretary; .
(e) "A Triplicate Counterfoil Requisition Book" of a different colour to the
latter showing the names of the stores or persons from whom materials were purchased, a list in detail of materials purchased,by whom received, date of <delivery, name of street on which materials are to be used, all tools, materials, etc., to be ordered under the signature of the Road Officer;
(1) "An Annual Financial Statement" showing in detail the revenue and
expenditure of the year, the amount allocated in the estimates for that year for each road, and amount of tools and materials on hand giving the names of them. This statement must be printed and copies typewritten and circulated to each member and a copy kept in the book by the Secretary;
(g) "A Time Book" which must be kept and signed daily by the drivers
,and also signed by the Road Officer before the pay sheets are made out;
(h) "A DJlplicate Counterfoil Receipt Book" showing sales, receipts, re-
Iunds, and repayments of any kind or of any money received by the Board.
'8. The Chairman shall from time to time as he may think fit appoint one of the members of the Board to be a witness to the payment of its fortnightly or monthly ,accounts by the Secretary-Paymaster. Such Paymaster shall satisfy himself as to the total amount passed by the Road Officer for payment, pay the workmen, and enslire that a full and proper discharge is given for each payment. Such fortnightly ·or monthly accounts to be signed by the mcmber of the Board as such witness as aforesaid.
9. (1) All officers of a Local Road Board who are directly connected with either. the cash or the accounts of the Board or who have control of the executive vvork of the Board shall give security for the faithful and proper performance of their duties.
(2) Such security shall be given by contract with an approved Guarantee Company; and it shall be thc duty of the Local Road Board concerned to sec th2.t
T.-VIII. 36
such security is given and that the renewal premiums due on account of its officers are duly paid. Such premiums inay be paid by the Local Road Board out of the-funds at its disposal.
(3) Unless and until the Central Road Board shall add to the list or shall alter
the amount of the security to be given the following officers of a Local Road Board shall be required to give security to the extent shovfTI_, viz. ;-
Road Officer $lA40.00
Assistant Road Officer 960.00
Secretary ... 1,200.00
Provided, however, that in the event of one officer being appointed to perform the joint duties of Secretary and Assistant Road Officer the amount of the security for the combined office shall be $1,440.00. .
(4) No officer of a Local Road Board of whom security is required shall hold office unless the requisite Security is given, and no salary shall in any case be paid to any such officer unless the required security has been given.
10. All contractors to a Local Road Board shall give security for the proper and efficient execution of their contracts. The premium of such security to be at the discretion of the Board.
11. (1) The Board shall inspect the roads in its charge annuaily. Such inspection may be made either by the Board as a whole or by a Sub-Committee of its members. which the Board may appoint and shall be carried out at such time or times as the Board shall fix for the purpose and at the cost and expense of the Board.
(2) It shall be competent for the Board, for the purpose of this inspection, to divide up, the Road Union under its control into sections and to apportion the-inspection of one section or more at a time either to the Board as a whole or to the- Sub-Committee appointed.
STANDING ORDERS OF THE CHAGUANAS, MANZANILLA, NAPARIMA, TACARIGUA AND
ST. ANN'S AND DIEGO MARTIN LOCAL ROAD BOARDS
. Suspension of Standing Orders.
1. A question, the object or effect of which may be to,suspend any standing order of the Board, shall not be proposed except with the consent of the Chairman" either after notice given or after the expression of the assent of the majority of the members present at the time.
Grdel' of Business.
2. The business of the Board shall be transacted in the following order;
1. Confirmation of Minutes 2. Oath or declaration of new members 3. Announcements 4. Correspondence 5. Reports of Committees 6. Petitions 7. Notice of Motions and Questions 8. Answers to Questions 9. Public Business in accordance with the Order of the Day.
Confirmation oj Minutes
3. The minutes of each meeting shall be written in the Minute Book and copies thereof shall be typewritten and circulated; at the next 'meeting thereafter the-question shall be put that the Minutes be confirmed and in the absence of objection or correction the Minutes as typewritten shall be taken to be confirmed.
Questions to the Chai1'1na-}/.
4. (1) Questions may be put to the Chairman relative to public affairs with which members are officially connected, proceedings pending in the Board, on any.matte-r of administration connected with such Board. '
(2) Questions may also be put relating to a motion or other public matter -{:onnected with the business of the Board.
(3) A question shall not contain arguments, inferences, opinions, imputations, epithets, ironical expressions or hypothetical cases.
(4) A question shall not contain the names of persons or statements not strictly necessary to render charges which the member who asks the question is not prepared to substantiate. A question must not be asked which makes or implies charges ofa personal character.
(5) A question must not be asked for the purpose of obtaining an expression of opinion, the solution of an abstract legal case, or the answer to a hypothetical :proposition, or which might prejudice a pending trial.
(6) A question shall not be asked without notice unless it is of an urgent character and the member has obtained the leave of the Chairman so to ask it.
(7) No member shall address the Board upon any question, and a question must not be made the pretext for a debate.
(8) A question fully answered may not be asked again during the same meeting; provided that a member may put a supplementary question for the purpose of elucidating any matter of fact regarding which an answer has been given, but a supplementary question must not be used to introduce matter not included in the original question.
(9) Questions shall not be put orally except in the case of a question which is put without notice in accordance with the provision of paragraph 6, hereof, and any such question shall be put orally.
A nswers to Questions
5. The answer to every question shall be put in writing, and the question and answer shall be typewritten and laid upon the table and circulated with the minutes of the Board; provided that a member to whom a question is put orally may in his discretion either give an oral answer there and then or require that notice of the question be given. A question not answered at the meeting for which it is first put down shall stand adjourned until the next meeting.
Notice of ~Motions and Manner of giving Notices
6. (1) Motio:ns or questions must be sent or posted to the Secretary eight (8) days before the meeting; or handed to him at the table during the sitting of a Board for the next meeting.
(2) Every such motion or question shall be typewritten and circulated by the Secretary to members not later than seven (7) days preceding the next meeting of the Board.
Rules of Debate
7. (1) A member desiring to speak shall rise in his place and address his observa-tions to the Chairman. When he has finished his observations he shall resume his seat, and any other member wishing to address the Chairman may rise.
(2) If two or'more Inembers rise at the same time, the Chairman shall call on the member who first catches his eye.
(3) A member shall not, except with the consent of the Chairman read his speech, but he may read extracts from written or printed papers in support of his argument, and may refresh his memory by reference to notes.
(4) A member whilst speaking is not to be interrupted, unless out of order.
(5) Except in Committee no member shall speak more than once on the subject under consideration except in explanation or to a point of order or, in the case of the mover of a substantive motion, in reply, but any member may second a motion or amendment by rising in his place and bowing to the Chair without prejudice to his right to speak at a later period of the debate.
(6) A member who has spoken to a question may again be heard to offer explanation of some material part of his speech which has been misunderstood; but he must not introduce new matter.
36 (2)
(7) A member who has spoken may speak again when a new question has been proposed from the Chair such as a proposed amendment on a motion for the adjourn-ment of a debate. .
(8) No member shall impute improper motives to any other member and all personalities are to be avoided.
(9) References shall not be made to any matter on which a judicial decision is pending, in such a way as may prejudice the interests of parties thereto. Amendments .'
(i) When any motion is under consideration in the Board Meeting or Committee
thereof, an amendment may be proposed· to such motion if it be relevant to the motion to which it is proposed.
(ii) An amendment may be proposed to any amendment proposed from the
Chair, or any member of the Board, if it be relevant to the original amendment.
Responsibility for Order
8. (1) The Chairman at a Board or a Committee Meeting shall be responsible for the observance of the rules of order at the meetings of the Board and Committees respectively and his decision upon any point of order is final except upon a substantive motion made after notice.
(2) Any member of the Board deviating from the rules may be immediately called to order by the Chairman or by any Member of the Board; and if any question shall arise as to ,,,,,hether it is or is not iI). order, the question shall be decided by the Chairman.
Breaches of Order.
9. (1) If a member shall show disregard for the authority of the Chair or abuse the rules of the Board by persistently and wilfully obstructing the business of the Board or by using unseemly language, or otherwise, the Chairman shall direct the attention of the Board to the incident mentioning by name the member concerned. A motion may then be made upon which the Chairman shall forthl'l'ith put the question, no amendment, adjournment or debate being allowed: "that such men,ber be suspended from this meeting of the Board". Such suspension shall last until the . next statutory meeting. .
(2) The Chairman, after ha\ring called the attention of the Board or Committee, to the conduct of a member who persists in irrelevance or tedic:)Us repetition either ·of his own argument or of the arguments used by other members in debate may direct the member to discontinue his speech ..
(3) The Chairman shall order . members whose conduct is grossly disorderly to withdraw immediately frolll the meeting of the Board during the remainder of the day's sitting. Should he refuse to compiy "vith this order he may be dealt ''''ith under paragraph 1 hereof of this order.
Questions of 01'der.
10. (1) Upon a matter of order suddenly arising any member may speak, if he does so immecliately, and commences by a statement that he rises to order. The member who was addressing the Chair, must thereupon resume his seat and so must the member who rose to order, ,,,hen he has concluded his appeal to the Chair.
(2) No other member may, except by leave of the Chairman, speak to the point of order, and the Chairman shall, after the statement of the point has been ('oncluded, give his decision thereon, after which the member who was first addressing the meeting shall be entitled to proceed with his speech, giving effect to the ruling of the Chair. '
Committees.
11.(1) A Committee shall be appointed on motion and shall consist of such members as may be nominated by the Chairman.
(2) The Chairman shall name a Chairman and fix the number of members thereof who shall form a quorum.
(3) The Committee shall report its findings to the Board, with any minority reports of any member.' The reports will then be put to the house for adoption or amendment or rejection.
Employment of 111embers in Professional Capacity.
12. No member of the Board shall appear before the Board or any Committee thereof as Counselor Solicitor for any party or in any capacity for which he is to receive a fee or re\vard.
Strangers.
13. Strangers shall be admitted to debates at the meetings of the Board in the place set apart for them but they shall forthwith withdraw if called upon to do so by the Chairman. .
14. These Bye-laws and Standing Orders may be cited as the Chaguanas, Manzanilla, Naparima, Tacarigua and St. Ann's and Diego Martin Local Road Board Bye-Laws and Standing Orders .
. THE ROADS (CHURCHILL-ROOSEVELT AND DEMERARA) REGULATIONS.
In exercise of the powers conferred upon the Governor in Council by section 7 of the Roads Ordinance, Ch. 16 No.1, the Governor in Council hereby makes the following regulations:-
1. These Regulations may be cited as the Roads (Churchill-Roosevelt and Demerara) Regulations. .
2. The public roads described hereunder ar.e hereby declared to be main roads:-
(i) The Road known as the Churchill-Roosevelt Highway in the Wards
of St. Ann's, Tacarigua and Arima, County of St. George, commencing at the 21; mile mark of the Eastern Main Road and running in a more or less southerly direction for a distance of approximately 3,000 feet and thence in a more or Jess easterly direction for approximately 83,328 feet, making a total of 86,328 feet or 16.35 miles and joining the Demerara Road at the 0.00 mile mark.
(ii) The Road known as the Demerara Road in the Ward of Arima, County
of St. George, commencing at the 16.35 mile mark of the. Churchill-Roosevelt Highway and running in a more or less northerly direction for approximately 9! 768 feet or 1.85 miles and joining the Eastern Main Road at the 17.52 mile mark.
CHAPTER 16. No.3.
MOTOR VEHICLES AND ROAD TRAFFIC.
Regulations lllade under Section 77 of the Motor Vehicles and Road
Traffic Ordinance.
PART 1.
GENERAL.
1. These Regulations may be cited as the Motor Vehicles and Road Traffic Regulations.
2. In these Regulations:-
" Ordinance ,,. means the Motor Vehicles and Road Traffic Ordinance;
" at night" means from half an hour after sunset until half an hour before
sunrise, or such other period for the lighting of vehicles as shall be fixed from time to time by the Commissioner of Police and published in the Royal Gazette;
"longitudinal overhang" means the distance measured from the centre of
a hub of a rear wheel to a vertical line let fall from the extreme limit of the body;
"lateral overhang" means the distance measured from the outer face of
the tyre on the rear wheel on the same side of the vehicle (and in the case of dual tyres from the outer face of the outer tyre) to a verticalliue let fall from the extreme limit of the body;
" overall length " in relation to a single vehicle means the length of the vehicle
exclusive of the starting handle, if any, and of the hood, if any, when down, or, in the case of a trailer, the draw-bar, measured between parallel planes passing through the extreme projecting points of the vehicle; and in relation to a combination of .rehicles the length measured between parallel planes passing through the extreme forward projecting point of the tractor and the extreme rear projecting point of the last trailer being towed;
"overall width" means the width measured betwe,n parallel lines passing
through the extreme projecting points of the vehicle exclusive of any driving mirror or of any direction indicator when in operation, and of so much of the distortion of any tyre as is caused by the weight of the vehicle;
"public stand" means an appointed place where public service vehicles
or any particular class of public service vehicles may wait;
" road signal" means lines or marks, direction arrows or words painted or
otherwise marked, with the approval of the Licensing Authority, on the surface of the roadway or on any sign post or elsewhere indicating the manner or direction in which any vehicle is to be driven on any road and the position on such road that any vehicle is to occupy when being driven or being parked thereon and when approaching corners or crossing street junctions;
" schedule" means a schedule to these regulations.
3. For the purposes of the Ordinance the forms set out in the First Schedule, .or forms to the like effect, may be used in the cases to which they refer, with such -variation as the Licensing Authority may consider desirable.
PART n.
REGISTRATION.
4. The owner of a motor vehicle or trailer who desires to register it shall apply to the Licensing Authority and shall furnish him with the particulars set out in -the form of application in the First Schedule.
,
5. On receipt of any such application, with the .particulars and the fee of $4.80, the Licensing Authority shall, subject to the provisions of the Ordinance, assign a separate number to the motor vehicle or trailer and register it by making the required entries in the register. On the registration of a motor vehicle or trailer the Licensing Authority shall furnish the owner thereof with a certified copy of -the entry.
6. If the Licensing Authority is satisfied that a motor vehicle or trailer which has been registered is destroyed, broken up or permanently removed from the ,Colony, he may cause the entries in the register with respect to that motor vehicle ,01' trailer to be cancelled, and may, if he thinks fit; assign the registered number -of the motor vehicle or trailer to any other motor vehicle or trailer.
7. (1) The identification mark to be carried by a registered motor vehicle or trailer in pursuance of section 6 of the Ordinance, shall consist of two plates which must conform as to size, lettering, numbering and otherwise with the following ;provisions :-
(a) (i) Each plate must be'rectangular and bear upon it the index mark
of" P" for private motor vehicles, " T" for goods vehicles, " R " for rented cars, " H " for public service vehicles, " X " for vehicles which the Licensing Authority has exempted from payment of licence fees or duties under section 19 (3) of the Ordinance, and the separate number assigned to the motor vehicle by the Licensing Authority: Provided that when the number 9999 shall have been assigned the Licensing Authority may thereafter from time to time re-assign -the numbers 1-9999 in series, each series having a letter of the alphabet prefixed to the number.
(ii) The mark and number shall be arranged in conformity with the
.arrangement of letters and figures shown on one or other of the alternative diagrams in the Second Schedule: Provided that as regards numbers re-assigned in series the letter of designation shall be prefixed as mentioned in the preceding ,-sub-paragraph (a) (i).
(b) The two plates may, at the option of the owner, be of either of the shapes
shown in the alternative diagrams in the Second Schedule or one of one shape .and one of the other.
(c) The ground of the plate must be black, the letters and figures must be
"White.
(d) All letters and figures must be three and a half inches high; every part
-of every letter and figure must be five-eighths of an inch broad; and the total width of the space taken by every letter or figure except in the case of the figure I, .must be two and a half inches.
(6) The space between' adjoining letters and between adjoining figures
. must be half an inch, and there must be a margin between the nearest part of .any letter or figure and the top and bottom of the plate of at least half an inch; and between the nearest part of any letter or :figure and th\} sides of the' plate of at least one inch.
(f) In the alternative diagram No.1, the space between the upper and lower.
lines must be three-quarters of an inch. In the alternative diagram No.2, the space between the letter and the figures must be one and a half inches.
(2) Letters and numbers as prescribed in the Second Schedule painted on or .otherwise fixed to the motor vehicle or trailer, may be used instead of plates. Any reference to plates in these regulations shall be construed to include a reference· to numbers and letters so painted or fixed, and any reference to the fixing of plates. to include a reference to the numbers and letters so painted or fixed or other delineation of the numbers and figures. .
8. \¥here the 0.W11er of a motor vehicle registered as a private motor car, goods; vehicle or public service vehicle, intends,to use such motor vehicle for any purpose other than that for which it is registered, he sh.fl.ll, before using it for such other purpose, give notice in writing to the Licensing Authority stating his intention, and shall alter the index marks on the identificati<m plates of his motor vehicle accordingly.,
N a motor vehicle shall be used for purposes other than that for which it is registered, "without previously complying with the provisions of this regulation.
9. The plates forming the identification mark shall be fixed, one on the front and the other on the back of the motor vehicle or trailer, in an upright position, so· that every letter or figure on the plate is upright and easily· distinguishable, in the case of the plate placed on the front of the motor vehicle or trailer from in front of the motor vehiCle or trailer, and in the case of the plate placed on the back, from behind the motor vehicle or trailer. .
In the case of a motor cycle, the plate fixed on the front of the cycle may, if it is a plate having duplicate faces conforming with the Second Schedule be fixed so that from whichever side the cycle is viewed the letters or figures on one or other face of the plate are easily distinguishable, though they may not be distinguishable from the front of the cycle.
Subject to the provisions of this regulation, the plates forming the identification mark shall be fixed on the motor vehicle or trailer in the position indicated in the particulars given in the application for the registration of the motor vehicle or trailer or subsequently furnished to the Licensing Authority or if he is not satisfied with the position so indicated, in such a position as he directs.
No motor vehicle shall be used on any road unless the plates forming the identification mark are clean and maintained in such condition as to render each letter or figure readily distinguishable.
10. vvilenever a motor vehicle or traiJeris used on a public road at night a lamp' shall be kept burning on the motor vehicle, or trailer so contrived as to illuminate by means of refiection, transparency, or otherwise, and render easily distinguishable, every letter or figure on the identificatioJ;l plate fixed all the back of the motor vehicle or trailer.
In the application of this regulation to'a motor cycle, the plate fixed on the front of the cycle may, if desired, be substituted for the plate fixed on the back of the cycle.
11. (1) The Licensing Authority may assign to thc holder of a dealer's general lkence such number of general identification marks as he thinks fit. The marks shall be such as the Licen~ing Authority directs in each case:
Provided that-
(a) they shall consist of two plates, each bearing some' distinguishing letter'
or letters, and each having placed thereon or annexed thereto some distinguishing-numb~r; and
(b) the 'colouring of the plates shall be different from that used for the plates.
fanning the ordinary identification mark; and
(c) the letiering and numbering of the plates shall, so far as possible,ibe-
similar to those required in the case of the plates forming the ordinafY' identification mark.
(2) Every holder of a dealer's general licence to whom general identificat~oll' marks have been assigned shall keep a Demonstration Book, and on every occaSlDn on 'which such'identification mark is used on a motor vehicle he shall make or cause ,to be made in the Demonstration 'Book an immediate entry of the following. particulars ;- '
Date. Identification mark. Description of motor vehicle on which used. Driver's name. Time of departure from gan~,ge. Time of return to garage. Purpose for which the motor vehicle is used.
(3) The provisions of these regulations which relate to the fixing and illumination of identification plates shall apply to the plates forming the general identification mark as they apply to the plates forming the ordinary identification mark.
(4) A motor vehicle used under the authority of a -dealer's general licence shall not be used for any purposes other than those specified in section 21 of the Ordinance and shall not be used for private or trade purposes either by the dealer or by any other person.
1IA. Every O\vner of a rented car shall keep a record book and on every occasion on which such car is hired out he' shall. make or cause to be made in such record book an immediate entry of the following particulars:-
Date. Identification mark of rented car. Hirer's name and address. Time vehicle is hired out. Period for which vehicle is hired out. Time and date vehicle is returned .
. PART III.
LICENSING.
12. (1) The oY'mer of a registered motor vehicle or trailer who desires to licence it shall apply to the Licensing Authority and shall furnish him with the particulars set out in the appropriate form in the First Schedule.
(2) On receipt of such application the Licensing Authority, if satisfied that the provisions of the Ordinance and these Regulations have been complied with, shall issue a licence' in the form in the First Schedule.
(3) The Licensing Authority shall not issue a licence for any public service vehicle or goods vehicle, until he has a valid certificate. from the Inspector in the Form in the First Schedule.
(4)VV"heneverthe licence in respect of a motor vehicle or trailer has been cancelled or suspended, the owner shall, within seven days of such cancellation or suspension, return the relative licence to the Licensing Authority or otherwise satisfy the Licensing Authority of his inability to return, the same.
13. The licence shall be overprinted with an index mark;, indicating the class of vehicle licensed. The index mark shall be the letter or lett.ers shown opposite to the appropriate class ·of vehicle giv~n below:- .
Index mark.
Private motor car P
Go'ods vehicle . - G
Motor omnibus and freight passenger vehicle o
. iIiring car or taxi H
Rented car R
Trailer T -
Motor cycle C
Motor vehicle exempt from licence duty under section 19(2) ... E
Motor vehicle exempt from licence duty under section 19 (3). A duplicate licence issued under section 18 shall be overprinted with the letter D and the index mark shall be written on the licence.
14.' The licence shall, on every occasion when the motor vehicle is used on a road, be carried on the motor vehicle in a waterproof holder of a type approved by the Licensing Authority, so that the front of the licence is displayed and can be read without removing it from the holder. The licence holder shall be fixed to the motor vehicle on the left or near side of the vehicle and in such a position that the licence is upright and can be easily seen and read by a person standing on the left or near side of the vehicle; but in the case of a motor vehicle fitted with a windscreen screening the driver's seat, the licence holder shall be fixed on or close to such windscreen: Provided that if the Licensing Authority is satisfied that waterproof holders are not available in the Colony, and a notice to this effect has been published in the Royal Gazette, the licence, if not carried as in this regulation provided shall be either pasted on the inside of the glass windshield screening the driver's seat and on the lower left hand side thereof, or carried by the driver. In the latter case, the licence shall be produced on demand to any constable.
15. The owner of every public service ,:,ehicle shall keep distinctly painted on the outside of such vehicle the number of persons which, under the terms of the licence, may be carried in such vehicle. Such number shall bepaintcd in conspicuous figures and shall ordinarily be placed in the following positions:-
(a) in the case of a taxi, on the outside of the right front door;
(b) in the case of a motor omnibus, on the outside of the driver's seat.
PART IV.
DRIVING AND CONDUCTOR'S PERMITS. (j
16. A person who desires to obtain the grant or renewal of a driving permit to drive a motor vehicle shall apply to the Licensing Authority and furnish him with the particulars set out in the form in the First Schedule.
17. If the Licensing Authority is satisfied that the provisions of the Ordinance and these regulat~ons have been complied with he shall, on payment of the prescribed fee, issue a driving permit in the form set out in the First Schedule.
18. Application for the grant or renewal of a driving permit may be received and dealt with at any time within one month before the date on which the renewal of the driving permit is to take effect.
19. (1) A person shall not act as a conductor of a motor omnibus unless he is the holder of a permit authorising him for the purpose; and no person shall employ any person who is not so authorised to act as such.
(2) A person who desires to obtain the grant or renewal of a permit as a conductor of a motor omnibus shall apply to the Licensing Authority and shall furnish him with the particulars set out in the form in the First Schedule.
(3) Every applicant for a permit as a conductor of a motor omnibus shall satisfy the Licensing Authority that'-----
(a) he is above the age of 21 years and is physically and mentally fit; .
(b) he is of good character;
(e) he has sufficient knowledge of the rules of the road and of the Motor
Vehicles and Road Traffic Regulations of the Colony.
(4) The Licensing Authority on being satisfied as above shall grant a permit to the applicant on payment of a fee of $1.20.
(5) The permit shall be in such form as may be approved by the Licensing Authority and shall have attached thereto a photograph of the applicant, such photograph being supplied in duplicate by the applicant when applYing for the permit. The permit shall commence on the day on which the same is granted and shall expire on the 31st December next following. . .
. (6) Every conductor shall on being so required by any constable in uniform, produce his permit for examination so as to enable such constable to ascertain. the name and address of the holder of the permit, the date of issue,and the Licensing Officer by whom it was issued, and if he fails so to do, he shall be guilty of an offence under this regulation:
;Provided that, if within forty-eight hours after the production of his permit was so required, the holder produces the permit in person at such Police Station ;as may be specified by him at the time its production was required, he shall not be convicted for such offence.
20. On the grant of a permit to a driver or conductor of a motor omnibus a metal badge upon which there shall be marked the word "driver" or "conductor" -respectively and the year of issue, shall be delivered to such person. Such badge :shall be worn at all times when the driver or conductor is on duty in such a manner as to be easily seen.
21. If a person to whom any permit o~ badge has been granted by the Licensing Authority satisfies the Licensing ,Authority that h,is permit or badge has been lost <>r defaced, the Licensing Authority shall on payment of a fee of 24 cents issue to him a duplicate of such permit or badge.
22. The Licensing Authority may cancel or suspend the permit of any conductor ,on any conviction or contravention of the Ordinance or of these regulations or on being satisfied that he is not a. fit and proper person to hold a conductor's licence.
23. (1) If it appears to the Licensing Officer that there is reason to believe that any person who holds a driver's permit is suffering from a disease or physical disability likely to cause the driving by him of a motor vehicle being a vehicle of any such class or description as he is authorised by the permit to drive, to be a source of danger to the public, and on enquiring into the matter, the Licensing Officer.is satisfied that the holder of the permit is suffering from such a disease or disability as aforesaid, then whether 'or not the holder of the permit so suffering as aforesaid has previously passed a driving test, the Licensing Officer may, after giving to the holder of the permit notice of his intention so to do, revoke the permit, and the. holder shall on receipt of such notice deliver the permit to the Licensing Officer for cancellation. . .
(2) If any person is aggri«::ved by the revocation of a driving permit under this regulation he may, atter giving notice to the Licensing Officer of hit? intention so to do, appeal to the Licensing Authority, and on any such appeal the Licensing Authority may confirm or revoke the decision of the Licensing Officer.
(3) If it appears to the Licensing Authority that a driving permit has been <>btained through misrepresentation or has been issued to a person under a wrong name or under an assumed name by which he is not generally known, the Licensing Authority may, after giving to the holder of the permit notice of his intention so to do, revoke the permit, and the holder shall on receipt of such notice deliver' the permit to the Licensing Authority for cancellation.
PART V.
INSPECTION OF MOTOR VEHICLES.
24. (1) Every applicant for the grant or renewal of a licence in respect of a public service vehicle, rented car, goods vehicle or trailer shall produce such vehicle to such Inspector as the Licensing Authority may direct for examination and shall, at the same time, furnish him with such information as may be required-including the following:-
(a) 'The name of the manufacturer of the chassis.
(b) Model.
(c) Engine number and number of cylinders.
(d) Year manufactured.
(e) Factory number of chassis.
(1) Maximum gross weight in lbs. avoirdupois.
(g) Type of body.
Provided that if the applicant is unable to furnish, the jnformation required the Inspector may describe such vehicle from his own observation: . Provided further that the production 9f an agricultural tractor or agricultural trailer shall be in the discretion of the Licensing Authority. . " (2) The Inspector shall examine such public service vehicle, rented car, goods vehicle or trailer, and if satisfied that it complies with the provisions of the Ordinance and these regulations and on payment of the prescribed fee, shall issue a certificate containing such particulars specified in the form in the Schedule, and no licence for such vehicle shall be issued or renewed unless the Inspector certifies that the vehicle complies with all the provisions of the Ordinance and of these regulations.
(3) The Inspector shall keep a register in the Form in the First Schedule of all certificates granted by him in respect of public service vehicles, goods vehicles or trailers.
(4) Every Licensing Officer shall have power to call up for examination bv an Inspector at any time any motor vehicle or trailer which he has reason to believ-e is not in a fit and Froper state of repair or does not conform to the provisions of the Ordinance and of the Regulations as regards construction, equipment and maintenance.
