(5) The Licensing Authority may at any time by notice in writing require the registered owner of a motor vehicle or trailer to produce the same for his inspection, and such owner shall produce the same accordingly.
(6) Owners intending to renew their ·licences in respect of public service vehicles, rented cars, goods vehicles or trailers, shall produce such vehicles to an Inspector for examination during such periods and at such times and places as shall be appointed by the Licensing AuthOrity by notice published in the Royal Gazette and in a daily newspaper circulating in the Colony. .
(7) If by the 31st of December the Inspector has not been able' to examine any vehicle produced to him for examination, he shall extend the last certificate for such further period as he may consider necessary in order to enable an examination to be carried out, and to enable a renewal of the licence to be granted: Provided that the renewal licence so issued shall be subject to modification in order to conform to the result of the su bsequentexamination of the vehicle: Provided further that no public service vehicle, or goods vehicle, or trailer shall be used on the road unless. the owner thereof has obtained a new certificate or an extension of his last certificate.
(8) If, after the granting of an extension of the certificate, the Inspector finds it necessary on ~ubsequent examination to condemn the vehicle and to prohibit its use on the road, the Licensing Authority may in his discretion authorise a rebate of such proportion of the licence duty paid in respect of such vehicle as he thinks fit.
(9) The Licensing Authority may issue renewals of licences and of driving permits between the 15th and 31st days of December, to become effective on the following 1st day of J annary.
(10) If the applicant for the. grant or renewal of a.licence under this regulation fails to have his vehicle in proper order to the satisfaction of the Inspector.and is required to produce his vehicle for inspection on more than two occasions, then for the third and any subsequent inspection it shall be lawful for the Inspector to charge a further fee of one dollar and twenty cellts for each such inspection.
25. No person shail cause or permit a motor vehicle to be· used on aIly~.]lblic road, or shall drive or have charge of a motor vehicle when so used' unfc::,s the. conditions hereinafter set forth are satisfied, namely;-
(1) The motor vehicle, if it exceeds in weight unladenSOO lbs., shall be
capable of being so worked that it may trayel either forward or backwards.
(2) No HlOtor vehicle or trailer shall exceed ninety-six inches in overall
width in Trinidad ·or ninety inches in overall width in Tobago.
j2A) (i) No motor' vehicle constructed for the carriage of passengers only
shall exceed thirty feet in overall length, except trolley buses operated 'by the Port-of-Spain Corporation which may be of an overall length not exceeding thirty feet six inches. No other motor vehicle shall exceed twenty-eight feet in overall length.
(ii) No trailer other than ~ semi-trailer shall exceed twenty-two feet
in overall length.
(iii) No motor vehicle and semi-trailer attached shall exceed thirty-five
feet in overall length. . .
(iv) No motor vehicle and trailer attached shall exceed forty-five feet
in overall length: Provided that the overall length of a motor vehicle and pole trai1er carrying pipes may extend to fifty feet and of an agricultural tractor drawing two trailers to fifty-five feet.
(3) Except with the permission of the Licensing Authority, no motor vehicle Overhang. or trailer shall have a lateral overhang of more than six inches or a longitudinal overhang which exceeds seven-twenty-fourths (7/24ths) of the overall length of the vehicle, and when a trailer is connected to a motor vehicle bv a bar' or pipe, such bar or pipe shall not be allowed to project more than -three feet beyond the rear of the trailer.
(3A) (i) The total weight transmitted to the road surface by the wheels of Distribution .any one axle of a motor vehicle or trailer shall not exceed eight tons. of :weight.
(ii) The combined gross weights of a motor vehicle and trailers shall not exceed the weight arrived at by the formula " 18,000 lb. + (L X 800) " where L equals the length in feet measured from the centre of the front wheels -to the centre of the rearmost wheels of the vehicle or combination of vehicles.
(iii) For the purposes of this paragraph two or more axles in line transversely 'and two or more axles the distance between the centre lines of -which is less than fifty inches shall be deemed to be a "single axle.
(4) (a) Every mQtor vehicle shall be equipped with an efficient braking Brakes . . system or efficient braking systems in either case having two means of operation .so designed and constructed that one means of operation shall apply brakes to not less than all the wheels on each of two axles (or in the case of a motor .cycle, one axle) of the motor vehicle and that notwithstanding the failure of any part (other than a fixed member or a brake shoe anchor pin) through or by means of which the force necessary to apply the brakes is transmitted, ·there shall still be available for application by the driver to not less than half the number of the wheels of the vehicle (or in the case of a motor-cycle or .a motor vehicle having less than four wheels to not less than one wheel) brakes sufficient under the most adverse conditions to bring the vehicle to rest 'withln .a reasonable distance: .
Provided that a motor vehicle manUfactured prior to the 1st January, 1935, may be fitted with brakes capable of being applied to all the wheels of one .axle but the braking system or systems of such motor vehicle shall have two independent means of operation as prescribed in the preceding paragraph.
(b) All the brakes of a motor vehicle which are operated by one of the means of operation shall be capable of being applied by direct mechanical .action without ·the intervention of any hydraulic, electric,. pneumatic or ·any ·other device. .
(c) One at least of the means of operation sha:n 'be capable of causing -the brakes to be applied directly and not through the transmission gear to not less than half the number of the wheels of the veJ:ticle.
(4) In the case of a steam driven vehicle which is not u'sed as a public :service vehicle for the conveyance 6f pa.~sengers for. gain' or hire, .the engine ·of the vehicle shall be deemed to be an efficic;lnt braking system if the engine is capable of being reversed and is incapable of being disconnected from any ,of the driving wheels except by the sustained effort of the driver.' . - ,.
(e) Every part of every braking system and of the means of operation ·thereof fitted to a motor vehicle or trailer, ,shall at all times, while the motor vehicle, or trailer is used on a road, be maintained in good and efficient working ·order and shall be properly adjusted. .
(5) Every trailer other than an agricultural trailer shall have an efficient Trailers. braking system, approved by the Licensing Authority, the brakes of which· ;are capable, when a trailer is being dra.wn, or being applied to at least two wheels: Provided that this paragraph shall not apply in the case of a pole trailer unladen or carrying an indivisible load.
(6) Every goods vehicle, motor omnibus or trailer shall have its tare and. Weight M.G.W. painted in white figures and letters not less than two and a half inches shmVuin 'in height upon a black ground on a conspicuous place on the right or off side certain eases. ·of. such vehicle to be specified by the Licensing Officer. Such weights shall ,be expressed in pounds avoirdupois.
(7) The motor vehicle or trailer and all the fittings thereof shall be in such.. a condition as not to cause, or to be likely to cause, danger to any person on the motor vehicle or trailer or on any public road.
(8) All motor vehicles shall be fitted with silencers to the satisfaction of the' Licensing Authority. The use of cut-outs is forbidden throughout the Colony_
(9) There shall not be affixed to the front of a motor vehicle any lighted lamp other than the head lights and side lights except with the approval in writing of the Licensing Authority, nor to the rear of a motor vehicle or trailer any lighted lamp other than the red light prescribed by these Regulations, except as permitted by paragraph (11) (e) of this Regulation.
(10) There shall not be painted or otherwise affixed to the front or rear of a motor vehicle any combination of letters or figures resembling, or likely to, obscure, or likely to be confused with the letters and figures on the identification place prescribed by these Regulations, and no letters or figures other -than those on the identification plate shall be painted or affixed within eighteen inches, of the identification plate.
(11) (a) Every motor vehicle shall be equipped with two lamps at the front, of the vehicle, one on each side. ,\Vhen a motor vehicle is in motion on a road at night, the two lamps at the front of the vehicle shall be lighted, and the rays-from the lamps shall be uncoloured, and if the vehicle is capable of proceeding at a speed greater than 20 miles an hour, of such intensity as to illuminate the road ahead' for a distance of at least 110 yards or such greater distance-as is sufficient to ensure the safety of the vehicle and the persons carried on, the vehicle, and to indicate clearly the presence of the vehicle to approaching traffic: Provided that a motor cycle shall show one such light as aforesaid" but if a side-car is attached to the motor cycle, there shall be shown on that, side of the side-car not adjacent to the motor cycle, an additional light of sufficient intensity to indicate the presence of the side-car from a reasonable-distance to approaching traffic.
(b) Save as is contained in regulation 73, when stationary on a road at night every motor vehicle and every trailer not attached to a motor vehicle' shall show two lights in front, one at each side, of sufficient intensity to indicate-the presence of the motor· vehicle or trailer from a reasonable distance to: approaching traffic: Provided that a motor cycle not attached to a side-car shall show one such light as aforesaid.
(c) ~ave as is contained in regulation 73, every motor vehicle arid. trailer, when on a road at' night, -whether in motion or stationary shall carry one lamp showing a red light to the rear, of such intensity as to indicate clearly within a reasonable distance its presence on the road to traffic approaching from behind, and the lamp shall be of such construction and shall' show ,an uncoloured lightof such intensity as to .illuminate clearly the figures and numbers on the rear identification plate. The lamp shall be so fixed that it is either on the centre-line or oft-side of the vehicle, and that, no part of the' vehicle projects beyond such lamp so as to obstruct ,the light from ,view of following vehicles: Provided that it shall be optional for a motor vehicle, or, trailer to carry a second red light fixed on the near side to the rear whenever the rear light is fixed on the .oft-side; and, except as aforesaid, no, other ,light shall be carried to the rear of any motor vehicle or trailer without the special written permission 'of the Licensing Authority: Provided further--that where-, a trailer is attached to a tractor it shall be sufficient if one such lamp as aforesaid is carried at the rear of the trailer only. '
(d) No spot lights or oth~r similar swivelling lights shall be carried on any motor vehicle without the authority of the Licensing Authority who may' authorise such lights at his discretion; and subject to such conditions as he may impose. .
(e) In addition to tlie f~-regoing provisions as to lights to be. carried,. motor omnibuses shall also carry a lighted lamp 011 the inside top of the hood showing a white light visible within a reasonable distance in all directions.
(1) In the case of motor omnibuses and goods vehicles, when either' of the lamps referred to in paragraph (a) hereof is placed in such a position that the distance of the centre of the lamp from the extreme outside point' of the vehicle on its appropriate side exceeds twelve inches, two additional
lamps shall be fitted at the front of the said vehicle one on each side so that its centre shall not exceed the aforesaid distance of twelve inches, and so as clearly to indicate the width of the vehicle to approaching traffic; the said lamps shall be focussed below the horizontal and shall exhibit a light of sufficient power.only to indicate the width of the vehicle. .
(12) The exhaust pipe of a motor vehicle shall not be fixed in such a position
that oil or any vaporisable or inflammable material is likely to be dropped upon it; the outlet thereof shall be placed far enough to the rear of the vehicle to prevent, as far as practicable, fumes from entering the vehicle.
25A. Notwithstanding the provisions of sub-paragraphs (b) and (e) of paragraph (11) of the last preceding Regulation, a motor vehicle when stationary at night within the boundaries of the City of Port-of-Spain, or the Borough of San Fernando, or the Borough of Arima, need not show two lights in front and one light at the back if all the following conditions are complied with;-
(a) If such vehicle is drawn up as close as possible to its left or near side
of the road,
(b) if there is painted on the rear of the vehicle and on the right or off-side
thereof (on or as near as possible to the rear off-side mudguard) a ·white rectangle or circle at least fifty square inches in area so as to face, and be visible to, traffic approaching from the rear.
26. (1) Every direction indicq,tor must be in the fo= of an illuminated sign of Direction amber colour diffused by means of frosted glass or other means, not less than six indicators .. inches illuminated length and in breadth no more than one-fourth of the illuminated length and must show both to the front and rear. Every indicator must be so designed and fitted that-
(a) it is not more than four feet behind the base of the windscreen;
(b) it is not more than six feet six inches above ground level;
(e) when in operation it alters the outline of the vehicle at least six inches,
measured horizontally, and the olitermost point of the indicator shall be at least six inches further from the longitudinal axis of the vehicle than is the outer-most point of the side of the body immediately behind the driver's seat, and it must remain steady whilst being used as a signal;
This paragraph shall not apply to pillarless saloon cars,provided the indicator
is not situated behind the widest part of the body;
(d) the one to indicate a right-hand turn shall be fitted on the off-side and· .
the one to indicate a left-hand turn shall be fitted on the near side of the vehicle;
(e) the driver when in the driving seat must be readily aware that. the
indicator is operating correctly. . .
(2) Additional direction indicators may be fitted at the rear of the vehicle provided they are only used coincidentally with those fitted at the. sides.
26A. (1) A motor omnibus or goods vehicle with left hand steering shall be fitted Left-hand with a mechanical or electrical signalling device approved by the Licensing Authority. driven
(2) All motor vehicles with left hand steering shall have a notice, affixed· vehicles. in a conspicuous position on the rear of the vehicle, bearing the words, in letters of not less than two inches in height, " Left Hand Drive."
PART VI.
USE OF MOTOR VEHICLES. Rules to be
observed by
27. Every driver of a motor vehicle shall comply with the following rules;- drivers .
. (1) He sha!l not cause the motor .v:ehicle to travel backwards f?r a greater. Travelling dlstaI).Ce or tIme than may be reqmslte for the s'afety or convemence of the . backwards occupants of the motor vehicle and of the passengers and other traffic on the road. ..
In the City of Port-of-Spain or the Borough of San Fernando he shall not
cause the motor vehicle to travel baCkwards at the junction of any roads or round a corner.
(2) He shall not, when on the motor vehicle, be in such a position that he cannot have full control over the same, or that he cannot obtain a full view of the road and traffic ahead of the motor vehicle.
(3) He shall before quitting the motor vehicle stop the engine and apply the brakes.
(4) In the case of goods vehicles and public service vehicles, not more than one person shall sit by the side of the driver unless such vehicle has been certified by an Inspector to have been constructed for the purpose of accommodating more than one person beside the driver.
:;-\[0 person shall sit on the right hand side of the driver of any motor vehicle unless such motor vehicle has been constructed with a left hand drive.
(5) (a) He shall keep the motor vehicle on the left of the road unless pre-vented by some sufficient cause.
(bf When meeting other vehicles he shall keep as close as possible to the left or near side of the road.
(e) When overtaking other vehicles he shall keep to the right or off side of such other vehicles. .
(d) When being overtaken byother vehicles he shall drive as close to the side of the road as possible so as to allow such other vehicles to pass, and in all cases he shall drive so as to give as much space as possible for the passing of other traffic and shall not speed abreast any vehicle when such vehicle is over-taking him. .
(e) 'He shall not drive so as to overtake other traffic unless he has a clear and unobstructed view of the road ahead and he shall not overtake such other traffic unless he sees that the road ahead is cle,ar for a sufficient distance to enable him to overtake and get back,to his 'proper side before meeting any traffic coming from the opposite direction. ' • .
(f) He shall not overtake other traffic when rounding a corner or when roads intersect or fork or where the road passes over the brow of a hill or over a humpbacked bridge or in any other circumstances where the driver cannot see sufficiently far ahead to overtake with safety. .
(g) He shall not cross a road or turn in a road or proceed from one Toad into another road, or drive from a place which is not a road into a road, or from a road into a place which is not a road unless he can do so without obstructing any other traffic on the road, and for this purpose he shall be held to be cb-structing other traffic if he causes risk of accident thereto.
(h) When turning from one road into another road to the left be s11all keep as close as possible to the left side of each road.
(i) When turning from one road into another road to the right he sha11 drive round the point of intersection of the two roads, and shall keep as close as possible to the left side of each road.
(j) He shall not park his motor vehicle within a distance of thirty feet from any corner or road intersection or nearer thereto than the distance in-dicated by the appropriate road signal, if any.
(Il) He shall not draw up the motor vehicle alongside any other motor vehicle nor shall he back the motor vehicle to the kerb stone of any foot-path or to the side of any road .except for the purpose of turning.
(I) He shall not draw up his motor vehicle on any road or leave his motol;' vehicle standing on any road, within ten feet of a fire hydrant;
(m) He shall Dot draw up his motor vehicle or permit it to remain on either side of any road, within ten feet of the frontage of any Fire Brigade Station.
(6) He shall not negligently or wil'fully prevent, hinder or interrupt the free passage of any vehicle, person or animal, and shall not allow such motor vehicle and any trailer drawn thereby to stand in such road so as to cause any un-necessarv obstruction thereto.
(7) (a) He shall, 'when approaching turnings and cross roads or coming from allY pri-,7ate road or place to any public road, slow down and make the appropriate traffic signaL
(0) .He shall, -,~Thell turning across traffic or turning to the right, sIc-", down
and make the appropriate traffic signal.
(e) He shall, when about to stop or slow down the vehicle on any public road, do so gradua.lly alld mccke the appropriate traffi-c signal.
(d) He shall, when quitting any place at which his vehicle has been
statiOllary, make the appropriate signal.
(e) He shall comply with the directions of all road signals 11nless prevented
by some sufiicient cause.
(8) He shall, when using a motor vehicle on any road, make. the following traffic signals;-
(a) \iVhen about to slow down or stop, he shall extend the right ann and
hand horizontally with the palm of the hand turned downwards and shall move the ann slc-wly and repeatedly downwards and upvrards keeping it bela-w the level of the shoulder.
(0) V\'hen about to turn across traffic or to turn to the right he shall
extend the right hand and ann horizontally, palm turned to the front or if the motor vehicle is fitted with direction' indicators the driver shall signal -with the indicators fitted on the off-side of the vehicle.
(e) vVhen acknowledging a signal indicating that the driver qf a vehicle
proceeding in the same direction wishes to pass he shall extend the right arm and hand below the level of the shoulder and nlove them backwards and forwards.
(d) ',,"1hen about to turn to the left he shall extend the right arm and
. rotate it from the shoulder in an anti-clockwise direction, or if the motor vehicle is fitted with direction indicators the driver shall signal with the· indicator on the near. side (li the vehicle.
Provided that in no case shall the required traffic signal be given by the driver cf a lTlOtor omnibus by means of a dummy arm unless the construction of the motor omnibus and the position of the driver's seat are suchas to render it impossible for the driver to make such signal with his arm;
Provided further that, in the case of a motor vehicle with left hand steering, he shall, when using the vehicle on anyroad, make the following traffic signals;-
(a) 'IiVhen about to slow down or stop, he shall extend the left arm and
hand horizontally with the palm of the hand turned downwards and shall move the arm slowly and repeatedly downwards and upwards keeping it . below the level of the shoulder. .
(b) When about to turn across traffic or to turn to the right, he shall
extend the left arm and rotate it from the shoulder in a clockwise direction or,if the motor vehicle is fitted with direction indicators, the driver shall signal with the indicator fitted to the offside of the vehicle.
(e) \iVhen about to turn to the left, he shall extend the left hand and
ann horizontally, palm turned to the front, or if the motor vehicle is fitted with direction indicators, the driver shall signal with the indicator on the near side of the vehicle.
(9) He shall not, when meeting or overtaking an electric tram car which is 'stationary for the purpose of taking on or discharging passengers, pass the tram car until the passengers have got on the car or have got safely to the side of the street, as the case may be. He shall pass tram cars when in motion T.-·-VIII. 37 with due care on whichever side is most consistent with the safety of foot passengers or other traffic.
(10) He shall conform to the rules of the road in regard to all kinds of traffic and shall comply with the lawful directions of any constable char6cd with the regulation of traffic. He shall give the following signals for the purpose of indicating the direction in which he wishes to proceed:
(a) "When it is proposed to go straight ahead, raise the hand towards the
shoulder, and move the forearm 'well forwards and then back in a vertical plane, making the movement suffIciently pronounced to be easily seen by the constable.
(b) '.'\Then about to tum to the left he shall extend the right arm and
rotate it from the shoulder in an anti-clockwise direction.
(e) 'I,'\Then it is proposed to turn to the right, extend the right arm and
hand 'with the palm turned to the front, and hold them rigid in a horizontal
straight out from the off-side of the vehicle. (This signal may be
used in any circumstances when it may be necessary to convey the warning to traffic following behind that it is dangerous to overtake.)
Provided that, in the case of a motor vehicle with left hand steering which is not fitted with direction indicators, he shall:-
(a) when about to turn to the left, extend the left arm and hand with the
palm turned to the front and hold them rigid in a horizontal position straight out from the near side of the vehicle;
(b) when it is proposed to turn to the right, extend the left arm and rotate
it from the shoulder in a clockwise direction.
(11) Slow moving traffic shall keep as much to the left as possible at all times, and unladen vehicles shall give way to laden vehicles.
(12) He shall, when about to stop the motor vehicle on any road, dra,v up as close as possible to the side of the road so as to allow a clear roadway for passing traffic; and shall remove the motor vehicle at the request of any constable when it is placed in such a position as to cause or be likely to cause obstruction on any road. He shall immediately remove it when requested so to do by the occupier or the servant of the occupier of any premises the access to which is obstructed thereby. He shall not draw up the motor vehicle on a road in such a manner as to prevent or hinder the drivers of other vehicles complying with the direc-tions of any road signal. He shall not leave the motor vehicle drawn up on any public road for an undue length of time having regard to all the circum-stances of the case, including the amount of traffic which is at the time, or which might reasonably be expected to be, on the road. ..
(13) (a) He shall carry attached to the motor vehicle a horn or other instru-ment capable of giving audible and sufficient warning of its approach or position and he shall not himself sound or allow any other person to sound s.uch horn or other instrument except for the purpose of giving reasonable ,8,net necessary warning of the approach or position of the motor vehicle.
(b) He shall not himself sound or allow any other person to sound such hom or other instrument between the hours of 9 p.m. and 5.30 a.m. in any Municipal area, and also in any other area in the Colony in respect of which the Licensing Authority shall issue a prohibition notice to that effect in the Royal Gazette.
(c) The Licensing Authority shall haye power at any time to prohibit the use of any horn or instrument, the use of which for the purpose authorised in this regulation is likely to cause annoyance to the genelal public.
(d) Every horn or other instrument used for the purposes authorised shall either be of a kind which has been approved by the Licensing Authority generally as proper to be used' on u10tor vehicles or which has been approved by him in a special case on the application of the owner of the motor vehicle.
(e) Any horn or ,other instrument which has been approved by the Licensing Authority for use on motor vehicles shall not be used on any other ele S3 of vehicles.
(14) He shall not permit sparks, smoke or visible "apour of any avoidable
nature which would cau5'e annoyance or danger to the public to come from any motor vehicle in his charge on or near any public road and shall take proper precautions to prevent the 1.1I1neCessary discharge of lubricating oil on any road.
(15) He shall, on the request of any constable in uniform, or of any person
having charge of a horse or any other animal, or if any such constable or person shall put up his hand as a signal for that purpose, cause the ni-otoT vehicle to stop and to remain stationary so long as may be reasonably necessary.
(16) He shall not carryon the motor vehicle or trailer, or being the owner of
. any motor vehicle or trailer shall not suffer or permit to be carried thereon, any tree, timber or iron beam or any lumber or other matter or thing projecting on either side of such motor vehicle or trailer more than one foot beyond the plane Df the wheels of such motor vehicle or trailer, or projecting more than five feet in front or behind, or in such a manner as to obstruct, endanger, or interfere with traffic, nor any load in such manner that the width of such load together "with' the overall width of the vehicle shall exceed ninety-~ix inches in Trinidad and ninety inches in Tobago. Officers of Police in charge of divisions, may grant permits for the transport over any road, within stated hours, by a stated route .and under stated conditions, of articles the transport of which would otherwise infringe the provisions of this regulation. Such permits shall be carried on the motor vehicle and shall be produced by the driver at the request of any member ·of the Police Force. Any motor vehicle operating under the authority of any such permit shall carry by day a white flag and by night a red lamp affixed at the extreme rear end of the load.
(17) He shall not allow the motor vehicle to be overcrowded with persons.
(18) \iVhere permission has been granted by the Licensing Authority for the
<conveyance of any person in Ol' on a goods vehicle, agricultural trailer or trailer, he shall not allow any such p,3rson to stand in or on the vehicle, agricultural trailer or trailer or on the tray or platform thereof whilst the vehicle is in motion, or to be carried in SUGh a positiOIi that danger is caused or is likely to be caused ±o such person or to any other person.
(19) He shall not leave or permit to be" left on any public road a"ny motor
vehicle which has broken c10vvn without taking the l:equisite steps to indicate its presence and position by lighting or otherwise to other persons using the road, . and shall take immediate . to have it l'CIlloved as near to the left of the' road as possible. .
(20) He shall on the approach of any engine or other apparatus of a Fire-
Brigade proceeding to or from a fire, or suspected fire draw up close to the left hand side of the road and stop leaving the centre of the road for the'passage of. :such engine or other apparatus, and he shall not stand abreast of any other vehicle.
(21) He shall not, without the written permission of the Licensing Authority,
tow anything except another motor vehicle in case of necessity, or a single .registered trailer, except that in the case of an agricultural tractor he may tow not more than two trailers.
(22) No motor vehicle shall tow any other vehicle unless the tow rope, chain
or draw bar be so adjusted that the distance separating the nearest two points of the two vehicles shall not, except in the case of a pole trailer, exceed fifteen feet, and the tow rope, chain or draw bar is easily distinguishable by other users of the road.
28. Any person who shall fill petrol into any receptacle on or adjacent to any" motor vehicle or into the tank of a motor vehicle while the engine is running, or ,vhile .any light, other than an electric light, is alight on the vehicle, and any person who shall smoke or light a match or expose a naked light in close proximity while petrol is being so filled shall he guilty of an offence. '
37 (2)
-------- ~~-- - -------
29. Every motor vehicle, other than a motor cycle, shall be equipped with a re-flecting mirror, so constructed and fitted to the motor vehicle as to enable the driver of such IDotor vehicle to be or become a"vare of the prc:sence in the rear thereof of any other vehicle, the driver of ",11ich is desirous of passing such motor vehicle: Provided that this requirement shall not apply to a illotor vehicle when dra;wing a hailer if a person is carried on the trailer in a position which affords an uninterrupted view to the re:lT and such person is provided ",~ith efficient means of communicating to the driver the effect of signals given by the drivers of other vehicles in rear thereof.
30. No~part of the fittings, accessories, or other equipment, of any motor vehicle shall be so arranged or fixed as to be capable of being displaced by the motion of travel in a manner which causes the permitted dimensions ,of such vehicle to be exceeded.
31. In the case of goods vehicles the load shall be properly secured in such a manllC] as effectively to prevent the displacement of any portion thereof by the motion of travel. Any tarpaulin or other covering used for the protection of the load shall be' a.djusted so as to :fit closely to the load or vehicle and shall be properly secured by ropes or othe:r fastenings so as to prevent flapping or other displacement of such covering while the vehicle is in motion. "
32. Any person driving or in charge of a motor vehicle which causes a loud noise-to the annoyance or disturbance of any person, shall be guilty of an offence under these l'egulations.
33. (1) All tyres of a motor vehicle or trailer shall at all times be maintained in such condition as to be free from any defect which might in any way cause damage to the surface of the road, or danger to persons in or on the vehicle or to any per,sons using the road: Provided that a person shall not be convicted under this regulation if he proves to the satisfaction of the Court that he could not by reasonable diligence 11a ve becorne a ware of the defect.
(2) Any constable may require the driver' of a motor vehicle, any of the tyres of which appears to the constable to be defective, to proceed to the nearest Police Station for examination and if the driver refuses to do so the motor vehicle may, at the expense and risk of the owner, be detained and taken to the said station or to a place for safe keeping until such request is complied with.
34. The glass windshield of any motor vehicle imported into the Colony after the" 31st day of December, 1937, shall be made of safety glass.
35. An effective wiper shall bc provided on the glass windshield of a motor vehicle. 36. No person, other than the driver, whilst travelling in a motor vehicle, 'shaH' 11'1ake any traffic signal.
37. \Nhencver any constable on point duty gives a traffic control signal to enable 3,ny vehicle to proceed in an indicated direction, no cyclist or pedestrian shall proceed across the current of traffic consequent upon such signal, or loiter in the roadway,' or otherwise impede any vehicle from proceeding in the direction indicated,
38. No musical instrument shall be played, no noisy instrument shall be playuI or operated ih any motor vehicle, whilst in motion, except on the written permission of the Licensing Authority; and any person who'plays or operates any such instru-o rnent in contravention of this regulation and the driver of the vehicle who permits such instrument to be played or operatcd shall each be guilty of an offence against this regulation.
PART VII,
PUBLIC SERVICE VEHICLES-CONSTRUCTION ANDIVIAINTEN_<\'NCE.
Motor Omnibuses.
"39. Revoked by G.N. 156-1950, 40. Revoked by G.N. 156-1950. 41. All bodics of motor omnibuses ~ s11all be of first-class workmanship and be securely bolted and braced. "\Vhere it is necessary to extcnd the chassis frame the "extension shall be inspected by an Inspector before the body is iinally bo!ted and. braced to the frame.
42. In the case of motor omnihuses, all curtains, or screens, or other devices for ·the protection of passengers from the weather shall be properly secured by straps or other fastenings so as to prevent flapping or other clisplacement of such curtains, ·or screens, or other devices while the vehicle is in motion.
43. Motor omnibuses shall be so constructed that the height of the main floor measured at a certain point shall not exceed 2 feet 6 inches measured vertically from the surface of the road when the vehicle is fully loaded: Provided that the . Inspecting Engineer shall be empowered to pass vehicles as motor omnibuses with 2 greater height of flooring (but not exceecling the floor height passed by the Ministry .of Transport) if he is satisfied as to the stability and safety of the vehicle.
Every motor omnibus shall be fitted with-
(a) a device by which a passenger may signa.l to the driver or conductor
when required;
(b) a speedometer;
(c) a fire extinguisher.
All such fittings shall be subject to the approval of the Inspector, and shall be
kept in ,vorking order.
44. (1) Seats.-The -width between the backs of seats when the seats are placed 'lengthwise shall benotless than fifty-six inches, \\Then the seats are placed behind each other, the space between the seats shall be unobstructed, and the distance between the backs of two seats so placed shall be not less than twenty-seven inches, :and when seats are placed crosswise facing each other the unimpeded space b~tween them shall be not less than twenty-six inches and the depth of each seat shall be not less than fourteen inches. At least fifteen inches of unimpeded space shall be .-allowed fat each passenger measured along each seat. The supports of all seats :shall be firmly fixed in position.
(2) Corridoys.-Every motor omnibus registered after the 31st of December, 1935, with seating accommodation for more than 10 passengers, shall have an internal corridor of not less than fifteen inches in width, ·so arranged as to admit ·of easy access from within the vehicle to all seats; and no person shall stand on or move along the footboardoutsii:1:e any such vehicle whilst it is in motion.
(3) Emergency Exit.-Every motor o~nibus> the sides of which are enclosed, shall be fitted at the rear of the vehicle with an emergency door opening outwards, -or other sufficient exit which shall be rt3adily accessible from all seats; such door ,or exit shall be k:ept secured against use .. by any passenger, e-?,cept in the case of 2.n emergency.
45. No stt3PS, footboard'or running-board shall be allowed on the right or offside' '.,of any motor omni.bus. Horizontal metal rails spaced not more than nine inches .apart shall run from end to end and from floor level to two feet above the level of ·the seats. Such rails shall not be moveable.
46. 'Where ru,nning boards, steps or footboards are fitted to the left or near side ·of the omnibus these must be :fitted with a toeguard at least four inches above the running board, step or foot board and securely fastened to same. The length ·ofthis toeguard not to be less than six inches longer than the outside diameter of the rear tyre aDd to be fitted equidistant from the centre of the rear wheel.
47. The driver's seat shall not be less than two feet wide, and shall be so ,constructed that there is a space of at least one foot on each side of the steering "column, the space between the rear lower part of the steering wheel and the top ,-of the driver's seat (inclusive of any cushion) shall not be less than seven inches. This seat shall slope backwards 'with a rake of one and one half inches per foot measured horizontally, th~ distance between the back pari; of the steering wheel :and the back of the driver's seat shall not be less than thirteen inches. The driver's seat shall be divided off from that of the J?erson sitting beside him on his left by a wooden partition not less than thirty inches high measured from the floor, and ,extending Jrom the front of the front seat to the dashboard. In any case this partition is to have a clearance of not less than t,yO inches to the left of the extreme' left lever controlled by the driver.
48. A iixed seat shall be provided for the conductor in every motor omnibus· which shall not in any 'Iyay interfere with the driver or passenger seating accommoda-· tion.
49. Every motor omnibus "with seating accommodation for more than 10 passengers when in use on any road shall at all times carry a conductor in addition to a driver ..
The owner and driver shall both be liable if the motor omnibus is used in contra-vention of this regulation.
50. vVhere passengers are carried on the left or near side of the driver's side provision shall be made for a suitable guard, barrier or rail to be fitted, so that in the' event of the omnibus stopping suddenly, the passenger or passengers cannot be thrown forward and out of the omnibus.
51. The internal height measured in the centre line of a motor omnibus fro111 floor to lower edge of hoop sticks or other projections on the ceiling shall 110t be less than iive feet six inches.
52. Every driver of a motor omnibus on a public road shall at all times-
(a.) wear his badge conspicuously and in such a manner that the whole of the
writing thereon is distinctly legible;
(b) be suitably attired and wear boots Dr shoes and a distinguishing cap ancl
uniform to consist of a forage cap and a khaki or blue serge suit,
53. Every conductor of a motor omnibus on a public road shall comply with the" following regulations;-
(a) He shall not smoke after objection made by any passenger.
(b) He shall not make use of any abusive language or insulting gestures.
(e) He shall not call out to or otherwise, 'importune any person' to travell
by any such 'omnibus.
(d) He shall not ride .otherwise than inside such omnibus except when collecting-
a fare. .." ~
(e) He shall not permit more passengers tha,n such omnibus is licensed for
to be carried in such omnibus, Ii there are passengers in excess, t1:e conductor, or the driver when there is no conductor, shall at. the request of any constable .. in uniform eject the passengers being carried in excess, and on his refusal S'o: to do the constable may arrest the conductor, or the driver, as the case may be, and take such steps as niay be nece.ssaryto prevent the vehicle from proceeding on its journey with such excess passengers. Three children, if they are each under the age of iifteen years, shall be counted as two passengers .for the purposes of' this paragraph. ' . . . (1) He shall not permit luggage other than personal. luggage to be carried; on such omnibus. "Personal Luggage" means wearing apparel and such. other personal requisites as are usually carried by a traveller for his comfort. and convenience, and includes the receptacle in which such articles are being conveyed.
(g) He shall not be under the influence of drink or drugs during his employment ...
(h) He shall at all times wear his badge conspicuously in such a manner
that the whole of the writing thereon is distinctly legible.
(il He shall at all times. be suitably attired. and' wear b00ts or shoes and
a distinguishing cap and uniform, to 'consist of a forage cap and a· khaki or blue serge suit.
54. Every person driving a motor omnibus on a road shall after quitting any stand or garage with passengers proceed direct to the place or places included in the·" route of such omnibus, and shall not delay in so doing, and shall not return to the place of departure after leaving it, except .on the return journey.
55. The driver or conductor of a motor omnibus on any road shall not permit passengers to enter or leave such omnibus otherwise than by the rear or by the left or near side thereof.
56. All acts and things required by this Part of these regulations to be done by the Licensing Authority may be done by any person or persons to whom he may from time to time delegate all or any of his powers.
57. The Licensing Authority in his discretion need not enforce any or all of these regulations in respect of motor omnibuses that 'were registered as such prior to the 1st January, 1935.
58. The Licensing Authority in his discretion may exempt freight passenger vehicles from any of the provisions of these regulations as relate to motor omnibuses having regard to the number of passengers and amount of load to be carried by such vehicles and to the safety and convenience of the public.
PUBLIC SERVICE VEHICLES.
59. The driver of a public service vehicle. shall comply with the following regulations :-
(1) He shall not use a horn or other instiument for the purpose of calling
passengers.
(2) He shall not smoke after objection made by any passenger.
(3) He shall not make use to any passenger of any abusive language or
insulting gestures.
(4) He shall not call out to or otherwise importune any person to travel
by such public service vehicle.
(5) He shall not loiter for the purpose of procuring passengers or otherwise.
(6) He shall not, while waiting to pick up passengers, race his engine or
make any loud or unnecessary noise with his engine.
(7) He shall at the request of any passenger immediately stop in order
. to enable such passenger to alight; Provided that when he is driving a motor omnibus in any area where stopping places are designated he shall not stop the motor omnibus to pick up or set dOWll passengers elsewhere than at a stopping place marked as such by signboard or other device, nor shall he stop at any such stopping place unless passengers are waiting to be picked up or desire to alight: Provided further than when stopping to pick up or set down passengers . he shall not cause any .obstruction to the ordinary traffic on the road.
(8) He shall not in the City of Port-af-Spain, the Boroughs of San Fernando
and Arima and in Princes Town draw up his omnibus on a road other than at a public stand appointed under these Regulations, except for the purpose of taking on or discharging passengers.
(9) He shall maintain the public service vehicle in a clean and sanitary
condition.
(10) He shall carry at least one spare inflated tyre fixed to the rim, spare
wheel or other device, capable of being fitted to a wheel or axle.
60. The driver of a public service vehicle and the conductor of a motor omnibus wherein any property is left shall within twenty-four hours deposit such property, if not sooner claimed, at the poli~e station nearest to his residence.
61. No owner, driver, or conductor of a public service vehicle plying for hire or any other person shall speak, make any noise, or sound any instrument in order to attract the attention of the public or of a possible passenger; or by troublesome and frequent demands or by persistent following hold out the vehicle for :bire to the public in such a manner as to constitute a nuisan-.;e, or act in any way so as to cause annoyance or inconvenience to any person. .
61A. Every driver of a taxi on a public road shall be suitably attired. 61B. No owner or driver of a taxi shall place or suffer to be placed any printed written or other matter by way of advertisement on any side window of the taxi.
62. (1) The driver of a taxi shall not without reasonablE: excuse-
(a) refuse to be hired when th~ taxi is stand~ng on a public stand; or
(b) refuse or neglect to drive the taxi to any accessible place indicated by the
hirer; Provided that the whole journey, out and return does not exceed 20 miles; or
(c) refuse or neglect to carry such number of passengers, not exceeq.lng the
number specified in the motor vehicle licence, as he may be required to carry by the hirer; or
(d) refuse or neglect to accept with a hirer, a reasonable amount of luggage.
(2) Where a· taxi is at the head of a rank at a public stand, the driver thereof shall, except for good and substantial cause, the proof whereof shall lie on him, remain standing by, or seated in, the taxi .
. 63. The following fares shall be payable in respect of the hire of a taxi whether standing at a.public stand or not:-
Fares by Distance:-
(1) From 6 a.m. to 9 p.m., for the first mile or part th.ereof, in Trinidad
36 cents for one or two persons, in Tobago 48 cents for one or two persons; for each additional half mile or part thereof, 18 cents; for each additional person above two, 18 cents for the whole distance.
(2) From 9 p.m. to 6 a.m., the above charges may be increased by 50 per
cent.
Fares by Time:-
(1) From 6 a.m. to 9 p.m. for a taxi licensed to carry not more than four
persons and hired for a journey within the City of Port-of-Spain as defined in the First Schedule to the Port of Spain Corporation Ordinance, or the Borough of San Fernando as defined in the First Schedule to the San Fernando Corpora-" tion Ordinance, or Princes Town as defined in the Fourth Schedule to the Public Health Ordinance and within two miles of any of 'the respective boundaries thereof measured in the directiori of havel from any such boundary, or within the Town of Scarborough in the Island-ward of Tobago as defined in the Fourth Sehedule to the Public Health Ordinallce and ·wi.thin one mile of any of the boundaries thereof measured in the direction of travel hom any such boundary, at the rate of $3.00 per hour;' .
(2) From 6 a.m. to 9 p.m. ror a taxi licensedio carry more than four passengers
and hired for a journey within the Ci:ty of Port-of-Spain as in this regulation. defined, or the Borough of San Fernando as in this regulationdefihed., or. Princes Town as in this regulation dt:fined and ,vithin two miles of any of the· respective boundaries thereof measured in the direction of travel from any si;rch boundary, or within the Tow.q. of Scarborough in thelsland-ward of Tobago as' in this regulation defined within one mile of any of the boundaries thereof J;D.easured in the direction of travel from any such boundary, at the rate of $3.60 per hour;
(3) From 9 p.m. to 6 a.m. the above charges may be increased by 50 percent.:
Provided that the minimum period of hiring ~y time shall be·.a quarter of an ~ur. c
64. (1) Every owner of a taxi sh~ll cause a stat~mellt of the fares prescribed for his district. to be printsd or painted. on a suitable plate in legible letters and figures of such a colour as to be clearly distinguishable froin the colour of the ground whereO!l such letters and figures are printed or painted. He shall cause sucilplate to be :l'ixed on the inside at the back of the front seat of such taxi or otherwise in such a position and manner that the letters and figures on such plate shall be at all times distinctly and plainly visible and legible to anyperso"n being conveyed iI;! such taxi. .
(2) Such owner shall, from time to time, as often as occasion may require, renew such plate or the letters and figures of such plate, and he shall not, nor shall any driver of suh taxi, wilfully or negligently cause or allow such plate: to be inverted or detached or the letters or figures on such plate to be in any manner or by any means concealed from the view of any person being conveyed in such taxi at any time while such taxi may ply or be used for hire. .
65. (1) The driver or conductor of any public service vehicle shall have the right to demand his fare of any person employing him or intending to travel in such -vehicle, upon such person entering the vehicle or ordering him to wait or to drive iurther, and may refuse to convey any such person who does not comply witb such -demand, and may require any constable to remove from the vehicle any person so refusing to pay his fare.
(2) Every driver or conductor of a public service vehicle vlho demands more -than his legal fare, and every person who refuses or omits to pay his legal fare, and .and every person who resists or obstructs a constable in the performance of bis duty under this regulation shall be liable on summary conviction to a penalty not .exceeding twenty-five dollars. .
PART VIII.
RESTRICTIONS RELATING TO MOTOR OMNIBUSES.
66. (a) A motor omnibus shall be permitted to ply for hire or reward only on the Toute or routes specified in the licence issued in· respect thereof and shall not be permitted to ply for hire or reward on any route not specified therein without a 'special permit from a Licensing Officer.
(b) If the driver of a motor omnibus uses any route other than that specified in the licence or permit for the purpose of plying for hire or reward, or of conveying any passenger, he shall be guilty of an offence.
67. Every licence for a motor omnibus shall specify the route or routes and the limits thereof on or along which the vehicle is permitted to ply for hire or reward.
68. A Licensing Officer may in his discretion issue occasional special permits to -enable any motor omnibus to operate on special occasions on a route other thari that for. which the nlOtor omnibus is licensed upon payment of a fee of twenty-four {;ents. Such permit shall be valid for the period therein stated but not exceeding seven days. The driver of the motor omnibus shall retain the permit in his possession ;and produce the same for inspection to any police officer on demand. .
69. In the case of an application for the renewal of an existing licence for a motor ,omnibus or for specifying therein a route the applicant shall state the route or routes ·over which he has been customarily operating, and over which he desires to operate ·regularly. In the case of an application for a new licence for a motor omnibus not already licensed, the applicant shall state the route over which he desires to .operate regularly. .
70. All applications in respect of licences for motor omnibuses shall be submitted to the Licensing Authority who will consider each application on its merits, taking iI).to account all the attendant circumstances in connection with such application, .aud inter .alia, the following matters, namely, whether the route applied for or . any part thereof is already sufficiently provided with passenger facilities, or is, or may become congested or dangerous to the public by reason of the amount of traffic thereon, or whether the dimensions of the omnibus, although 'Within the legal limits, are excessive for use. on the route applied for, or whether the' issue of such licence is in the public interest, and the Licensing Authority may grant or refuse such applications as he thinks fit and proper. The Licensing Authority may refer any application to the Trinidad Transport Board for their consideration and advice before giving his decision thereon.
71. On receipt of an application for a licence to operate a motor omnibus on a route for which such motor omnibus has not been previously licensed, but on which other motor omnibuses have already been licensed to oper~te, the Licensing Authority shall cause notification of such application to be posted at the office of the Licensing Authority and at least two public offices on or adjacent to the route, for a period of fourteen days. Any person or body interested in or affected by the proposed additional service, and who desires to oppose the issue of the licence, may, during that period, communicate his reasons therefor in writing to the Licensing Authority, and the Licensing Authority shall consider such representations before determining whether or not the licence applied for shall be issued. .
72. Any person aggrieved by any decision of the Licensing Authority or of a Licensing Officer shall have the right to submit his application by way of appeal to the Trinidad Transport Board for further consideration.
PART IX.
RULES FOR PARKING OF VEHICLES.
73. Any motor vehicle while waiting at t.."!J.e Parking Places specified in the Third. Schedule and at the Public Stands specified in the Fourth Schedule shall be parked in the prescribed manner only, and at night when so parked shall not be required to carry any lighted lamps.
74. The ta..""{is shall stand in line commencing 15 feet from the near kerb of any intersecting road unless otherwise provided and not less than three feet nor more than four feet apart from each other: Provided that where taxis are required or permitted to stand alongside each other they shall so stand not less than one foot, apart, and not less than three feet between the lines.
75. (1) Taxis standing for hire in stands shall leave such stands for the purpose of answering calls according to their priority of position in such stands and not, otherwise.
(2) On the departure of any taxi standing for hire in any such stands the' taxi next in rear thereto shall move forward and occupy the vacant place, and in like manner other taxis in rear sl).all move forward in the line and occupy the' vacant spaces immediately in front of them.
76. No vehicles other than taxis may park in Public Stands appointed for taxis. 77. Motor omnibuses shall stand in line nbt less than three feet nor more than four 'feet apart from each other.
78. On the departure of any motor omnibus standing for hire in any such stands, the motor omnibus next in rear thereto shall move forward and occupy the vacant place and in like manner other motor omnibuses in rear shall move forward in line, and occupy the vacant spaces immediately in front of them. '
79. No vehicles other than motor omnibuses may park in Public Stands appointed. for motor omnibuses.
80. (1) All vehicles, other than tramcars and bicycles, while waiting on roads, in respect of which one-way traffic has been pre-scribed, shall be drawn up and parked in the direction of such traffic and in the manner herein prescribed, namely:- '
Where the traffic proceeds-
(a) Either in a southerly or in a northerly direction only~before l'p,.i:n~
on the East side; after 1 p.m. on the West side:
(b) In a' westerly direction only-before 1 p.m. on th~'South side and
after 1 p.m. on the North side:: .
Provided, that the provisions of this sub-paragraph shall not apply to MarinI;"
Square South. . , '
(2) All vehicles, other than tramcars and bi«ycles while waiting'at the following: places in Port-of-Spain shall be drawn up and parked in the ,manner herein prescribed .. namely:- '
(a) Queen street from Chacon Street to Richmond Street~before 1 p.m.
on the South side facing West; after 1 p.m. (:)11 the North side facing East;
(aa) Frederick Street from Park Street to Queen"s Park West-before i p.m.
all. the East side; ,after 1 p.m. on the West side. .
(b) Prince Street-before 1 p.m. South side facing East;' after l'p.m.'·North.
side facing East.
(c) Broadway on either, side with the rear of each vehicle parallel to the curb.
. (3) Between the hours of 7 a.m. and 5 p.m. daily, except on closed days-for shops (as prescribed in the Shop (Hours of Opening and Employment) Ordinance), no vehicle shall be drawn up on any of the following streets for longer than is necessary" to load or unload goods or to take up or set down passengers; and no vehicle shall 'be parked or left unattended in any of the following streets, namely:-
(a) Faure Street between George Street and Nelson Street.
(b) Cockerton Street between George Street and Nelson Street.
(e) Park Street, between Charlotte Street and St. Vincent Street.
(d) George Street, between Marine Square South and Marine Square North.
(e) Nelson Street, between Marine Square North and Marine Square South.
(f) Duncan Street, between Marine Square South and Marine Square North.
(4) All vehicles while waiting on the Eastern Main Road, Sangr~ Grande, between Ojoe Road and Railway Road West, shall be drawn up as follows:-
Before 1 p.m. on the North side; and After 1 p.m. on the South side.
81. All bicycles while waiting at the places and during the hours mentioned in the preceding regulation shall be drawn up on the opposite side of the street to that on which the other vehicles are required to be drawn up and shall be drawn up in line not less than two feet apart from each other.
82. Any applicant to whom the issue of a licence has been refused, may make further application in respect of any other route, or may, after the lapse of a period of three months, renew his application in respect of the same route.
83. The Licensing Authority may require the owner of the motor omnibus to indicate clearly thereon the route specified in the licence.
PART X.
SPECIAL TRAFFIC DIRECTIONS.
84. One-way traffic shall be observed by vehicles other than tramcars as prescribed in the Fifth Schedule to these regulations.
85. Wherever dual carriageways are provided along a public road all vehicles other than tramcars shall proceed along or keep to the left hand carriageway. Any vehicle other than a tramcar entering such road from a side road or intersecting road will bear to the left and if desiring to cross the road or to pass on to the further carriageway it will keep to the left of the traffic island situated opposite to the junction from which it has emerged and forming part of the central strip dividing the dual carriageway. .
86. At any place where a traffic roundabout has been constructed and where such roundabout is indicated by road signals, all vehicles other than tramcars will proceed in a clockwise direction only.
87. Any person who is driving a motor vehicle or who is driving or is in contiol of any other vehicle shall slow down when coming from any private road or place to any public road or when approaching any cross-roads or road junction, and if on his near side ·of the road along which he is proceeding there is erected a road signal bearing the legend" Stop-Major Road Ahead" he shall stop his vehicle before reaching, and as near as possible to, the line painted upon his near side half of the surface of suc:g. road or marked thereon by means of metallic studs approxi-mately at right angles to the carriageway and shall not move his vehicle or cause his vehicle to move to the cross roads or road junction next ahead until he shall have satisfied himself that it is safe to do so and he shall then proceed slowly and give way to any traffic which may be on such cross-roads or road junction.
88. The following route shall be followed by buses coming from or going to Arima when entering or leaving the City of .port-of-Spain respectively;-
From South Quay along Duncan Street North, into Queen Street West,
along Henry Street North to the Bus Station; then back along Henry Street North, to Duke Street East, along Nelson Street South, on to Prince Street East, along Duncan Street South, on to South Quay East.
89. No person shall drive a lorry on High Street, in the Borough of San Fernando, between Cuenca Street~and the Library Corner except for the purpose of delivering ·or reIIl,(1ving goods within that area.
90. (1) Xo cart or handcart shall be allowed to be backed into any pavement within the Borough of San Fernando. All such carts shall be drawn up parallel to the pavements for the purpose of loading or unloading any goods, articles or things, such loading to be done by means of skids: .
Provided that this Regulation shall not apply to that part of High Street between Pointe-a-Pierre Road and Penitence Street.
(2) All yehicles shall draw up in the following streets in San Fernando Det,,'een the hours of 6 a.m. to 6 p.m. as specified hereunder:-
(a) High Street, between Penitence and Hollis Streets-on the southern side
facing ,Vest;
(b) St. James Street betvveen Quenca Street and Man Chagrin Street-on
the northern side facing East;
(e) Penitence Street, between High Street and Harris Promenade-on the
eastern side;
(d) Penitence Street, between High Street and St. James Street-on the
'western side facing North;
(e) Man Chagrin Street between High Street and St. James Street-on
the eastern side facing South;
(1) Lord Street, between Coffee and Mucurapo Streets-on the southern siele
facing "West; .
(g) )Vlucurapo Street, between Coffee and Lord Streets-on the western ,side
facing North;
(h) Harris Promenade, North,' between High Street and Chancery Lane-all
the southern side only;
(i) Royal Road-on the western side only;
(j) Coffee Street-on the southern side only;
(k) Chancery Lane-on the western side only.
PART XA.
TAXIS.
90A •. (1) No person shall drive on any road a taxi licensed as such nnJe~s he is the holder of a taxi driver's licence issued to him by the Licensing Anthority under these Regulations. .
(2) Every application for a "taxi' driver's licence shall be in ·th·e form' set out in the Sixth Schedule hereto and shall be signed by the applicant: Duplic,ate. photographs of the applicant of passport 'size and his driving permit. iSSllyd under the Ordinance shall accompany each application.
(3} The Licensing Authority' may refuse- a taxi driverislicence to any applicant who fails to satisfy him that he is a fit and proper pers0n to hold such a licence : Provided that before refusing a licence the Licensing Authority shall inform the applicant 'of the grounds for the proposed refusal and shall' consider any reprlOsentations made in writing in· support of his application within seven days thereafter. If the applicant notifies the Licensing Authority that he wishes to make ,oral representations and/or adduce witnesses in support of his application the Licensing Authority shall hear such representations and/oT' witnesses or shall direct his Deputy or such other officer as he may appoint to hear and deterniine the matter in dispute. A taxi driver's licence may be refused notwithstanding that the applicant is the holder of a driving permit under the Ordinance, but shall in no case be issued to an applicant who is not the holder of such a per:mit.. The fee for a taxi driver's licence shall be .$2 and shall be paid to the Licensing.Authority before the licence is issued. The licence shall be in the form setout in the Seventh Schedule hereto .. A taxi driver's licence shall not be valid during any period in which the holder is not the holder of a driving permit under the Ordinance.
(4) On the grant of a taxi driver's licence there shall be issued to the hoJder thereof a badge on. which is embossed the number of the licence and a photograph of the holder if the Licensing Authority provides such badge and/or photograph. Such badge shall be displayed therein at all times when any taxi of which such holder is the driver is standing, plying or being driven for hire, in such a manner as to be plainly and distinctly visible.
90B. If the holder of a ,taxi driver's licence shall satisfy the Licensing Authority that the licence issued to him has been lost, destroyed or defaced and that he has not wilfully effected or been a party to such loss, destruction or defacement, it shall be lawful for the Licensing Authority to issue a duplicate licence: ,Provided that if the former licence has been defaced it shall be surrendered to the Licensing Authority prior to the issue of a duplicate licence. The provisions of paragraph (3) of the preceding regulation shall apply in relation to duplicate licences and every such duplicate licence shall be endorsed as such by the Licensing Authority: Provided that the Licensing Authority shall not refuse to issue a duplicate licence on the ground that the applicant is not a fit and proper person to hold a licence except on facts which have come to his knowledge since the original licence was issued.
90c. (1) The Licensing Authority may cancel any taxi driver's licence (which expression vvhen hereinafter used shall include a duplicate licence) if the holder thereof shall be convicted of any offence against any of the regulations contained in this Part of these Regulations or if the Licensing Authority shall be satisfied that it was obtained by any false representation, or that the holder has ceased to be a fit and proper person to hold a taxi driver's licence: Provided that the Licensing Authority shall inform him of the substance of what is alleged against him and shall consider any explanations or representations made by him in writing in connection with such proposed cancellation within seven days thereafter. If the holder of tt'le taxi driver's licence notifies the Licensing Authority that he wishes to make oral representation and/or adduce witnesses in connection with such proposed cancellation the Licensing Authority shall hear such representations and/or witnesses or shall direct his Deputy or such other officer as he may appoint to hear and determine the matter in'dispute. Cancellation of a taxi driver's licence may be effected by notice to the holder and on such cancellation the holder shall forthwith surrender his licence and' badge to the Licensing Authority. When the driving permit of any person has been' cancelled under the Ordinance it shall be in the discretion of the Licensing Authority to refuse allY further taxi driver's licence to that person.
(2) The holder of a taxi driver's licence shall surrender such licence and the badge issued therewith forthwith to the Licensing Authority on ceasing 'to be the holder of a driving permit issued under the Ordinance.
(3) \Vhenever a taxi driver's licence and badge are svrrendered for any reason by the holder thereof the Licensing Authority shall refund to such holder the sum of 80 cents.
90D. It shall be the duty of the holder of a taxi driver's licence to notify the officer in charge of the police station nearest his residence of any change in the particulars set out in his licence and to produce ,such licence to such officer. If required to do so by the officer, he shall sign a statement containing full particulars of the information given and the officer shall forward the licence and the particulars to the Licensing Authority who may make any l1ecessary correction on the licence and return the same to the holder.
90B. It shall be the duty of every taxi-driver to take all proper steps to preserve his licence and badge against loss, destruction or defacement and to produce the same on demand for inspection by any member of a police force constituted by law in the Colony or by any passenger in any taxi of which he may be the driver.
90F. (1) 'Whenever au}' taxi is on a taxi stand, or is plying for hire or is under hire, no person (other than the driver) shall be in the taxi unless he is a bona fide passenger.
(2) In this regulation the expression" bona fidepassellger " means a person (hereinafter referred to as "the hirer") who, on his own account or jointly with .others, has hired the taxi for reward and any other person who has entered the taxi with the permission of the hirer.
(3) Uthe preceding provisions of this regulation are contravened, the 'driver of the taxi and the persons who were in the taxi in contravention of those provisions shall be deemed to have contravened these Regulations; and in any prosecution for any such contravention any person (other than the driver) proved to have been in the taxi who cannot give a good account'of himself shall be deemed not to be a bona fide passenger.
(4) If any person shall loiter on or about any taxi stand (whether in a vehicle or otherwise) and shall fail to give a good account of himself, he shall be deemed to have contravened these Regulations.
90G. A driver of a taxi shall not himself usc, or knowingly permit the taxi to be used by any other person for any indecent or immoral purpose.
90H. The Licensing Authority may cancel the licensing of any taxi under the Ordinance-
(a) if the owner or taxi driver has been convicted in respect of the commission
of any criminal offence, whether against the regulations or otherwise in connection with the use of the taxi unless the owner shall prove that the offence was committed without his knowledge and that he has taken all reasonable steps to a void the commission of the offence; or
(b) where the owner is also the driver of the taxi and has had his taxi driver's
licence cancelled under these Regulations:
Provided that the Licensing Authority shall inform the owner of the substance of what is alleged against him and shall consider any explanations or representations made by the owner in writing in connection with any such proposed cancellation within seven days thereafter. If the owner notmes the Licensing Authority that he wishes to make oral representations and/or adduce witn((sses in connection with any such proposed cancellation the Licensing Authority shall hear such representations and/or witnesses or shall direct his Deputy or such other officer as he may appoint to hear and determine the matter in dispute. Cancellation may be effected by notice to the owner and on any such cancellation it shall be in the discretion of the Licensing Authority to refuse any further licensing of the vehicle as a taxi whilst in the same ownership.
90I. The provisions of paragraph (1) of regulation 90A and of regulation 90F shall not apply in relation to any taxi when it is on a road or is being driven on a road for the purpose of test by any garage keeper or any employee of any garage keeper and is not being used for hire or reward. -
90J. No person shall cause or permit a rented car to ply for hire on any road.
PART XI.
Mrs CELLANEOUS.
91. (1) No person shall drive or cause or permit any person employed by him or subject to his orders to drive any hiring car 91' motor omnibus, or any goods vehicle, continuously for more than five and a half·hours without at least a half hour intervening during. which the driver can obtain rest and refreshment, nor for an aggregate of more than ten hours in any twenty-four-hour period, nor for more than fifty-six hours in anyone week, and provided that the driver shall have at least ten consecutive hours for rest duhng such twenty-four-hour period and shall not be employed' in any capacity by his employer for more than eleven hours during such twenty-four-hour period. .
(2) For the purposes of this. r~gulation, a. twenty-four-hour period shall .b~ the period of time commencing from the hour at which the d'riv'er first took up work on any day to the corresponding hour on the following day.
91A. The Licensing Authority, in his discretion, need not enforce any or all of the provisions contained in Regulations 41 to 48, 50 and 51 of these Regulations;. and the provisions of paragraphs (e) and (/) of Regulation 53 of these Regulations shall have effect in relation to any motor omnibus subject to any modWcation approved by the Licensing Authority and endorsed on the licence relating:to such vehicle by such Authority or on his behalf.
92. (1) No person shall, except for the purpose of taking up or setting down passengers, allow a vehicle to remain stationary at any time between the hours -of 7.30 a.m. and 9.00 a.m. 0:' between the hours of 4.00 p.m. and 5.30 p.m. 011 any day at any place on the follovving sections of road:-
(a) that portion of South Quay and the Eastern Main Road which lies
between Piccadilly Street in the City of Port-of-Spain and Bushe Street at San Juan, and
(b) that portion of the Eastern Main Road which lies between St. John's
Road and Freeling Street at Tunapuna.
(2) No goods shall be loaded or unloaded from any vehicle at any place on the aforesaid sections of road at any time between the aforesaid hours; and the ·driver or person in charge of any vehicle on or from which any goods are loaded -or unloaded in contravention of this Regulation and any person assisting in such loading or unloading shall each be guilty of an offence against this Regulation.
(3) No person shall drive an animal-drawn cart in an easterly direction .along that section of the Eastern Main Road which lies between its junctions ,yith the Old St. Joseph Road at any time between the aforesaid hours.
93. No person shall, except for the purpose of taking up or setting down passengers, .allow a vehicle, other than a vehicle belonging to the Government of the United States, to remain stationary at any time at any place on the following sections of roads:-
(a) that portion of the Eastern Main Road which lies between the 17.21 and
the 21.86 mile marks;
(b) the whole of Demerara Road;
(e) that portion of the Aripo Road which lies between the 0.00 and 1.93 mile
marks;
(d) that portion of the Heights of Guanapo Road which lies between the
0.00 and 2.27 mile marks.
94. (1) Between the hours of 7.00 a.m. and 9.00 a.m. and 4.00 p.m. and 6.00 p.m· daily no vehicle shall be drawn up and parked on the north side of the 'Nestern Main Road between Mucurapo Road on the east and the Diego Martin Road on the west.
(2) Between the aforesaid hours waiting will be permitted on the south side ,of the Western Main Road only.
95. No person shall, except for the purpose of taking up or setting down passengers, .allow a vehicle to remain stationary at any time on that portion of the Chaguanas Eoad, between Endeavour Street on the East and the Careni-Savannah Road on the ·West.
